

## CRITICAL (34)

### Admin console

**Audit Logs page crashes the whole app on render — l.severity.toUpperCase() on rows that have no `severity` field**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminAuditPage.tsx:104` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: As soon as the audit_logs table contains at least one row (which every admin action itself creates), /admin/audit throws a TypeError during render. There is no ErrorBoundary anywhere in frontend/src (grep for ErrorBoundary/componentDidCatch returns nothing), so the entire SPA unmounts to a blank white page. Typing anything in the search box hits a second TypeError on l.user_name.toLowerCase() (line 67). The page is only 'usable' while the table is empty.
- Evidence: Page assumes flat, pre-formatted rows — AdminAuditPage.tsx:17-26 `interface AuditLog { id; action; user_name; user_phone?; ip; timestamp; details; severity: 'info'|'warning'|'critical' }` and AdminAuditPage.tsx:101-105 renders `<span className={...severityColors[l.severity]}>{severityIcons[l.severity]}{l.severity.toUpperCase()}</span>`, plus AdminAuditPage.tsx:67-70 `l.user_name.toLowerCase()... l.details.toLowerCase()... l.ip.includes(searchQuery)`. Server returns raw Prisma rows with none of those keys — packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:601-615 `prisma.auditLog.findMany({ where, include: { user: {select:{id,name,phone}}, business: {...} }, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' 
- Fix: Either map on the server (add severity derived from action, user_name from user.name, ip from ip_address, timestamp from created_at, details from new_data/old_data) or, in the page, read the real fields defensively: `l.created_at`, `l.user?.name`, `l.ip_address`, `l.entity_type`/`l.new_data`, and compute severity locally with a fallback (`const sev = l.severity ?? 'info'`) before calling .toUpperC

**Subscriptions page table is permanently fabricated mock data — there is no list-subscriptions endpoint at all**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:110` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: The main table on /admin/subscriptions always shows the same 4 invented rows ('Sharma Trading Co.', 'Gupta Mandi Commission', ₹4999/₹14999, INV-2024-001…) regardless of real data, because the field it reads does not exist in the API response and the `||` fallback silently swaps in mock data even on a 200 OK. Admins can search, filter, export CSV and download invoice PDFs of these fake subscriptions. A real manual purchase created seconds earlier never appears in the list.
- Evidence: AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:108-110 `if (analyticsRes?.data?.data) { setAnalytics(analyticsRes.data.data); setSubscriptions(analyticsRes.data.data.recentSubscriptions || defaultSubs); }`. The analytics endpoint returns no such key — packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:646-667 `async getSubscriptionAnalytics() { const [byStatus, byPlan, monthlyRevenue] = await Promise.all([...]); return { byStatus, byPlan, monthlyRevenue }; }` (admin.controller.ts:154-157 wraps it as `{success:true,data:analytics}`). defaultSubs is hardcoded mock — AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:67-72. And packages/admin-service/src/routes/admin.routes.ts:28-29 registers only `router.post('/subscriptions/manual', 
- Fix: Add `GET /admin/subscriptions` in admin.routes.ts + a paginated `listSubscriptions()` in admin.service.ts returning business/plan/cycle/status/period/invoice joined rows, expose it as `adminApi.subscriptions(params)`, and delete the `|| defaultSubs` fallback so an empty result renders the empty state instead of invented rows.

### Auth & session

**Phone/OTP login is completely non-functional — no SMS provider is wired, so the OTP is never delivered**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:124` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: On LoginPage the user picks the "Phone" tab, enters a phone number and clicks "Send OTP". The UI shows a success toast ("OTP sent") and advances to the OTP entry step, but no SMS is ever sent. In production the OTP is not even logged, so the code exists only inside Redis and is unobtainable. Every user who chooses the phone tab is stuck on the OTP screen forever — half of the login page is dead.
- Evidence: packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:118-131 — the code is generated, stored, and then nothing sends it: const otp = generateOTP(6); const otpKey = REDIS_KEYS.OTP(phone); await redis.setex(otpKey, OTP_EXPIRY_SECONDS, otp); // TODO: Send OTP via SMS provider (Twilio/MSG91) // In development, log OTP if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production') { logger.info(`OTP for ${phone}: ${otp}`); } logger.info('OTP sent', { phone, environment: process.env.NODE_ENV }); return { success: true, message: 'OTP sent successfully' }; A repo-wide grep for an SMS integration finds only that TODO: `grep -rn "msg91|MSG91|twilio|sendSMS|sms" --include=*.ts packages/` -> `auth-service/src/services/auth.s
- Fix: Either implement the SMS send (MSG91/Twilio) inside sendOTP and fail the request when the provider errors, or hide the Phone tab in LoginPage.tsx until it is implemented. Do not return success:true for an OTP that was never dispatched.

### Business management

**Cross-tenant write: PATCH /business/:id (and logo upload/delete, shops) authorizes the header business but mutates the URL business**
- `packages/business-service/src/shared/middleware/rbac.middleware.ts:13` — *authorization*
- Impact: Any authenticated user can rename, re-address, change the GST/PAN/phone/email of, or overwrite/delete the logo of ANY other tenant's business. One curl with a normal token silently corrupts a stranger's business profile (and the audit log is written against the victim's business id, so it looks like the victim did it).
- Evidence: Authorization is computed from the header, the mutation from the path param. rbac.middleware.ts:13 `const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string || req.params.businessId;` then :23 `(req as unknown as AuthenticatedRequest).businessId = businessId;` — no membership lookup at all. rbac.middleware.ts:34 `const businessId = authReq.businessId || req.headers['x-business-id']` then :52 `prisma.businessUser.findUnique({ where: { user_id_business_id: { user_id: userId, business_id: businessId } } })` — so requirePermission('BUSINESS_EDIT') validates the ATTACKER'S OWN business. But business.controller.ts:74 `const { businessId } = req.params;` / :76 `await businessService.updateBusiness
- Fix: Delete the header fallback for :businessId routes: in requireBusiness, when req.params.businessId exists, require that it equals the header (or ignore the header entirely) and do the businessUser membership lookup there; then have every controller read the single authorized `(req as AuthenticatedRequest).businessId` instead of `req.params.businessId`.

**Privilege escalation: POST /business/:id/invite adds a member to any business chosen by URL**
- `packages/business-service/src/controllers/business.controller.ts:91` — *authorization*
- Impact: An attacker with any account can grant themselves MANAGER on any other tenant's business, after which every other service (purchases, sales, ledger, parties) legitimately accepts their x-business-id and they have full read/write on the victim's books. This is total tenant takeover, not just a read leak.
- Evidence: business.routes.ts:62 `router.post('/:businessId/invite', requireBusiness, requirePermission('USER_INVITE'), validate(inviteUserSchema), ctrl.inviteUser);` — requirePermission checks membership for `authReq.businessId`, which rbac.middleware.ts:13 set from `req.headers['x-business-id'] || req.params.businessId` (attacker sends their own business in the header and passes as OWNER, since constants/index.ts:71 gives OWNER 'USER_INVITE'). The controller then ignores that: business.controller.ts:91 `const { businessId } = req.params;` / :95 `await businessService.inviteUser(businessId, phone, role, invitedBy)`, and business.service.ts:216 `prisma.businessUser.create({ data: { user_id: user.id, bu
- Fix: Same root fix as the PATCH issue — resolve exactly one authorized businessId in middleware and use it in inviteUser; additionally reject the request when req.params.businessId !== the membership-verified business id.

### Dashboard & Reports

**GET /business/dashboard has no membership check — any authenticated user can read any business's dashboard by changing x-business-id**
- `packages/business-service/src/routes/business.routes.ts:23` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: Any logged-in user (of any business, or a brand-new trial signup) can send x-business-id: <someone-else's-business-uuid> to GET /api/v1/business/dashboard and receive that tenant's purchase totals, sales totals, outstanding receivable/payable, low-stock count and partial-payment count. The frontend already sends this header from Redux, so it is a one-line change in devtools. The gateway forwards x-business-id verbatim.
- Evidence: packages/business-service/src/routes/business.routes.ts:23 — `router.get('/dashboard', requireBusiness, ctrl.getDashboardFromHeader); // GET /business/dashboard (business from header)` — only requireBusiness, no requirePermission. Compare the legacy param variant, packages/business-service/src/routes/business.routes.ts:33 — `router.get('/:businessId/dashboard', requireBusiness, requirePermission('PURCHASE_VIEW'), ctrl.getDashboard);` requireBusiness does NOT verify membership — packages/business-service/src/shared/middleware/rbac.middleware.ts:12-25: `export function requireBusiness(req, res, next): void { const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string || req.params.businessId; if
- Fix: Add the membership/permission guard to the header-based route: `router.get('/dashboard', requireBusiness, requirePermission('PURCHASE_VIEW'), ctrl.getDashboardFromHeader);` — matching line 33. (The same gap exists on the other requireBusiness-only routes in this file, e.g. /:businessId/bank-accounts GET and /:businessId/credit-cards GET.)

### Expenses & Bills

**No /api/v1/expenses prefix on the gateway — every expense API call 404s**
- `packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:120` — *missing-endpoint*
- Impact: Opening /expenses shows a permanently empty page plus the "fetch_error" toast; the stat cards never render (stats stays null); creating an expense always fails with the "create_error" toast; Delete always fails. Every single expenseApi call in the cluster is dead in dev (Vite proxy -> gateway) and in prod (vercel.json rewrite -> gateway).
- Evidence: frontend/src/lib/api.ts:5 `const API_BASE = import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || '/api/v1';` and :512 `listAll: (params?) => api.get('/expenses', { params })` -> GET /api/v1/expenses. packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:120-160 `const services: Record<...> = { '/api/v1/auth': ..., '/api/v1/business': ..., '/api/v1/purchases': ..., '/api/v1/sales': ..., '/api/v1/inventory': ..., '/api/v1/ledger': ..., '/api/v1/subscriptions': ..., '/api/v1/billing': ..., '/api/v1/notifications': ..., '/api/v1/admin': ..., '/api/v1/profile': ..., '/api/v1/referrals': ..., '/api/v1/wa': ... };` — no '/api/v1/expenses' key, and no EXPENSE_SERVICE entry in SERVICE_PORTS (index.ts:9-23). packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts
- Fix: Add `EXPENSE_SERVICE: 3014` to SERVICE_PORTS and `'/api/v1/expenses': { target: serviceHost('expense-service', SERVICE_PORTS.EXPENSE_SERVICE) }` to the services map in packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts.

**expense-service mounts its router at /api/expenses, not /api/v1/expenses**
- `packages/expense-service/src/index.ts:13` — *path-prefix-mismatch*
- Impact: Even after the gateway prefix is added, every expense request still 404s: the gateway forwards the path unchanged (no pathRewrite is configured for any service), so /api/v1/expenses hits a service that only answers /api/expenses. List, stats, create, get-by-id, update and delete all return Express's default 404 HTML.
- Evidence: packages/expense-service/src/index.ts:13 `app.use('/api/expenses', expenseRoutes);` — the only mount in the file (grep of `app.use` in that file returns cors, express.json, and this line). Every other service uses the v1 prefix, e.g. packages/purchase-service/src/index.ts:33 `app.use('/api/v1/purchases', purchaseRoutes);` and packages/profile-service/src/index.ts:28 `app.use('/api/v1/profile', profileRoutes);`. Gateway forwards untouched: packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:120 services map entries carry no `pathRewrite`.
- Fix: Change to `app.use('/api/v1/expenses', expenseRoutes);`.

### Inventory

**Stock adjustment mutates an arbitrary item — frontend sends item_id, service reads itemId**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/StockAdjustPage.tsx:64` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: User picks "Rice Basmati" on /inventory/adjust, enters qty 50, picks a reason, clicks "Adjust Stock". The request succeeds (toast "stock adjusted", navigates to /inventory) but the quantity is added to/removed from whatever item Postgres returns first for that business — a completely different product. Stock levels silently become wrong, and an ADJUSTMENT inventory_transaction is written against the wrong item_id. The frontend's own "insufficient stock" guard is evaluated against the selected item, so the backend can even drive the wrong item negative-adjacent (backend guard runs on the wrong item's stock).
- Evidence: Frontend payload is snake_case (StockAdjustPage.tsx:18-24): `const [form, setForm] = useState({ item_id: '', type: 'ADD' as 'ADD' | 'REMOVE', quantity: 0, reason: '', notes: '' });` and StockAdjustPage.tsx:64: `await inventoryApi.adjustStock(form);` → api.ts:379 `adjustStock: (data: any) => api.post('/inventory/adjust', data)`. The route has NO zod validator (inventory.routes.ts:26: `router.post('/adjust', requirePermission('INVENTORY_EDIT'), ctrl.adjustStock);`) so the raw body reaches inventory.service.ts:192-199: `async adjustStock(businessId: string, _userId: string, data: { itemId: string; quantity: number; reason: string; type: 'ADD' | 'REMOVE'; }) { return prisma.$transaction(async (t
- Fix: Either send camelCase from the page (`itemId`, and drop/rename `notes`) or normalise in the controller. Best: add a zod validator to POST /adjust (`z.object({ itemId: z.string().uuid(), type: z.enum(['ADD','REMOVE']), quantity: z.number().positive(), reason: z.string().min(1), notes: z.string().optional() })`) so a missing itemId returns 400 instead of corrupting a random item, and make the servic

**Delete item is a silent no-op — is_deleted is not a schema field and updateItem ignores it**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryPage.tsx:53` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On /inventory the row Trash icon opens the confirm dialog; confirming shows "item deleted" and refetches — the item is still in the list. On /inventory/:id the Delete button shows success and navigates back to /inventory, where the item is still present. The item is never deleted or deactivated; users will repeatedly "delete" items that never go away.
- Evidence: InventoryPage.tsx:50-56: `const handleDeleteItem = async () => { if (!deleteItem) return; try { await inventoryApi.updateItem(deleteItem.id, { is_deleted: true }); toast.success(t('inventory:item_deleted'));` and identically InventoryDetailPage.tsx:92-96: `await inventoryApi.updateItem(id!, { is_deleted: true }); toast.success(t('inventory:item_deleted')); navigate('/inventory');`. The PATCH lands on inventory.routes.ts:18 → inventory.service.ts:46-57 which whitelists fields and has no is_deleted/soft-delete branch: `data: { ...(data.name && { name: data.name }), ...(data.sku !== undefined && { sku: data.sku }), ...(data.categoryId && { category_id: data.categoryId }), ...(data.unit && { uni
- Fix: Send `{ isActive: false }` (the supported soft-delete field) from both pages, and make listItems default to `is_active: true` so deactivated items disappear — or add a real `DELETE /items/:itemId` route + controller and point the UI at it.

**prune-seeded silently deletes user-created inventory items whenever the purchase form is opened**
- `packages/inventory-service/src/services/inventory.service.ts:272` — *data-bug*
- Impact: A user adds items via /inventory/new (e.g. "Kota Stone", "Tiles"). Because create never persists opening stock (see the item-create finding), every new item has current_stock=0 and quantity_in=0. The next time anyone opens /purchases/new or edits a purchase, PurchaseForm fires POST /inventory/prune-seeded on mount and every such item that isn't literally named "Marble" or "Granite" is hard-deleted from the database. No confirmation, no UI, no error surfaced (the call is inside `catch { /* ignore */ }`). Users lose their catalogue and cannot understand why.
- Evidence: PurchaseForm.tsx:339-345 runs it unconditionally on mount: `// Always seed first (idempotent — fast no-op if already seeded)\n await inventoryApi.seedDefaults();\n // Remove old seeded varieties that have no stock/lots/transactions\n await inventoryApi.pruneSeeded();\n } catch { /* ignore if seed/prune fails — items may already exist */ }` → api.ts:383 `pruneSeeded: () => api.post('/inventory/prune-seeded')` → inventory.routes.ts:38 → inventory.service.ts:274-295: `const candidates = await prisma.inventoryItem.findMany({ where: { business_id: businessId, current_stock: 0, quantity_in: 0, NOT: { name: { in: ['Marble', 'Granite'], mode: 'insensitive' } } }, select: { id: true, name: true, _cou
- Fix: Stop calling pruneSeeded from PurchaseForm entirely. If cleanup is needed, add an explicit `is_seeded`/`source` flag written by seedDefaults and restrict the prune to rows carrying that flag, include `inventory_txns` in the _count guard, and gate the route behind an admin-only action with explicit user confirmation rather than a page-mount side effect.

### Ledger

**Create ledger entry always 500s — dialog sends snake_case, service reads camelCase**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/CreateLedgerEntryDialog.tsx:49` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: Ledger -> "New Entry" -> fill amount/narration -> Create Entry. The POST always fails and the user gets the generic "entry_create_error" toast. Manual/adjustment ledger entries can never be created through the UI.
- Evidence: Frontend form state is snake_case and is posted verbatim — CreateLedgerEntryDialog.tsx:22-29 `const [form, setForm] = useState({ entry_type: 'DEBIT', account_type: 'EXPENSE', amount: 0, entry_date: ..., party_id: '', narration: '' });` then :49 `await ledgerApi.createEntry(form);` (api.ts:391 `createEntry: (data: any) => api.post('/ledger/entries', data)`). The route has no zod validator (ledger-service/src/routes/ledger.routes.ts:12 `router.post('/entries', requirePermission('LEDGER_EDIT'), ctrl.createManualEntry);`) and the controller forwards req.body untouched (controllers/ledger.controller.ts:21 `ledgerService.createManualEntry(businessId, userId, req.body)`). The service reads camelCas
- Fix: Either map in the dialog (`{ accountType: form.account_type, entryType: form.entry_type, entryDate: form.entry_date, partyId: form.party_id || undefined, amount, narration }`) or, better, accept both casings in the controller/service and add a zod validator on POST /entries so a bad payload returns 400 with field names instead of a Prisma 500.

**Outstanding tab reports receivables as payables and vice versa**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:397` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Ledger -> Outstanding tab. "Total Receivable" shows the money the business OWES suppliers and "Total Payable" shows the money customers owe. Every party row's label is flipped too (a customer who owes 50k is shown in red as "payable"). Collections/payment decisions are made off inverted numbers.
- Evidence: ledger-service treats a positive party.balance as receivable — services/ledger.service.ts:397-403 `const totalReceivable = parties.filter((p:any) => Number(p.balance) > 0).reduce(...); const totalPayable = parties.filter((p:any) => Number(p.balance) < 0).reduce((sum,p) => sum + Math.abs(Number(p.balance)), 0);` and :381-384 `if (type === 'receivable') { partyWhere.balance = { gt: 0 }; } else if (type === 'payable') { partyWhere.balance = { lt: 0 }; }`. The writers use the opposite convention: packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:358-364 `// Update party balance ... // Negative = receivable (customer owes us)` / `balance: { decrement: new Prisma.Decimal(partyBalanceAmount) }`,
- Fix: Flip the sign test in getOutstandingSummary (receivable = balance < 0, payable = balance > 0) and in LedgerPage.tsx:511-513, or normalize the whole codebase on one sign. The same inverted convention is in getBalanceSheet (ledger.service.ts:306-314) so Sundry Debtors/Creditors on the balance sheet are swapped as well.

### Parties & Cutters

**Delete/Deactivate party is a complete no-op — frontend sends is_active, service only reads isActive**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:186` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On /parties, click the red trash icon on any party row and confirm "Deactivate party". A success toast appears ("Party deactivated"), the list refetches, and the party is completely unchanged — still active, still listed. The user believes the party was deactivated. It can never be deactivated from the UI (there is no DELETE route either).
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:186 `await profileApi.updateParty(deleteParty.id, { is_active: false });` -> api.ts:431 `updateParty: (id, data) => api.patch(`/profile/parties/${id}`, data)` -> route profile.routes.ts:42 `router.patch('/parties/:partyId', ctrl.updateParty);` (no zod validator, so the bad key is not rejected) -> packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:203 `...(data.isActive !== undefined && { is_active: data.isActive }),` — `is_active` is never read anywhere in updateParty (lines 195-222), so prisma.party.update receives an empty data object and returns 200 with the unchanged row. Compounding it, profile.service.ts:241 `if (query.isActive !== u
- Fix: Send `{ isActive: false }` from PartiesPage.tsx:186 (matching the camelCase contract the service uses for every other field), and add `where.is_active = true` as the default in profile.service.ts listParties so deactivated parties actually disappear from the list.

**Party & cutter endpoints trust the x-business-id header with no membership check — cross-tenant read/write**
- `packages/profile-service/src/routes/profile.routes.ts:36` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: Any logged-in user (valid JWT for their own business) can change the x-business-id header to another tenant's business id and GET /api/v1/profile/parties to dump that tenant's entire party list — names, phones, WhatsApp, email, GSTIN, PAN, credit limits, balances, contacts and bank account numbers (account_number/ifsc_code are included via `include: { bank_accounts: true }`). The same holds for GET/POST/PATCH on parties, and all cutter + cutter-transaction routes, so they can also mutate another tenant's data.
- Evidence: packages/profile-service/src/routes/profile.routes.ts:36-52 — `router.use(requireBusiness);` then `router.post('/parties', validate(createPartySchema), ctrl.createParty); router.get('/parties', ctrl.listParties); ... router.patch('/cutters/:cutterId/transactions/:transactionId', ctrl.updateCutterTransaction);` — not one route carries requirePermission/requireRole. packages/profile-service/src/shared/middleware/rbac.middleware.ts:12-25 `export function requireBusiness(...) { const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string || req.params.businessId; if (!businessId) {...400...} (req as unknown as AuthenticatedRequest).businessId = businessId; next(); }` — it only copies the header, it
- Fix: Add `requirePermission('PARTY_VIEW')` / `requirePermission('PARTY_CREATE')` (or at minimum a membership check equivalent to requirePermission) to every business-scoped route in profile.routes.ts lines 39-56, exactly as ledger-service and billing-service do.

### Profile, Notifications, Help

**Profile save returns 400 for every user who has no email address (empty string vs zod .email())**
- `frontend/src/components/profile/ProfilePage.tsx:62` — *zod-validation*
- Impact: Users who signed up by phone OTP (the default flow — email is optional, schema.prisma:24 `email String?`) can never change their name: clicking Save Changes always shows the generic "profile_update_error" toast. It is also impossible to clear an email once set, for the same reason.
- Evidence: ProfilePage.tsx:21 seeds the form with an empty string when the user has no email — `const [form, setForm] = useState({ name: user?.name || '', email: user?.email || '' });` — and ProfilePage.tsx:62 sends the whole form unchanged: `const { data } = await profileApi.update(form);` -> api.ts:419 `update: (data: any) => api.patch('/profile/me', data)` -> profile.routes.ts:14 `router.patch('/me', validate(updateProfileSchema), ctrl.updateProfile)` -> validators/index.ts:250 `email: z.string().email().optional(),`. `''` is present (so `.optional()` does not apply) and fails `.email()`, so validate.middleware.ts:17-27 returns `res.status(400).json({ success:false, message:'Validation failed', ... 
- Fix: Either strip empty strings before sending (`profileApi.update({ name: form.name, ...(form.email.trim() ? { email: form.email.trim() } : {}) })`) or, better, make the schema tolerant: `email: z.union([z.string().email(), z.literal('')]).optional().transform(v => v === '' ? null : v)` and let profile.service.ts:34 write null.

### Purchases

**createPurchaseSchema strips GST, discount, round-off and reminders from the create payload — saved total does not match the total shown in the form**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:188` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On "Save Purchase" (create), everything the user typed into GST / Discount / Round Off and every Payment Reminder row is silently discarded. The form shows e.g. Total ₹118,000 (₹100,000 + 18% GST) but the purchase is stored with total_amount ₹100,000, gst_mode NULL, gst_amount 0, and no reminder rows. The purchase detail page then shows a different total than the form did, the supplier payable is understated, and reminders never fire. Editing the same purchase afterwards works (PATCH has no validator), so the bug looks intermittent.
- Evidence: Frontend sends these fields — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:784-795: `gstMode: g.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? g.gst_mode : undefined,` / `gstValue: ...` / `gstAmount: g.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? gadiGstAmount(g) : undefined,` / `discount: g.discount || undefined,` / `roundOff: g.round_off || undefined,` / `reminders: g.reminders.filter(r => r.remind_on).map(...)`. Schema has none of them — packages/purchase-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:188-199: `export const createPurchaseSchema = z.object({ shopId, partyId, gadiNumber, billNumber, purchaseDate, items, expenses, cutterTransactions, payments, notes });` And the validator REPLACES the body with the stripped parse result — pa
- Fix: Add the missing keys to createPurchaseSchema: `gstMode: z.enum(['NONE','PERCENT','AMOUNT']).optional(), gstValue: z.number().nonnegative().optional(), gstAmount: z.number().nonnegative().optional(), discount: z.number().nonnegative().optional().default(0), roundOff: z.number().optional().default(0), reminderDate: z.string().datetime().optional(), reminderAmount: z.number().nonnegative().optional()

### Sales

**createSaleSchema silently strips gstMode/gstValue/gstAmount/roundOff/reminders — GST, round-off and collection reminders are never saved**
- `packages/sales-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:216` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On /sales/new the user sets GST (e.g. 18%), a round-off, and one or more collection reminders. The form's 'Total Amount' shows subtotal + GST - discount + round-off. The saved sale has total_amount = subtotal - discount: GST and round-off are gone, gst_mode/gst_value stay NULL, and zero PaymentReminder rows are created. The user is redirected to the detail page which now shows a different (lower) total than the form did, no GST line, and no reminder will ever fire.
- Evidence: Frontend sends them — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:626-641: `const payload = { partyId: entry.party_id, saleDate: ..., notes: ..., gstMode: entry.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? entry.gst_mode : undefined, gstValue: ..., gstAmount: entry.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? entryGstAmount(entry) : undefined, discount: entry.discount || undefined, roundOff: entry.round_off || undefined, reminders: entry.reminders.filter(r => r.remind_on).map(r => ({ remindOn: ..., amount: ..., note: ... })), ...` Schema has no such keys — packages/sales-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:216-225: `export const createSaleSchema = z.object({ shopId: ..., partyId: z.string().uuid(), saleDate: ..., saleLots: z.arr
- Fix: Add the missing keys to createSaleSchema: `gstMode: z.enum(['NONE','PERCENT','AMOUNT']).optional(), gstValue: z.number().min(0).optional(), gstAmount: z.number().min(0).optional(), roundOff: z.number().optional(), reminderDate: z.string().datetime().optional(), reminderAmount: z.number().optional(), reminders: z.array(z.object({ remindOn: z.string().datetime(), amount: z.number().optional(), note:

**Bulk 'Delete selected' on the sales list deletes nothing but reports success**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:57` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: User ticks 5 sales, clicks 'Delete selected', confirms the destructive red dialog, and sees a green toast 'N deleted'. Nothing is deleted server-side. The list is refetched and all 5 rows are still there, so the user believes the delete failed silently — or worse, believes it succeeded after navigating away.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:57-66: `const handleBulkDelete = async () => { if (!bulkDeleteItems) return; try { toast.success(t('sales:deleted_count', { count: bulkDeleteItems.length })); setBulkDeleteItems(null); fetchSales(); } catch { toast.error(t('sales:delete_error')); } };` — there is no salesApi.delete call anywhere in the function, and the try block contains nothing that can throw, so the catch is unreachable. The endpoint exists and is wired — frontend/src/lib/api.ts:349: `delete: (id: string) => api.delete(`/sales/${id}`),` and packages/sales-service/src/routes/sales.routes.ts:33: `router.delete('/:saleId', requirePermission('SALE_DELETE'), ctrl.deleteSale);`
- Fix: `await Promise.all(bulkDeleteItems.map(s => salesApi.delete(s.id)))` before the toast, and surface per-item failures (Promise.allSettled) instead of a blanket success.

### Subscription & Referrals

**Paid upgrade takes no payment: no Razorpay checkout client-side, and the server marks the subscription usable immediately**
- `frontend/src/components/subscription/SubscriptionPage.tsx:101` — *missing-integration*
- Impact: Every "Upgrade"/"Subscribe to <plan>" button grants the paid plan for free. The user is never shown a payment sheet, the invoice stays PENDING forever, and the app shows "Subscription updated"/"Plan activated". Revenue is zero and every paid tier is effectively public.
- Evidence: Frontend fires and forgets, discarding the order id the server returns: `await subscriptionApi.subscribe({ planId, billingCycle: cycleMap[billing] });` (SubscriptionPage.tsx:101) then straight to `toast.success(t('subscription:plan_activated'))` / `toast.success(t('subscription:subscription_updated'))` (SubscriptionPage.tsx:110,113). The API client has no checkout method: `subscribe: (data: any) => api.post('/subscriptions', data)` (frontend/src/lib/api.ts:459). `grep -rni razorpay frontend/src frontend/index.html` returns exactly one hit — a hard-coded demo string in AdminAuditPage.tsx:35 — so checkout.js is never loaded and no `new window.Razorpay(...)` handler exists. Server side returns 
- Fix: Split the flow: POST /subscriptions should only create a PENDING invoice + Razorpay order and return {orderId, keyId, amount}; the subscription row must stay PENDING/INCOMPLETE until the webhook confirms. In SubscriptionPage.handleSubscribe, load https://checkout.razorpay.com/v1/checkout.js, open `new window.Razorpay({key, order_id, amount, handler})`, and only refresh state/toast from the handler

**With Razorpay unconfigured, POST /subscriptions grants a full paid period AND credits referral rewards with no payment (repeatable indefinitely)**
- `packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:171` — *business-logic*
- Impact: In the current environment (no Razorpay keys anywhere) each click of a paid plan creates a fresh 1-month/1-year subscription and, on top of that, sets user.has_paid_plan = true and credits ₹100 / 30 redeemable days to whoever referred them. Two throwaway accounts can farm unlimited free subscription days; clicking the same plan repeatedly renews access forever.
- Evidence: Razorpay is never initialised because the key is empty — `grep -c RAZORPAY .env` = 0 and `.env.example` = 0, and `function getRazorpay() { if (!razorpay && RAZORPAY_KEY_ID) {...} return razorpay; }` (subscription.service.ts:15-22) returns null, so `razorpayOrderId` stays undefined. That is precisely the branch that self-certifies payment: `if (!isFree && !razorpayOrderId) { await this._triggerFirstPaidPurchaseForBusiness(businessId); }` (subscription.service.ts:171-173), which does `data: { has_paid_plan: true }` (subscription.service.ts:199-201) and `data: { status: ReferralStatus.COMPLETED, reward_amount: new Prisma.Decimal(100), reward_credited: true, redeemable_days: { increment: 30 } }`
- Fix: Delete the `!razorpayOrderId` "assume paid" branch — first-paid-purchase must only be triggered from a verified `payment.captured` webhook (or an admin manual-subscription action). If no payment gateway is configured, fail the request (`throw new BadRequestError('Payments unavailable')`) instead of provisioning the plan.

**POST /referrals/first-paid-purchase is callable by any logged-in user and self-grants paid status + referral rewards**
- `packages/referral-service/src/routes/referral.routes.ts:22` — *authorization*
- Impact: A trial user can call this endpoint once with their own token to set has_paid_plan = true — unlocking the referral dashboard/code generation that the 400 "You need an active paid plan" check is supposed to protect — and to complete any APPLIED referral, crediting 30 redeemable subscription days to an accomplice account. Free subscription extensions are mintable at will.
- Evidence: The route is documented as internal but carries only the generic auth middleware — no super-admin guard, no service-to-service secret: `router.use(authenticateToken);` (referral.routes.ts:7) then `// Internal: called after first paid purchase ...` / `router.post('/first-paid-purchase', ctrl.handleFirstPaidPurchase);` (referral.routes.ts:21-22), contrast with the line below it `router.post('/:referralId/credit', requireSuperAdmin, ctrl.creditReward);` (referral.routes.ts:25). The controller takes the subject from the caller's own token: `const userId = (req as unknown as AuthenticatedRequest).user?.userId; const result = await referralService.handleFirstPaidPurchase(userId);` (referral.contro
- Fix: Remove the HTTP route entirely (subscription-service already performs the same work in-process), or gate it behind `requireSuperAdmin` plus an `x-internal-secret` shared-secret check and take the target userId from the body rather than the caller's token.

### WhatsApp AI service

**Global sanitizeInput destroys the webhook's raw Buffer body — every inbound WhatsApp message is either 401'd or silently dropped**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/index.ts:90` — *broken-pipeline*
- Impact: The entire WhatsApp product is dead on arrival. With WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_SECRET set (production), every message from Meta gets HTTP 401 and Meta eventually disables the webhook. With no secret (dev), the request 200s but the payload is unrecognisable, so processWebhookPayload returns on the first line and the user never gets a reply — with zero error logged.
- Evidence: index.ts:85-90 registers raw-body parsing for the webhook and then applies sanitizeInput to EVERY route including it: `app.use('/api/v1/wa/webhook', express.raw({ type: 'application/json' }));` ... `app.use(sanitizeInput);`. sanitize.ts:93-97 recurses into anything object-typed: `if (req.body && typeof req.body === 'object') { req.body = sanitizeValue(req.body); }` and sanitize.ts:76-82 rebuilds it as a plain object: `if (value && typeof value === 'object') { const sanitized: Record<string, unknown> = {}; for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(value)) { sanitized[k] = sanitizeValue(v, depth + 1); } return sanitized; }` — a Buffer is object-typed, so Object.entries yields index→byte pairs. Verif
- Fix: Do not apply sanitizeInput globally. Mount it per-router on the JSON API routers only (conversationRouter/agentRouter/mcpRouter/adminRouter/metricsRouter/sendRouter), or guard it: `if (Buffer.isBuffer(req.body) || typeof req.body !== 'object') return next();` at the top of sanitizeInput. Additionally use `express.json({ verify: (req, _res, buf) => { (req as any).rawBody = buf; } })` and have verif

**Dashboard double-prefixes /api/v1 — all three of its endpoints 404 at the gateway**
- `frontend/src/components/whatsapp-ai/WhatsAppAIDashboard.tsx:4` — *wrong-path*
- Impact: Every request the dashboard makes goes to /api/v1/api/v1/wa/... The gateway's pathFilter is '/api/v1/wa', which does not match, so nothing is proxied and the page shows zero agent cards, no classification result, and no chat reply — forever.
- Evidence: WhatsAppAIDashboard.tsx:4 `const API_BASE = import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || 'http://localhost:3000';` and frontend/.env:1 sets `VITE_API_URL=/api/v1`. So API_BASE === '/api/v1' and WhatsAppAIDashboard.tsx:40 `await axios.get(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/wa/agents/status`)` resolves to `/api/v1/api/v1/wa/agents/status`; same for line 51 (`${API_BASE}/api/v1/wa/agents/classify`) and line 75 (`${API_BASE}/api/v1/wa/agents/test`). The gateway only registers the prefix at api-gateway/src/index.ts:157-159 `'/api/v1/wa': { target: serviceHost('whatsapp-ai-service', SERVICE_PORTS.WHATSAPP_AI_SERVICE) }` matched via `pathFilter: path` (index.ts:168). Contrast frontend/src/lib/api.ts:5 which correctly treats t
- Fix: Delete the hand-rolled axios client and use the shared client: `api.get('/wa/agents/status')`, `api.post('/wa/agents/classify', { text })`, `api.post('/wa/agents/test', { text })` from frontend/src/lib/api.ts — which also attaches the Bearer token and x-business-id headers this component omits entirely.

**UserResolutionService never queries the database — any phone matching /^[6-9]\d{9}$/ is granted OWNER of a fabricated business**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/services/user-resolution.ts:78` — *auth-bypass*
- Impact: Anyone who can reach the webhook (i.e. any WhatsApp number that messages the business line) is treated as an onboarded OWNER of a business whose id is invented from their own phone number. There is no membership check, no User lookup, no BusinessUser lookup. Every downstream entry is attributed to a business that does not exist, and the onboarding gate that is supposed to block unregistered numbers never fires for any valid Indian mobile number.
- Evidence: user-resolution.ts:68-78 short-circuits to the mock in BOTH branches, so DATABASE_URL being set changes nothing: `if (!config.database.url) { return this.mockResolution(...); }` then inside the try `// TODO: In production, query Prisma:` ... `// For now, use the session/mock approach` ... `return this.mockResolution(normalisedPhone, senderName, session);`. mockResolution at user-resolution.ts:155-166 fabricates the identity: `if (/^[6-9]\d{9}$/.test(phone)) { return { resolved: true, user: { userId: `user_${phone}`, ..., businessId: session?.selectedBusinessId || `biz_${phone}`, businessName: `${senderName}'s Business`, role: 'OWNER', isOnboarded: true } ... }`. The caller trusts it uncondit
- Fix: Implement the real resolution: look up `prisma.user.findFirst({ where: { phone: normalisedPhone } })`, then `prisma.businessUser.findMany({ where: { userId } , include: { business: true } })`, and return resolved:false (onboarding message) when either lookup is empty. Until that exists, gate mockResolution behind an explicit `WA_ALLOW_MOCK_USERS=true` flag and hard-fail (resolved:false) when it is

**Hisaab agent returns hardcoded fabricated financials to end users as if they were real reports**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/agents/hisaab.agent.ts:59` — *fabricated-data*
- Impact: A business owner asking "Aaj ki bikri?", "Ram ka baaki?", "GST kitna hai?" or "stock kitna hai?" over WhatsApp receives confidently formatted invented numbers — ₹25,000 outstanding, ₹1,25,000 sales, ₹9,100 GST payable due 20 Jul, ₹6,82,000 cash — with no indication they are placeholders. Users can file GST or chase payments on numbers that came from a string literal.
- Evidence: Every report method returns a literal. hisaab.agent.ts:56-65 `if (partyName) { // TODO: Query database` / `return `📊 *${partyName} ka Hisaab:*\n\n` + `💰 Total Baaki: ₹25,000\n` + `📋 Pending Bills: 3\n\n` + `1. Bill #RT-450 (2 Jul) — ₹19,000\n` ...`. hisaab.agent.ts:110-115 `return `📊 *Aaj ka Hisaab (${today}):*\n\n` + `📈 *Bikri (Sales):* ₹1,25,000 (8 entries)\n` + `📉 *Kharidi (Purchase):* ₹85,000 (5 entries)\n` ...`. hisaab.agent.ts:96-99 `*Net Payable:*\n` + ` CGST: ₹3,300\n` ... ` *Total: ₹9,100*`. hisaab.agent.ts:170-181 invents named bank accounts: ` HDFC Current: ₹4,52,000\n` + ` SBI Savings: ₹1,85,000`. The orchestrator ships the string straight to WhatsApp — orchestrator.ts:401-
- Fix: Wire these six methods to the real services through the gateway (ledger-service for outstanding, sales/purchase for daily summary, billing for GST) using the resolved businessId. Until then return an explicit "Report feature abhi available nahi hai" message rather than sample data — never emit a number the system did not compute.

**Unconfigured Azure OpenAI makes every bill photo produce a fake ₹10,000 purchase from "Sample Vendor" and asks the user to post it**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/services/azure-openai.service.ts:81` — *fabricated-data*
- Impact: A user photographs a real bill; the bot replies with a fully formed Purchase Entry for ₹10,000 from "Sample Vendor", bill INV-001, dated 03/07/2026, and asks "Post kar doon?". The mock is indistinguishable from a real extraction (confidence is reported as 0.85), so the user is invited to confirm a transaction that has nothing to do with their bill.
- Evidence: azure-openai.service.ts:79-87 returns a hardcoded invoice when credentials are missing: `if (!this.endpoint || !this.apiKey) { console.warn('⚠️ Azure OpenAI not configured - using mock vision response'); return JSON.stringify({ partyName: 'Sample Vendor', billNumber: 'INV-001', totalAmount: 10000, date: '03/07/2026' }); }`. dastaveez.agent.ts:70-72 guarantees that path is taken (`if (!endpoint || !apiKey) { return 'UNKNOWN'; }` → processGenericDocument → processWithVision) and dastaveez.agent.ts:244-253 stamps it as a high-confidence extraction: `const result = await openai.chatCompletionWithVision(prompt, ...); const parsed = JSON.parse(result); return { documentType, confidence: 0.85, extr
- Fix: Make the mock opt-in (`if (!endpoint && process.env.WA_ALLOW_AI_MOCKS === 'true')`) and otherwise throw, so dastaveez's catch produces createEmptyResult and the user is told document reading is unavailable. If a mock must stay for tests, tag the result (`confidence: 0`, `documentType: 'UNKNOWN'`) so enrichWithDocumentData cannot promote it into entities.

**Every text-based transaction entry fails validation: the rule-based path never extracts PARTY_NAME, but Jaanch requires it, and the ≥0.85 short-circuit means the AI path is never reached**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/agents/samajh.agent.ts:314` — *logic*
- Impact: 100% of the product's flagship text flows fail. "Ram ko 15 hazaar diye", "50 bag cement aaya", "Payment mila 25000 Suresh se", "Invoice bana do Ramesh ke liye 10000" — the exact strings shipped as the dashboard's quick-test chips and as admin/test-scenarios — all return "❌ Entry mein kuch problem hai: • Party ka naam nahi mila" and stop. There is no path to a successful entry.
- Evidence: extractEntitiesRuleBased (samajh.agent.ts:314-430) emits only AMOUNT, GSTIN, DATE, UPI_REF, QUANTITY, UNIT and PAYMENT_MODE — there is no PARTY_NAME/ITEM_NAME/RATE branch anywhere in those 116 lines. Yet jaanch.agent.ts:192-198 requires it: `const requiredByIntent: Record<string, string[]> = { 'PURCHASE_ENTRY': ['PARTY_NAME', 'AMOUNT'], 'SALES_ENTRY': ['PARTY_NAME', 'AMOUNT'], 'VENDOR_PAYMENT': ['PARTY_NAME', 'AMOUNT'], 'CUSTOMER_RECEIPT': ['PARTY_NAME', 'AMOUNT'], 'EXPENSE_ENTRY': ['AMOUNT'] };` and jaanch.agent.ts:201-214 pushes MISSING_FIELD for each absent type. The AI path — the only code that can produce PARTY_NAME (samajh.agent.ts:203-208 maps `parsed.entities`) — is unreachable for t
- Fix: Add PARTY_NAME extraction to extractEntitiesRuleBased (the classifier patterns already locate it — e.g. capture group 1 of /(\w+)\s*ko\s*\d+/ and /(?:se|from)\s+([A-Z][\w ]+)/), and treat rule-based results as partial: when a required field for the matched intent is missing, fall through to aiClassify instead of returning at 0.88.

**Party-clarification dead-end: Pehchaan can never return a match, and the resulting pendingClarification is unescapable**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/agents/pehchaan.agent.ts:134` — *logic*
- Impact: Once a message does carry a PARTY_NAME (document flow or AI flow), the user is told "koi party nahi mili" and the conversation is pinned into pendingClarification with an empty option list. Every subsequent message — including the "cancel" the bot itself suggests — returns "Sahi number ya naam batayein. Ya \"cancel\" likhein." The user is permanently locked out of the bot with no reset path.
- Evidence: pehchaan.agent.ts:127-135 always returns zero matches: `private async fuzzyDatabaseSearch(query: string, _tenantId: string): Promise<EntityMatch[]> { const variants = this.generateFuzzyVariants(query); // This would normally query the database // For now, return empty - will be connected to Prisma console.log(...); return []; }` and searchWithAzureAI returns [] when unconfigured (pehchaan.agent.ts:90 `if (!endpoint || !apiKey) return [];`), so resolveEntity hits pehchaan.agent.ts:44-51 `if (rankedMatches.length === 0) { return { resolved: false, matches: [], needsClarification: true, clarificationMessage: ... } }`. orchestrator.ts:305-312 persists the dead state with an empty options array: 
- Fix: Add an escape hatch at the top of handleClarificationResponse/handleApprovalResponse: if the text matches /^(cancel|band|ruko|stop|exit)$/i, clear pendingClarification/pendingApproval and reply that it was cancelled. Also treat an empty `options` array as unrecoverable (clear the state immediately), enforce pendingApproval.expiresAt, and never set needsClarification with zero matches — return reso

**Media pipeline writes attacker-controlled WhatsApp filenames straight into path.join — arbitrary file write outside media-store**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/services/media-pipeline.ts:67` — *security*
- Impact: Any WhatsApp user who can reach the webhook can send a document whose filename is `../../../../app/dist/index.js` (or `../../.env`) and overwrite arbitrary files that the service process can write, with content of their choosing. In local/dev storage mode (the default, since AZURE_STORAGE_CONNECTION_STRING defaults to '') this is remote code execution on the service host.
- Evidence: media-pipeline.ts:67 takes the caller-supplied name verbatim in preference to the safe hashed name: `const filename = originalFilename || `${Date.now()}_${hash}${extension}`;` and media-pipeline.ts:162-163 joins and writes it with no normalisation or containment check: `const filePath = path.join(dirPath, filename); fs.writeFileSync(filePath, buffer);`. The value originates in the Meta payload and is passed through unvalidated: message-normaliser.ts:131 `filename: message.document?.filename,` → webhook.routes.ts:162-166 `const stored = await mediaPipeline.processMedia(normalised.media.mediaId, normalised.media.mimeType, normalised.media.filename);`. Local mode is the default: media-pipeline.
- Fix: Never use the remote filename as a path component. Always store as `${Date.now()}_${hash}${extension}` and keep the original only as metadata. If a human-readable name is wanted, sanitise with `path.basename(originalFilename).replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9._-]/g, '_')` and assert `path.resolve(filePath).startsWith(path.resolve(dirPath) + path.sep)` before writing.

**POST /api/v1/wa/send is a public, unauthenticated WhatsApp send relay whenever INTERNAL_SERVICE_KEY is unset**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/routes/send.routes.ts:22` — *security*
- Impact: The route is exposed through the gateway's /api/v1/wa prefix and its own guard opts out when the key is missing. Anyone on the internet can POST arbitrary text, buttons, lists, templates and reactions to any phone number from the company's verified WhatsApp Business number — phishing sent from a trusted brand identity, plus uncapped Meta messaging spend and template-quality damage.
- Evidence: send.routes.ts:19-28 skips auth entirely when the key is absent outside production: `const internalKey = process.env.INTERNAL_SERVICE_KEY; if (!internalKey) { if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') { res.status(401)...; return; } return next(); }` — and send.routes.ts:40 applies it as the only protection: `sendRouter.use(verifyInternalKey);`. index.ts:128 mounts it publicly with no API-key middleware at all (unlike every sibling router): `app.use('/api/v1/wa/send', apiRateLimiter, sendRouter);`. The gateway forwards the prefix unconditionally — api-gateway/src/index.ts:157-159 `'/api/v1/wa': { target: serviceHost('whatsapp-ai-service', ...) }`. The handler then sends whatever it is given
- Fix: Require the internal key unconditionally (`if (!internalKey) { res.status(503)...; return; }`) and compare with crypto.timingSafeEqual. Better: do not expose /send through the gateway at all — remove '/api/v1/wa/send' from the proxied surface (or add a gateway-level deny rule) so it is reachable only on the internal network.

**Dev-mode auth bypass grants read+write+admin+super on every /api/v1/wa route through the public gateway**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/middleware/auth.ts:107` — *security*
- Impact: When NODE_ENV is not 'production' and no WHATSAPP_AI_* keys are configured — which is the checked-in default (root .env contains none of them, and the service directory has only .env.example) — every request with no credentials whatsoever is handed the full permission set. Unauthenticated callers can read and wipe any user's conversation history, mutate their preferences, execute MCP tools, and read admin config and cost metrics.
- Evidence: auth.ts:104-112 `export function apiKeyAuth(requiredPermissions: string[] = ['read']) { return (req, res, next) => { // Skip auth in development if no keys configured if (config.nodeEnv === 'development' && API_KEYS.length === 0) { (req as AuthenticatedRequest).apiKeyId = 'dev-mode'; (req as AuthenticatedRequest).tenantId = 'dev'; (req as AuthenticatedRequest).permissions = ['read', 'write', 'admin', 'super']; return next(); }`. API_KEYS is empty unless env vars exist (auth.ts:31-71, each push guarded by `if (process.env.WHATSAPP_AI_*_KEY)`), and config/index.ts:11 defaults the mode in: `nodeEnv: process.env.NODE_ENV || 'development'`. Confirmed the keys are not configured anywhere: root .en
- Fix: Fail closed: if API_KEYS.length === 0, return 503 'service not configured' instead of granting access, and require an explicit `WA_ALLOW_UNAUTH_DEV=true` opt-in for the local-development shortcut. Never grant 'admin'/'super' from a fallback branch.

**Conversation routes are mounted with read-only permission but include destructive writes, and accept any :userId with no ownership check**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/index.ts:113` — *cross-tenant*
- Impact: A holder of the read-only dashboard key (or any caller at all in the default dev config) can enumerate any WhatsApp user's full conversation state — message history, learned party mappings, pending approvals with amounts — by guessing/knowing a phone number, and can also overwrite that user's preferences and wipe their history. Overwriting approvalThreshold removes the human approval gate for that user's future high-value entries.
- Evidence: index.ts:113 grants only read but mounts a router containing PATCH and DELETE: `app.use('/api/v1/wa/conversations', apiRateLimiter, requireRead, conversationRouter);` where requireRead = apiKeyAuth(['read']) (auth.ts:182). The router's writes are unguarded — conversation.routes.ts:53-60 `conversationRouter.patch('/:userId/preferences', async (req, res) => { const { userId } = req.params; const updates = req.body; const state = await memoryService.getConversationState(`whatsapp:${userId}`); state.preferences = { ...state.preferences, ...updates }; await memoryService.saveConversationState(state);` and conversation.routes.ts:72-81 `conversationRouter.delete('/:userId', ... state.messageHistory
- Fix: Split the mounts: keep GETs under requireRead and re-mount PATCH/DELETE behind requireWrite (or requireAdmin). Then bind identity: derive the userId from the authenticated principal (or verify the caller's business owns that WhatsApp number via BusinessUser) instead of trusting the path parameter, and validate the PATCH body with the already-written preferencesBodySchema.

**The approval flow tells the user "Entry post ho gayi!" although nothing is ever persisted anywhere**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/agents/orchestrator.ts:451` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: The user says "Haan" to approve a ₹2,00,000 entry and receives "✅ Entry post ho gayi! (₹2,00,000) 📝 Transaction ID: <uuid>". No purchase, sale, payment, expense, stock movement, party or item is written to any database or posted to any service. Users believe their books are updated when nothing happened — the books silently diverge from reality.
- Evidence: orchestrator.ts:449-454 claims success with no call of any kind before it: `if (messageText.includes('haan') || messageText.includes('yes') || messageText.includes('approve')) { // Post the transaction state.response = { text: `✅ Entry post ho gayi! (₹${approval.amount.toLocaleString('en-IN')})\n\n📝 Transaction ID: ${approval.transactionId}` }; state.conversationState.pendingApproval = undefined; }` — the comment "// Post the transaction" is the entire implementation. Every Lekha writer only formats a string and returns `posted: false`: lekha.agent.ts:102-122 `return { transactionId, confirmationMessage: message, transaction: { ... status: 'pending_approval', ... }, posted: false };` (ident
- Fix: Either implement posting (call the gateway's /api/v1/{purchases,sales,ledger} endpoints with the resolved businessId and the user's token, and only then confirm with the real returned id), or change the copy to state that the entry was captured but not posted. Never emit a success confirmation from a branch that performs no write.


## HIGH (91)

### Admin console

**Manual Subscription Purchase silently discards paymentMode, paymentRef and notes — nothing is ever persisted**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:249` — *field-dropped*
- Impact: The dialog's Payment Mode dropdown (Cash/UPI/Bank Transfer/Cheque/Card/Other), Payment Reference / Transaction ID and Admin Notes fields are collected, shown in the Purchase Summary, and then thrown away. After 'Subscription purchased successfully! Invoice generated.' there is no record anywhere of how the cash was collected or which receipt/cheque number it was — the exact reconciliation data this offline-payment feature exists to capture. The paid Invoice row has no payment-mode column at all.
- Evidence: Frontend collects and sends them — AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:191-199 `await adminApi.createManualSubscription({ businessId, planId, billingCycle, paymentMode: subForm.paymentMode, paymentRef: subForm.paymentRef || undefined, amount: subForm.amount, notes: subForm.notes || undefined })` (fields rendered at AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:503-552). Service signature accepts them but never writes them — admin.service.ts:249-254 `async createManualSubscription(data: { businessId; planId; billingCycle; paymentMode: string; paymentRef?: string; amount: number; notes?: string })`; the only writes are `prisma.subscription.create` (admin.service.ts:278-288 — business_id, plan_id, billing_cycle, status, p
- Fix: Add `payment_mode`, `payment_ref` and `notes` columns to Invoice (or a ManualPayment record) in schema.prisma + a migration, and persist `data.paymentMode/paymentRef/notes` in the `prisma.invoice.create` call at admin.service.ts:295. Until the columns exist, at minimum store them in the audit log's new_data so the information is recoverable.

**Every Platform Settings boolean round-trips as an object, so all 6 toggles read back as ON and the next Save corrupts the stored value**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminSettingsPage.tsx:65` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: Admin turns Maintenance Mode / User Registration / Email / SMS / Auto-Backup OFF, sees 'Platform settings saved successfully', reloads the page and every switch is back ON. Worse, the second Save writes the object back: the server sees a non-boolean and forces `is_enabled: true`, so a deliberately disabled flag is permanently flipped to enabled in the DB.
- Evidence: Server writes booleans into `config` as `{ value: <bool> }` and always sets config — admin.service.ts:510-516 `data: { is_enabled: typeof value === 'boolean' ? value : true, config: typeof value === 'object' ? value : { value } }`. Read path therefore never falls through to the `{ enabled }` shape — admin.service.ts:495-498 `for (const flag of flags) { const key = flag.feature.replace('PLATFORM_',''); settings[key] = flag.config || { enabled: flag.is_enabled }; }` (config is `{value:false}`, which is truthy). Frontend expects `.enabled` — AdminSettingsPage.tsx:65-66 `...(d.maintenanceMode !== undefined && { maintenanceMode: d.maintenanceMode.enabled ?? d.maintenanceMode })`. `.enabled` is un
- Fix: Make getSettings return the primitive: `settings[key] = (flag.config as any)?.value !== undefined ? (flag.config as any).value : flag.is_enabled;` and read it uniformly on the client (`d.maintenanceMode?.value ?? settings.maintenanceMode`). Also stop coercing unknown types to `is_enabled: true` in updateSettings.

**All 12 Platform Settings are write-only — nothing in the codebase ever reads them**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminSettingsPage.tsx:82` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Toggling Maintenance Mode does not block anyone; turning off User Registration does not stop signups; OTP Expiry, Session Timeout, Max Businesses per User, Max Users per Business, Email/SMS notification switches and the backup schedule change nothing at all. The admin gets 'Platform settings saved successfully' and reasonably believes the platform is now in maintenance mode.
- Evidence: handleSave persists them as PLATFORM_* feature flags — AdminSettingsPage.tsx:85-98 `await adminApi.updateSettings({ appName, supportEmail, maxBusinessesPerUser, maxUsersPerBusiness, maintenanceMode, registrationEnabled, otpExpiryMinutes, sessionTimeoutMinutes, ... })` → admin.service.ts:502-531 writes `feature: \`PLATFORM_${key}\``. No consumer exists: `grep -rn "PLATFORM_" packages/*/src --include=*.ts | grep -v admin-service` returns zero hits, and `grep -rni "maintenanceMode|maintenance_mode|registrationEnabled"` across packages/*/src and frontend/src matches only AdminSettingsPage.tsx itself (lines 19,20,34,35,65,66,90,91,171,172,181,182). Concretely, session length is hardcoded at packa
- Fix: Either wire the flags up (a gateway middleware reading PLATFORM_maintenanceMode, an auth-service check on PLATFORM_registrationEnabled before OTP send/register, OTP TTL from PLATFORM_otpExpiryMinutes) or remove the inert controls so the page only exposes settings that take effect.

**'Suspend User' has no effect — neither OTP login nor token refresh checks is_active**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:168` — *auth-enforcement*
- Impact: The confirm dialog promises 'They will not be able to log in.' and the API returns 200, but a suspended user can immediately log in again through the normal phone+OTP flow, and any already-issued session keeps refreshing forever (7-day rotating refresh token). Suspension is cosmetic — it only changes the badge in the admin table.
- Evidence: Admin write happens correctly: AdminUsersPage.tsx:183-186 `case 'suspend': await adminApi.toggleUser(user.id, false)` → api.ts:480 `api.patch(\`/admin/users/${id}/status\`, { isActive })` → admin.controller.ts:24 `adminService.toggleUserStatus(req.params.userId, req.body.isActive)` → admin.service.ts:126-129 `prisma.user.update({ where:{id:userId}, data:{ is_active: isActive } })`. Dialog copy at AdminUsersPage.tsx:223 `return \`Suspend "${user.name}"? They will not be able to log in.\``. The primary login path never reads it — auth.service.ts:167-190 verifyOTP: `const user = await prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { phone }, include: {...} }); ... if (!user) { return {success:false, ... 'No a
- Fix: Add `if (!user.is_active) return { success:false, message:'Account suspended', code:'ACCOUNT_SUSPENDED' }` in verifyOTP (auth.service.ts ~line 190) and in refreshAccessToken (~line 474), and have toggleUserStatus(false) also `prisma.refreshToken.deleteMany({ where: { user_id: userId } })` so live sessions die immediately.

**'Delete User' always fails with 500 for any user who has a referral code (referrals.referrer_id is ON DELETE RESTRICT)**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:139` — *correctness*
- Impact: Clicking Delete User on essentially any real user (a Referral row is auto-created the first time they open the Referrals screen) raises a Postgres FK violation. The comment in the code claims cascades handle it, so nothing is caught: the admin sees 'Internal server error' (production) and the user is never deleted. The dialog had just promised 'will remove all their data'.
- Evidence: admin.service.ts:139-144 `async deleteUser(userId){ const user = await prisma.user.findUnique({where:{id:userId}}); if(!user) throw new NotFoundError('User'); /* Cascade deletes are configured in the schema, so just delete the user */ return prisma.user.delete({ where:{id:userId} }); }`. The FK is RESTRICT, not CASCADE — packages/shared/prisma/migrations/20260301173928_add_password_hash/migration.sql:1082 `ALTER TABLE "referrals" ADD CONSTRAINT "referrals_referrer_id_fkey" FOREIGN KEY ("referrer_id") REFERENCES "users"("id") ON DELETE RESTRICT ON UPDATE CASCADE;` (schema.prisma Referral: `referrer User @relation("ReferrerRelation", fields:[referrer_id], references:[id])` — required relation,
- Fix: Delete or reassign dependents inside a transaction before deleting the user: `await prisma.$transaction([prisma.referral.deleteMany({where:{referrer_id:userId}}), prisma.user.delete({where:{id:userId}})])` (also consider referrals where referred_id = userId), or change the relation to `onDelete: Cascade` in schema.prisma + migration.

**'Delete Plan' guard only counts ACTIVE/TRIAL subscriptions, so deleting a plan with EXPIRED/CANCELLED subs 500s (subscriptions.plan_id is ON DELETE RESTRICT)**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:473` — *correctness*
- Impact: A plan that looks safe to delete (no active subscribers) cannot be deleted if it has any historical subscription — and every Manual Subscription Purchase deliberately marks the previous subscription EXPIRED, so this is the normal state. The admin gets 'Internal server error' instead of the intended explanatory message, with no way to tell why.
- Evidence: Guard is status-scoped — admin.service.ts:477-485 `const activeSubCount = await prisma.subscription.count({ where: { plan_id: planId, status: { in: ['ACTIVE','TRIAL'] } } }); if (activeSubCount > 0) { throw new Error(...) } return prisma.plan.delete({ where: { id: planId } });`. The FK covers every status — migration.sql:1073 `ALTER TABLE "subscriptions" ADD CONSTRAINT "subscriptions_plan_id_fkey" FOREIGN KEY ("plan_id") REFERENCES "plans"("id") ON DELETE RESTRICT ON UPDATE CASCADE;`. EXPIRED rows are produced by the admin console itself — admin.service.ts:272-275 `await prisma.subscription.updateMany({ where: { business_id: data.businessId, status: { in: ['ACTIVE','TRIAL'] } }, data: { stat
- Fix: Count all subscriptions (`prisma.subscription.count({ where: { plan_id: planId } })`) and throw `new ConflictError(...)` (imported from ../shared) instead of a bare Error so the 409 + message reaches AdminPlansPage.tsx:175. For plans with only historical subs, offer soft-delete via `is_active: false` (the Plan model already has that column).

**Admin Dashboard MRR shows ₹NaNk and 4 secondary metric cards are blank — the page reads field names the dashboard endpoint does not return**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminDashboardPage.tsx:134` — *field-name-mismatch*
- Impact: On the very first admin screen the MRR tile literally renders '₹NaNk', and the New-today / New-this-week / Churn-rate / ARPU tiles render an empty value, '%' and '₹' with nothing in front. Because setStats replaces the whole object with the server payload, the placeholder defaults are wiped out rather than filled in.
- Evidence: Page reads `revenue`, `newUsersToday`, `newUsersThisWeek`, `churnRate`, `avgRevenuePerUser` — AdminDashboardPage.tsx:134 `<StatCard title="MRR" value={\`₹${(stats.revenue / 1000).toFixed(0)}k\`} ... />`, :145 `{stats.newUsersToday}`, :156 `{stats.newUsersThisWeek}`, :167 `{stats.churnRate}%`, :178 `₹{stats.avgRevenuePerUser}`. Server returns a different key for revenue and none of the other four — admin.service.ts:32-41 `return { totalUsers, totalBusinesses, activeSubscriptions, totalRevenue: Number(totalRevenue._sum.total_amount || 0), totalPurchases, totalSales, recentSignups, recentBusinesses };`. And the whole object is swapped in, not merged — AdminDashboardPage.tsx:93-95 `if (dashRes.s
- Fix: Rename to `totalRevenue` on the client (or alias it on the server) and compute the four missing metrics in getPlatformDashboard (new users today/this week via created_at ranges, churn from CANCELLED+EXPIRED subscriptions, ARPU = totalRevenue/totalUsers). Merge instead of replace: `setStats(prev => ({...prev, ...dashRes.value.data.data}))`.

**Subscriptions page stat tiles are hardwired to zero — analytics endpoint returns byStatus/byPlan/monthlyRevenue, page reads totalActive/totalRevenue/mrr/cashPayments**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:367` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: Active Subscriptions shows '0', Total Revenue '₹0', Monthly Recurring Revenue '₹0' and Cash Payments '0' on every successful load, even with hundreds of paying businesses. The three real aggregates the backend computes (per-status counts, per-plan counts, 12-month revenue) are fetched and then never rendered anywhere on the page.
- Evidence: AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:365-388 `<StatCard title="Active Subscriptions" value={analytics?.totalActive?.toLocaleString() || '0'} .../> <StatCard title="Total Revenue" value={formatCurrency(analytics?.totalRevenue || 0)} .../> <StatCard title="Monthly Recurring Revenue" value={formatCurrency(analytics?.mrr || 0)} .../> <StatCard title="Cash Payments" value={analytics?.cashPayments?.toLocaleString() || '0'} .../>`. admin.service.ts:646-667 returns only `{ byStatus, byPlan, monthlyRevenue }` (byStatus from `prisma.subscription.groupBy({ by:['status'], _count:true })`, monthlyRevenue from the `SELECT TO_CHAR(paid_at,'YYYY-MM') ... SUM(total_amount)::float` raw query). No totalActive / totalRev
- Fix: Add the four scalars to getSubscriptionAnalytics (`totalActive` = byStatus ACTIVE count, `totalRevenue` = sum of monthlyRevenue, `mrr` = latest month, and a cash-payment count once payment_mode is persisted), or derive them client-side from byStatus/monthlyRevenue. Note `cashPayments` is uncomputable until the payment-mode finding above is fixed.

**Business Revenue is hardcoded to 0 in the service, so the Revenue column and 2 of 4 stat tiles are always ₹0**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:373` — *correctness*
- Impact: On /admin/businesses every row's Revenue reads ₹0, and 'Total Revenue' and 'Avg. Revenue/Biz' are always ₹0 because they are summed from that field. The same 0 is re-displayed in the business detail dialog and exported to CSV, so the exported report is wrong too.
- Evidence: admin.service.ts:357-376 transform: `return { id: b.id, name: b.name, ... users_count: b._count?.business_users || 0, transactions_count: (b._count?.purchases || 0) + (b._count?.sales || 0), revenue: 0, created_at: b.created_at, last_active: b.updated_at };` — literal `revenue: 0`, and the `include` at admin.service.ts:340-348 never selects invoices at all. Consumers treat it as real — AdminBusinessesPage.tsx:307-310 `{ key:'revenue', header:'Revenue', render:(b)=> <span className="font-medium">{formatCurrency(b.revenue)}</span> }`, :250 `const totalRevenue = businesses.reduce((s,b)=> s + b.revenue, 0);`, :374-375 `<StatCard title="Total Revenue" value={formatCurrency(totalRevenue)} .../> <S
- Fix: Include paid-invoice totals in listBusinesses — e.g. add `invoices: { where: { status: 'PAID' }, select: { total_amount: true } }` to the include and set `revenue: Number(b.invoices.reduce((s, i) => s + Number(i.total_amount), 0))` — or drop the column and the two tiles.

**Plans page subscriber counts and MRR are always 0, and the delete confirmation understates the blast radius**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:420` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: Every plan card reads '0 subscribers · ₹0 MRR' and the Total Subscribers / Total MRR tiles are 0, so the page cannot be used to see plan adoption. Worse, the delete dialog states 'This will affect 0 subscribers' for a plan with hundreds of them, inviting a destructive click that then fails with an opaque 500.
- Evidence: admin.service.ts:420-422 `async listPlans() { return prisma.plan.findMany({ orderBy: { sort_order: 'asc' } }); }` — no `_count`, no subscriber aggregation. Page reads fields that therefore never arrive — AdminPlansPage.tsx:20-23 `interface AdminPlan extends Plan { subscriber_count?: number; revenue?: number; }`, :179-180 `const totalSubscribers = plans.reduce((s,p)=> s + (p.subscriber_count || 0), 0); const totalRevenue = plans.reduce((s,p)=> s + (p.revenue || 0), 0);`, :282-283 `<span className="text-muted-foreground">{plan.subscriber_count || 0} subscribers</span><span className="font-medium">{formatCurrency(plan.revenue || 0)} MRR</span>`, :441 `description={\`...This will affect ${delete
- Fix: Return counts from listPlans: `prisma.plan.findMany({ orderBy:{sort_order:'asc'}, include:{ _count:{ select:{ subscriptions:true } } } })` and map `subscriber_count = _count.subscriptions` (count only ACTIVE/TRIAL if that is the intent), plus a revenue aggregate over paid invoices joined through subscriptions.

### Auth & session

**401'd requests hang forever when a refresh is already in flight from App.tsx's proactive refresh (queue is never drained)**
- `frontend/src/lib/api.ts:191` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: The user switches back to the app tab (or the 12-minute timer fires) while a page request 401s. The 401'd request is pushed onto failedQueue and its promise is never resolved or rejected, so the awaiting page (dashboard, list, form submit) spins on its loading state indefinitely with no error toast and no retry. Only a manual page reload recovers.
- Evidence: frontend/src/lib/api.ts:190-200 queues on `isRefreshing || refreshPromise`: if (isRefreshing || refreshPromise) { return new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => { failedQueue.push({ resolve, reject }); }) .then((newToken) => { originalRequest.headers.Authorization = `Bearer ${newToken}`; return api.request(originalRequest); }) but processQueue() is only ever called from the *other* branch (api.ts:208, 217, 223), i.e. only when the interceptor itself started the refresh. `refreshPromise` is module state set by refreshAccessToken() (api.ts:42-45 `if (refreshPromise) return refreshPromise; refreshPromise = (async () => {...`) and `isRefreshing` is set only inside the interceptor (api.ts:203). 
- Fix: Make refreshAccessToken() itself own the queue: call processQueue(null, token) on success and processQueue(err, null) on failure inside refreshAccessToken's then/catch, and drop the separate `isRefreshing` flag. Also add a safety timeout that rejects anything still in failedQueue.

**Logout never revokes the server-side session — authApi.logout() is defined but never called**
- `frontend/src/components/layout/AppLayout.tsx:65` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: The user clicks "Logout" in the sidebar (AppLayout) or in the admin sidebar (AdminLayout). Redux/localStorage are cleared and they land on /login, but the refresh-token row stays in the database, valid for 7 days. Anyone who captured/kept that refresh token (shared machine, copied localStorage, XSS payload, backup) can mint fresh 15-minute access tokens for a week after the user believes they logged out.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/layout/AppLayout.tsx:65-69 — no API call: const handleLogout = () => { dispatch(logout()); dispatch(clearBusiness()); navigate('/login'); }; Identical in frontend/src/components/layout/AdminLayout.tsx:40-44. The endpoint exists and is wired end-to-end (frontend/src/lib/api.ts:255 `logout: () => api.post('/auth/logout')`; packages/auth-service/src/routes/auth.routes.ts:50 `router.post('/logout', authenticateToken, logout)`; auth.service.ts:490-502 deleteMany on refresh_tokens) but `grep -rn "authApi\." frontend/src` returns only me/sendOtp/verifyOtp/login/register — authApi.logout has zero call sites.
- Fix: In both handleLogout functions, `await authApi.logout()` (fire-and-forget with a catch) before dispatching the local logout, and also call it from the interceptor's forced-logout paths where a refresh token is still present.

**Deactivating a user does not lock them out — neither token verification nor refresh checks user.is_active**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:462` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: An admin clicks "Suspend" on a user in Admin > Users. The user's browser keeps working: every 12 minutes App.tsx silently refreshes, refreshAccessToken issues a brand-new access token plus a brand-new 7-day refresh token with no is_active check, and authenticateToken never touches the database. The suspended (or fired) employee retains full read/write access to the business indefinitely.
- Evidence: Refresh path performs no is_active check — packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:462-484: const storedToken = await prisma.refreshToken.findUnique({ where: { token: refreshToken }, include: { user: true } }); if (!storedToken || storedToken.expires_at < new Date()) { ... return { success: false, ... }; } await prisma.refreshToken.deleteMany({ where: { id: storedToken.id } }); const newAccessToken = this.generateAccessToken(storedToken.user); const newRefreshToken = await this.generateRefreshToken(storedToken.user.id); (compare loginWithEmail, auth.service.ts:387-389, which *does* check `if (!user.is_active) return { success:false, message:'Account is deactivated...' }`). Access-
- Fix: In refreshAccessToken, reject when `!storedToken.user.is_active` (and delete the user's refresh tokens). In admin.service.toggleUserStatus, add `prisma.refreshToken.deleteMany({ where: { user_id: userId } })` when deactivating. Optionally cache-check is_active in authenticateToken.

**OTP verification has no attempt limit and OTPs come from Math.random — 6-digit codes are brute-forceable**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:137` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: Anyone who knows a registered phone number can request one OTP and then POST /api/v1/auth/verify-otp repeatedly; there is no per-phone attempt counter, no lockout, and no rate limiting anywhere in the stack, so the 1,000,000-code space can be walked inside the 5-minute window and the attacker receives a full access + refresh token pair for that account.
- Evidence: verifyOTP does a plain compare with no attempt accounting — packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:150-165: const storedOTP = await redis.get(otpKey); if (!storedOTP) { return { success: false, message: 'OTP expired or not found' }; } if (storedOTP !== otp) { return { success: false, message: 'Invalid OTP' }; } await redis.del(otpKey); REDIS_KEYS.OTP_ATTEMPTS is only ever incremented in sendOTP (auth.service.ts:107-115), never in verifyOTP. HTTP-level limiting is deliberately absent at both tiers: packages/auth-service/src/routes/auth.routes.ts:15-17 "NO IN-PROCESS RATE LIMITING" and packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:69-71 "NO IN-MEMORY RATE LIMITING" / index.ts:227 `Rate limitin
- Fix: Increment a Redis counter keyed on the phone inside verifyOTP, delete the OTP and return a lockout after ~5 wrong attempts, and generate the OTP with crypto.randomInt.

**Invalid (non-expired) tokens return 403, which the interceptor ignores — the session wedges and the user is bounced to "create a business"**
- `packages/shared/src/middleware/auth.middleware.ts:31` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: If a token fails signature verification rather than expiry (JWT_SECRET rotated, a service deployed without JWT_SECRET so it falls back to the hard-coded default, a token from another environment), every request returns 403. The interceptor only reacts to 401, so there is no refresh and no logout: the app stays "authenticated", /auth/me and /business both fail, businesses stays empty, and ProtectedRoute redirects the user to /business/new. The user sees a "create your business" wizard that also 403s, with no way back to /login except clicking Logout.
- Evidence: packages/shared/src/middleware/auth.middleware.ts:23-35 — expiry is 401, everything else is 403: if (error instanceof jwt.TokenExpiredError) { res.status(401).json({ success:false, message:'Token has expired' }); return; } res.status(403).json({ success: false, message: 'Invalid token' }); The interceptor only handles 401 — frontend/src/lib/api.ts:173 `if (error.response?.status === 401 && originalRequest) {` (403 falls through to `return Promise.reject(error)` at api.ts:242). The mount fetch then swallows it and keeps the session — frontend/src/App.tsx:129-135: } catch { if (!store.getState().auth.isAuthenticated) { dispatch(logout()); } and ProtectedRoute redirects on the now-empty busines
- Fix: Return 401 (not 403) for every token-verification failure in authenticateToken; reserve 403 for RBAC denials. Keep the interceptor's 401 handling as the single recovery path.

### Business management

**GET /business/:id has no membership check — any business record is readable by id**
- `packages/business-service/src/routes/business.routes.ts:24` — *authorization*
- Impact: Any authenticated user can read any other tenant's full business record: GST number, PAN, phone, email, address, shops, active subscription + plan, and counts of parties/purchases/sales. Enumerating uuids dumps the customer base's tax identifiers.
- Evidence: business.routes.ts:24 `router.get('/:businessId', requireBusiness, ctrl.getBusiness);` — only requireBusiness, which performs no authorization (rbac.middleware.ts:12-24 just sets req.businessId and calls next()).
