The controller then reads the path id unconditionally: business.controller.ts:56 `const { businessId } = req.params;` / :57 `const business = await businessService.getBusinessById(businessId);`, and business.service.ts:98-117 `prisma.business.findUnique({ where: { id: businessId }, include: { shops: ..., subscriptions: { include: { plan: true } }, _count: { select: { business_users: true, parties: true, purchases: true, sales: true } } } })` — no business_users/user_id filter anywhe
- Fix: Add `requirePermission('BUSINESS_EDIT')` (or a read-level permission) to the route and have getBusiness use the verified businessId; getBusinessById should additionally filter on `business_users: { some: { user_id: userId, is_active: true } }`.

**Bank account numbers / IFSC / UPI of any business are readable — GET :id/bank-accounts has no permission check and falls back to the URL id**
- `packages/business-service/src/routes/business.routes.ts:39` — *authorization*
- Impact: Sending a request with no x-business-id header but a victim's uuid in the path returns that business's full bank account list — account_name, full account_number, ifsc_code, bank_name, upi_id. Same hole on GET :id/credit-cards and :id/credit-cards/:cardId (card_holder, last 4, limit, outstanding) once that table exists.
- Evidence: business.routes.ts:39 `router.get('/:businessId/bank-accounts', requireBusiness, ctrl.listBankAccounts);` and :50-51 `router.get('/:businessId/credit-cards', requireBusiness, ctrl.listCreditCards); router.get('/:businessId/credit-cards/:cardId', requireBusiness, ctrl.getCreditCard);` — no requirePermission on any of the three, and requireBusiness never verifies membership (rbac.middleware.ts:13 `req.headers['x-business-id'] as string || req.params.businessId`). The controller consumes that unverified value: business.controller.ts:159 `const businessId = (req as any).businessId || req.params.businessId;` -> :160 `businessService.listBankAccounts(businessId)` -> business.service.ts:410 `prisma
- Fix: Add a permission/membership middleware to all three GET routes (the write routes already have requirePermission) and drop the `|| req.params.businessId` fallback so the id can only come from a membership-verified source.

**Settings "Save" returns 400 for every business created without a phone number**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:108` — *validation*
- Impact: The create wizard does not require a phone (it only validates name and type), so any business created without one can NEVER be edited: every click of Save on Business Settings shows "Validation failed" and nothing is saved. Clearing the phone field on an existing business bricks Save the same way.
- Evidence: The page always sends the key, even when empty — BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:86 `phone: currentBusiness?.phone || '',` and :108 `const { data } = await businessApi.update(currentBusiness!.id, form);` (whole form object, no empty-field pruning). Server side, updateBusinessSchema is `createBusinessSchema.partial()` (validators/index.ts:49) whose phone is `phone: phoneSchema.optional()` (validators/index.ts:45) with `phoneSchema = z.string().regex(/^[6-9]\d{9}$/, 'Invalid Indian phone number')` (validators/index.ts:8) — `.optional()` accepts `undefined`, not `''`, so `''` fails the regex and validate.middleware.ts:19 returns `res.status(400).json({ success: false, message: 'Validation failed', err
- Fix: Either prune empty strings before PATCH in handleSave (same pattern as BusinessCreatePage.tsx:321-326), or make the shared schema tolerant: wrap phone/gst/pan in the existing `z.preprocess(v => typeof v === 'string' && v.trim() === '' ? undefined : v, ...)` helper already defined at validators/index.ts:65-80.

**GST number can never be saved from Business Settings — frontend sends gst_number, zod declares gstNumber**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:82` — *field-name-mismatch*
- Impact: A user edits or first enters their GSTIN on the Settings page, sees the "settings saved" toast, and the value is silently discarded — it is not persisted, so every invoice/report that prints the business GSTIN keeps showing the old (or empty) number. Settings offers no other way to set it.
- Evidence: Frontend state and payload use snake_case: BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:82 `gst_number: currentBusiness?.gst_number || '',`, edited at :218-219 `value={form.gst_number} onChange={(e) => updateField('gst_number', ...)}`, sent whole at :108 `businessApi.update(currentBusiness!.id, form)` -> api.ts:274 `update: (id, data) => api.patch(`/business/${id}`, data)`. The schema declares only the camelCase name: validators/index.ts:39 `gstNumber: z.string().regex(...).optional(),` (updateBusinessSchema = the same object `.partial()` at :49). Because validate replaces the body with the stripped parse result — validate.middleware.ts:10-11 `const data = schema.parse(req[source]); req[source] = data;` — `gst_
- Fix: Rename the form key to `gstNumber` in BusinessSettingsPage (or map it in handleSave), and make the shared schema strict about unknown keys (`.strict()`) so a stripped field 400s loudly instead of being silently dropped.

**Deactivate / Activate business is completely dead — is_active is stripped by zod on every path**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessListPage.tsx:84` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Both the list-page dropdown "Deactivate", the inactive-card "Activate" button, and the Settings page Danger Zone show a success toast but change nothing. On the list page the immediately-following refetch restores the business to Active, so the user watches their action undo itself; on Settings the business is only removed from local redux, so it reappears on reload. Users cannot deactivate a business at all.
- Evidence: Both callers send only that one key: BusinessListPage.tsx:83-85 `const newStatus = !deactivateBiz.is_active; await businessApi.update(deactivateBiz.id, { is_active: newStatus }); toast.success(newStatus ? ... : t('business:deactivated_toast'));` then :87 `fetchBusinesses();`, and BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:126-127 `await businessApi.update(currentBusiness!.id, { is_active: false }); toast.success(t('business:business_deactivated'));`. `is_active` is not a key of createBusinessSchema (validators/index.ts:36-47: name, type, gstNumber, panNumber, address, city, state, pincode, phone, email) and updateBusinessSchema is just `createBusinessSchema.partial()` (:49), so validate.middleware.ts:11 `req[
- Fix: Add `is_active: z.boolean().optional()` to updateBusinessSchema (keeping the primary-business guard, which then becomes reachable) — or expose a dedicated PATCH /business/:id/status route like admin-service already has.

**BusinessSettingsPage ignores the :id route param and edits whichever business is currently selected**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:37` — *wrong-record*
- Impact: From the business list, choosing "Settings" on business B while A is the active business opens a form pre-filled with A's data and every action on the page (Save, logo upload/delete, bank accounts, credit cards, Deactivate) targets A. The user believes they are configuring B and instead silently overwrites A's profile and adds A's bank accounts.
- Evidence: The route is parameterized — App.tsx:260 `<Route path="/business/:id/settings" element={<ProtectedRoute allowWhenExpired><BusinessSettingsPage /></ProtectedRoute>} />` — and the list page navigates per-card: BusinessListPage.tsx:204 `onClick={() => navigate(`/business/${biz.id}/settings`)}`. But the page never reads the param (no useParams import at all); it takes everything from redux: BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:37 `const { currentBusiness, businesses } = useAppSelector(s => s.business);`, :79-87 form seeded from `currentBusiness?.*`, :108 `businessApi.update(currentBusiness!.id, form)`, :50 `businessApi.uploadLogo(currentBusiness!.id, file)`, :68 `businessApi.removeLogo(currentBusiness!.id)`
- Fix: Read `const { id } = useParams()`, resolve `const biz = businesses.find(b => b.id === id)`, drive the form/API calls from that id, and pass the id down to BusinessBankSection/BusinessCreditCardSection as a prop (or redirect to the switcher if the id is not in the user's list).

**Statement "Match" reconciliation persists nothing — the reconcile endpoint is a no-op Prisma update**
- `packages/business-service/src/services/business.service.ts:568` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Both the bank and credit-card reconciliation flows show "Matched", a rising "N reconciled" badge and an "All entries reconciled!" banner while writing nothing to the database. After a reload every entry is unmatched again, and because there is no dedupe key, a user re-uploading the same statement and clicking "Create" duplicates every ledger entry — permanently inflating the books.
- Evidence: Server: business.service.ts:568-574 `if (data.ledgerEntryId) { return prisma.ledgerEntry.update({ where: { id: data.ledgerEntryId }, data: { narration: prisma.ledgerEntry.fields ? undefined : undefined } }); }` (every branch is `undefined`, so no column is written) and the CC twin at :771-777 `data: { narration: undefined }, // no-op update to acknowledge match`. There is no reconciled/reconciled_at column on LedgerEntry at all (packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma LedgerEntry: id, business_id, party_id, purchase_id, sale_id, payment_id, entry_date, account_type, entry_type, amount, balance_after, narration, reference_type, reference_id, created_at). Client treats it as durable: BusinessBank
- Fix: Add a reconciliation marker (e.g. `reconciled_at DateTime?` + `statement_ref String?` on LedgerEntry, or a BankStatementEntry table keyed by business_id + bank_account_id + date + amount + narration hash) and write it in reconcileEntry/reconcileCreditCardEntry; then have the parse endpoint return the already-reconciled state so re-uploads cannot double-post.

**reconcileCreditCardEntry increments the balance of any card id without checking it belongs to the business**
- `packages/business-service/src/services/business.service.ts:797` — *authorization*
- Impact: POST /business/<my business>/credit-cards/<someone else's cardId>/reconcile writes the ledger entry into the caller's own business (so requirePermission passes) while mutating the victim's card outstanding balance — corrupting another tenant's credit-card utilization and due amounts with no trace.
- Evidence: Unlike its siblings, this method never scopes the card. Compare getCreditCard/updateCreditCard/deleteCreditCard which all guard first — business.service.ts:660 `const existing = await prisma.businessCreditCard.findFirst({ where: { id: cardId, business_id: businessId } }); if (!existing) throw new Error('Credit card not found');` — with reconcileCreditCardEntry, which does no such lookup and goes straight to business.service.ts:796-800 `const balanceDelta = data.type === 'debit' ? data.amount : -data.amount; await prisma.businessCreditCard.update({ where: { id: cardId }, data: { current_balance: { increment: balanceDelta } } });`. cardId is taken raw from the URL at business.controller.ts:263
- Fix: Start reconcileCreditCardEntry with the same `findFirst({ where: { id: cardId, business_id: businessId } })` guard (throwing NotFoundError) before creating the ledger entry, and wrap the ledger insert + balance increment in a single prisma.$transaction.

**Preferences card on Business Settings is entirely dead UI — nothing is ever sent, and the two supported fields are unreachable through zod**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:89` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Invoice prefix, purchase prefix, sale prefix, currency symbol, financial-year start, WhatsApp/SMS notification toggles and auto-backup all reset to their hard-coded defaults ('INV'/'PUR'/'SAL'/₹/April/on) on every page load, yet Save reports success. A user who sets invoice_prefix expecting their invoices to be numbered accordingly gets no change at all.
- Evidence: The 8 preference values live in a second state object that no request ever reads: BusinessSettingsPage.tsx:89-98 `const [settings, setSettings] = useState({ invoicePrefix: 'INV', purchasePrefix: 'PUR', salePrefix: 'SAL', currencySymbol: '₹', financialYearStart: 'April', autoBackup: true, enableWhatsApp: false, enableSMS: false });` — and handleSave only ships the other object, :108 `const { data } = await businessApi.update(currentBusiness!.id, form);` followed by the unconditional :116 `toast.success(t('business:settings_saved'));`. Even if they were sent, the two the backend supports would be stripped: business.service.ts:171-172 `invoice_prefix: data.invoicePrefix, purchase_prefix: data.p
- Fix: Add invoicePrefix/purchasePrefix (and financialYearStart as a 1-12 number) to updateBusinessSchema, merge those keys into the PATCH body in handleSave, and either add columns for salePrefix/currencySymbol/notification toggles or remove those controls so the card stops promising persistence.

### Dashboard & Reports

**Trial Balance and P&L silently exclude the whole end date (all of today) — totals disagree with Day Book for the same range**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:175` — *data-bug*
- Impact: ReportsPage defaults the range to `to = today`. On the Trial Balance and Profit & Loss tabs every entry created today (and generally every entry on the chosen end date) is missing, so debit/credit totals, Total Income, Total Expenses and Net Profit are all understated. The user sees a purchase in the Day Book tab for the exact same date range but it is absent from Trial Balance / P&L — the two tabs never reconcile. Balance Sheet has the same defect via asOnDate.
- Evidence: Frontend sends a bare date string: frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:120 — `const res = await ledgerApi.trialBalance({ startDate: dateRange.from, endDate: dateRange.to });` frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:91-94 — `to: new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0]` (i.e. "2026-07-27"). Backend parses it as UTC midnight and uses it as an inclusive upper bound with no end-of-day normalisation: packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:174-175 — `const startDate = query.startDate ? new Date(query.startDate) : start;` `const endDate = query.endDate ? new Date(query.endDate) : end;` ledger.service.ts:186-187 — `AND entry_date >= ${startDate}\n AND entry_date <= 
- Fix: Normalise the bounds in getTrialBalance, getProfitAndLoss and getBalanceSheet the way getDayBook does, e.g. `const endDate = query.endDate ? (() => { const d = new Date(query.endDate); d.setHours(23,59,59,999); return d; })() : end;` and likewise clamp startDate to 00:00:00.000. Best fix is one shared `parseDayRange(from, to)` helper in shared/utils/helpers.ts used by all four report methods.

**Day Book 'Type' column always shows JOURNAL — service reads e.ref_type, the Prisma field is reference_type**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:451` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On Reports -> Day Book, every row's Type badge renders the grey fallback 'JOURNAL'. The colour coding for PURCHASE / SALE / PAYMENT / EXPENSE is unreachable, and the exported CSV/JSON has `type: "JOURNAL"` on every row, so the user cannot tell a purchase entry from a payment entry.
- Evidence: packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:445-453 (the mapper the controller returns verbatim): `return entries.map((e: any) => ({ id: e.id, date: e.entry_date, narration: e.narration || e.description || '', debit: e.entry_type === 'DEBIT' ? Number(e.amount) : 0, credit: e.entry_type === 'CREDIT' ? Number(e.amount) : 0, type: e.ref_type || 'JOURNAL', party_name: e.party?.name, }));` There is no `ref_type` on the model — packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:931-945 (model LedgerEntry): ` narration String? reference_type String? reference_id String?` The same service uses the correct name elsewhere, e.g. ledger.service.ts:29 — `where.reference_type = { in: ['purchase', 'sale', 'pa
- Fix: Change ledger.service.ts:451 to `type: (e.reference_type ? String(e.reference_type).toUpperCase() : 'JOURNAL'),` so it emits PURCHASE / SALE / PAYMENT / MANUAL matching the colour map keys (reference_type is stored lower-case, see ledger.service.ts:110 `reference_type: 'manual'`). Also drop the dead `e.description` fallback on line 448 — that field does not exist either.

**Dashboard "Recent Transactions" card is permanently empty — backend never returns recentTransactions**
- `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:107` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: The Recent Transactions card on /dashboard always renders as a bare header + "View all" button with zero rows and no empty-state message, for every business no matter how many purchases/sales exist. The whole feature is non-functional.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:107 — `if (d.recentTransactions) setRecentTransactions(d.recentTransactions);` and the render, DashboardPage.tsx:519 — `{recentTransactions.map((tx: any, i: number) => (` … reading `tx.party`, `tx.date`, `tx.type`, `tx.amount`, `tx.status`. The only producer of that payload returns no such key — packages/business-service/src/services/business.service.ts:390-404: `return { purchaseToday: { count: purchasesToday._count, total: Number(purchasesToday._sum.total_amount || 0) }, salesToday: { count: salesToday._count, total: Number(salesToday._sum.total_amount || 0) }, outstandingPayable: Number(outstandingPayable._sum.balance_amount || 0), outst
- Fix: In business.service.ts getDashboard, add a query for the latest N transactions and include it in the return, e.g. fetch `prisma.purchase.findMany({ where: { business_id: businessId, status: 'ACTIVE' }, include: { party: { select: { name: true } } }, orderBy: { purchase_date: 'desc' }, take: 5 })` plus the sale equivalent, merge, sort by date desc, slice(0,5) and map to `{ id, party: p.party.name, 

**All four dashboard quick-action report buttons land on the wrong page — /ledger/* renders LedgerPage on the default 'entries' tab**
- `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:435` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Clicking Day Book, Trial Balance, P&L Report or Outstanding in the dashboard's Quick Actions panel navigates to /ledger/day-book etc., which the router catches with the wildcard /ledger/* and renders LedgerPage with tab='entries'. The user always lands on the generic ledger entries list — none of the four buttons reaches the report it names, even though LedgerPage has exactly those four tabs.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:435-446: `<Button ... onClick={() => navigate('/ledger/day-book')}>📖 {t('dashboard:day_book')}</Button>` `<Button ... onClick={() => navigate('/ledger/trial-balance')}>📊 {t('dashboard:trial_balance')}</Button>` `<Button ... onClick={() => navigate('/ledger/profit-loss')}>💰 {t('dashboard:pl_report')}</Button>` `<Button ... onClick={() => navigate('/ledger/outstanding')}>📋 {t('dashboard:outstanding')}</Button>` frontend/src/App.tsx:234 — `<Route path="/ledger/*" element={<ProtectedRoute><LedgerPage /></ProtectedRoute>} />` LedgerPage hard-codes the initial tab and never reads the URL — frontend/src/components/ledger/LedgerPage.tsx:34 — `c
- Fix: Seed the tab from the URL in LedgerPage: `const { pathname } = useLocation(); const sub = pathname.replace(/^\/ledger\/?/, ''); const [tab, setTab] = useState(['day-book','trial-balance','profit-loss','outstanding'].includes(sub) ? sub : 'entries');` (and keep it in sync on navigation). Alternatively point the dashboard buttons at /reports, whose tab ids are 'daybook' | 'trial-balance' | 'profit-l

### Expenses & Bills

**expense-service is never started: no dev script, absent from docker-compose, and default PORT 3009 collides with notification-service**
- `packages/expense-service/src/index.ts:8` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: There is no supported way to run the service that backs the whole Expenses page. `npm run dev:all` starts 13 services and expense-service is not one of them, and `docker compose up` never builds it. If a developer starts it manually it binds 3009 and either fails with EADDRINUSE or steals the port from notification-service, breaking the notifications feature instead.
- Evidence: packages/expense-service/src/index.ts:8 `const PORT = process.env.PORT || 3009;` packages/notification-service/src/index.ts:11 `const PORT = process.env.NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_PORT || 3009;` Root package.json scripts list dev:auth/business/purchase/sales/inventory/ledger/subscription/billing/notification/admin/profile/referral/gateway/whatsapp-ai/frontend/website — no `dev:expense`; `"dev:all": "concurrently ... npm:dev:referral\" \"npm:dev:frontend\""` omits it. infra/docker/docker-compose.yml service list: postgres, redis, api-gateway, auth-service, business-service, purchase-service, sales-service, inventory-service, ledger-service, subscription-service, billing-service, notification-servic
- Fix: Give the service its own port constant (e.g. EXPENSE_SERVICE_PORT 3014), read `process.env.EXPENSE_SERVICE_PORT`, add `"dev:expense": "cd packages/expense-service && npm run dev"` to root package.json plus an entry in dev:all, and add a docker-compose service block.

**POST /expenses/upload-receipt does not exist — attaching a receipt makes the whole expense save fail**
- `packages/expense-service/src/routes/expense.routes.ts:6` — *missing-endpoint*
- Impact: In the New Expense dialog, if the user picks a receipt image/PDF, the upload call rejects and the exception is caught before expenseApi.create is ever reached — so the expense is not saved at all and the user only sees the generic "create_error" toast. Choosing a receipt silently converts "save my expense" into "lose my expense".
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/expenses/CreateExpenseDialog.tsx:45-48 `if (form.receiptFile) { const { data } = await expenseApi.uploadReceipt(form.receiptFile); receiptUrl = data?.data?.url; }` then :50 `await expenseApi.create({...})` — both inside one try whose catch (:73-75) only toasts. frontend/src/lib/api.ts:517-523 `uploadReceipt: (file) => { ... return api.post('/expenses/upload-receipt', formData, ...) }`. packages/expense-service/src/routes/expense.routes.ts:6-11 is the complete route list: `router.post('/', ...createExpense); router.get('/', ...listAllExpenses); router.get('/stats', ...getExpenseStats); router.get('/:id', ...); router.put('/:id', ...); router.delete('/:id', ...);` — no 
- Fix: Either implement `POST /upload-receipt` with multer in expense.routes.ts (mirroring packages/billing-service/src/routes/billing.routes.ts:25-35), or point expenseApi.uploadReceipt at the existing purchase-service endpoint `/purchases/expenses/receipt-upload` (already wrapped as purchaseApi.uploadExpenseReceipt, frontend/src/lib/api.ts:334-337).

**Expense routes have no authenticateToken/requireBusiness/requirePermission — tenant isolation is a client-supplied header**
- `packages/expense-service/src/routes/expense.routes.ts:4` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: Any unauthenticated caller who can reach the service can list, read, create, edit and delete another business's expenses simply by sending `x-business-id: <victim-business-uuid>` with no Authorization header at all. Delete is destructive and permanent (prisma.expense.delete).
- Evidence: packages/expense-service/src/routes/expense.routes.ts:1-11 — `const router = Router();` followed directly by the six route registrations; there is no `router.use(authenticateToken)` / `router.use(requireBusiness)` and no requirePermission on any mutating route, even though the vendored middleware exists at packages/expense-service/src/shared/middleware/{auth,rbac}.middleware.ts. packages/expense-service/src/controllers/expense.controller.ts:10-11 `const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string; if (!businessId) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Missing x-business-id header' });` — the header is the only authorization check, and it is never cross-checked against a JWT. Contrast
- Fix: Add `router.use(authenticateToken); router.use(requireBusiness);` at the top of expense.routes.ts and `requirePermission(...)` on POST/PUT/DELETE, then derive businessId from the authenticated request rather than trusting the raw header.

**No /expenses/:id route exists — the Edit button and every row click land on the 404 page, and expenseApi.update is unreachable**
- `frontend/src/App.tsx:226` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Clicking the pencil icon on a standalone expense, or clicking anywhere on a standalone expense row, navigates to /expenses/<uuid> which matches only the catch-all and renders NotFoundPage — the user loses the page they were on. There is consequently no way to edit an expense anywhere in the app: expenseApi.update / expenseApi.get are defined but called from nowhere.
- Evidence: frontend/src/App.tsx:225-226 `{/* Expenses */}` / `<Route path="/expenses" element={<ProtectedRoute><ExpensesPage /></ProtectedRoute>} />` — the only expense route; :282 `<Route path="*" element={<NotFoundPage />} />`. Compare the sales block which does define detail routes (App.tsx:216-217 `/sales/:id/edit`, `/sales/:id`). frontend/src/components/expenses/ExpensesPage.tsx:219 `<Button ... onClick={() => navigate(`/expenses/${e.id}`)}>` and :336-337 `onRowClick={(e) => { if (e.source === 'standalone') navigate(`/expenses/${e.id}`); ...}`. Backend side is ready and unused: packages/expense-service/src/routes/expense.routes.ts:9-10 `router.get('/:id', ...); router.put('/:id', ...)`.
- Fix: Add an ExpenseDetail/Edit page and register `/expenses/:id` (and optionally `/expenses/:id/edit`) in App.tsx, or replace the navigate() calls with an edit dialog that calls expenseApi.update.

**Import on ExpensesPage is a fake progress bar — nothing is ever sent to any API, and it validates against the wrong template**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: The user picks an expenses CSV, is told "Missing columns: name, phone, email, type, opening_balance" (because the module silently defaults to 'parties' and 'expenses' is not even an option), and if they instead upload a parties-shaped file they get a progress animation and a green "Import successful — N records imported" toast while zero rows are written to the database. The list then refetches and shows nothing new. Guaranteed silent data loss for anyone who trusts the confirmation.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146-162 `const handleImport = async () => { if (errors.length > 0) ...; setStep('importing'); ... // Simulate import progress\n const total = parsedData.length; for (let i = 0; i <= total; i++) { await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 30)); setProgress(...); } ... setStep('done'); toast.success(t('common:import_success_toast', { count: parsedData.length, module })); }` — no api call anywhere in the file (no import of @/lib/api at all). :13 `type ImportModule = 'parties' | 'inventory' | 'purchases' | 'sales' | 'payments' | 'ledger';` and :53 `defaultModule = 'parties'` — 'expenses' does not exist. frontend/src/components/expenses/ExpensesPage.
- Fix: Wire handleImport to a real bulk-import endpoint per module and add an 'expenses' module template; until the endpoint exists, disable/remove the Import button on ExpensesPage instead of showing a success toast.

**Recording a payment mutates purchases/sales/parties without any business_id check (cross-tenant write)**
- `packages/billing-service/src/services/billing.service.ts:151` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: The Pay button on BillsPage posts {referenceType, referenceId} straight through. The service looks the reference up by primary key only, so a request carrying another tenant's purchase/sale id (or party id) overwrites that tenant's paid_amount, balance_amount and payment_status and increments/decrements their party balance, while the attacker's own business gets the ledger entries. Cross-tenant data corruption on a money field, reachable from a normal user session with PAYMENT_CREATE.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/bills/BillsPage.tsx:197-206 `await billingApi.createPayment({ referenceType: selectedBill.type, referenceId: selectedBill.id, payerPartyId: ..., payeePartyId: ..., paymentMode: payMode, amount, ... });` packages/billing-service/src/services/billing.service.ts:151 `const purchase = await tx.purchase.findUnique({ where: { id: data.referenceId } });` then :159-166 `await tx.purchase.update({ where: { id: data.referenceId }, data: { paid_amount: ..., balance_amount: ..., payment_status: paymentStatus } });` :199 `const sale = await tx.sale.findUnique({ where: { id: data.referenceId } });` then :207-214 the matching `tx.sale.update`. :247-258 `if (data.payerPartyId) { awai
- Fix: Use `findFirst({ where: { id: data.referenceId, business_id: businessId } })` for purchase and sale, throw NotFound when absent, and scope the two party.update calls with `updateMany({ where: { id, business_id: businessId } })`.

### Inventory

**New item form drops category, min stock and opening stock (snake_case body stripped by zod)**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryCreatePage.tsx:56` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On /inventory/new the user selects a category, sets "Min stock = 20" and "Opening stock = 100", saves, and gets "item created". The created item has no category (list shows blank category, detail page shows "uncategorized"), min_stock = 0 (so low-stock alerting can never fire for it) and current_stock = 0 (opening stock vanishes). The Category dropdown and both number inputs on this form are effectively decorative.
- Evidence: InventoryCreatePage.tsx:18-25 sends snake_case: `const [form, setForm] = useState({ name: '', sku: '', unit: 'Kg', category_id: '', min_stock: 0, current_stock: 0 });` then line 56 `await inventoryApi.createItem(form);`. The route validates with a non-strict zod object (shared/validators/index.ts:137-145): `export const createItemSchema = z.object({ name: ..., sku: ..., categoryId: z.string().uuid().optional(), unit: z.string().default('KG'), minStock: z.number().min(0).default(0), hsnCode: ..., gstRate: ... });` and validate.middleware.ts:9-11 REPLACES the body with the parsed result: `const data = schema.parse(req[source]); req[source] = data;` — zod strips unknown keys, so category_id / m
- Fix: Send `{ name, sku, unit, categoryId: form.category_id || undefined, minStock: form.min_stock }` from the page. For opening stock, either extend createItemSchema/createItem with an `openingStock` that sets current_stock + quantity_in and writes an ADJUSTMENT transaction, or remove the Opening Stock input and direct users to the adjust page.

**Edit item dialog never saves min stock (min_stock vs minStock)**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/EditInventoryItemDialog.tsx:32` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: From /inventory row Pencil or /inventory/:id Edit, changing "Min stock alert" and clicking Save Changes shows "item updated" and the dialog closes, but the value reverts on refetch. name/sku/unit do save, so the failure looks arbitrary. Combined with the create-page bug, min_stock is unsettable anywhere in the app.
- Evidence: EditInventoryItemDialog.tsx:19-24: `const [form, setForm] = useState({ name: item.name, sku: item.sku || '', unit: item.unit, min_stock: item.min_stock });` then line 32 `await inventoryApi.updateItem(item.id, form);` → PATCH /inventory/items/:itemId (inventory.routes.ts:18, no zod validator here) → inventory.service.ts:53 reads a different key: `...(data.minStock !== undefined && { min_stock: new Prisma.Decimal(data.minStock) }),`. `min_stock` is simply never looked at, so the update object omits it.
- Fix: Send `minStock: Number(form.min_stock)` from the dialog (and keep name/sku/unit as-is), or accept both spellings in InventoryService.updateItem.

**Item detail transaction history is not filtered to the item (item_id vs itemId) — shows the whole business's transactions**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryDetailPage.tsx:79` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: Opening any item at /inventory/:id shows, under the Transactions tab and in the header count, the most recent 20 inventory transactions of the ENTIRE business — other items' purchases, sales and adjustments — as if they belonged to this item. Every item's detail page shows the identical list, and the derived "Total purchased / Total sold / Stock value" stats are computed from foreign rows.
- Evidence: InventoryDetailPage.tsx:77-82: `const [itemRes, txRes] = await Promise.all([ inventoryApi.getItem(id!), inventoryApi.transactions({ item_id: id }), ]);` → api.ts:380 `transactions: (params?: any) => api.get('/inventory/transactions', { params })`. The service only honours camelCase (inventory.service.ts:251-253): `const where: any = { business_id: businessId }; if (query.itemId) where.item_id = query.itemId; if (query.txnType) where.txn_type = query.txnType;` — `item_id` in the querystring matches nothing, so the filter is dropped and all business rows are returned (findMany take=DEFAULT_LIMIT 20, helpers.ts:25-32).
- Fix: Call `inventoryApi.transactions({ itemId: id, limit: 100 })`. Alternatively have the controller accept `req.query.item_id ?? req.query.itemId`.

**Item detail transaction table and all four stat cards read field names the API does not return**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryDetailPage.tsx:141` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: On /inventory/:id the Transactions tab renders rows with an empty/Invalid Date date column, no coloured type badge (undefined CSS class), and "—" in Rate, Amount, Reference and Party for every row. The stat cards "Total purchased" and "Total sold" are always 0, "Stock value" and the item-details "Avg purchase rate" / "Total stock value" are always ₹0, because the reducers filter on `t.type === 'PURCHASE'` which is never true. Export of transactions produces a CSV of empty columns.
- Evidence: Page expects (InventoryDetailPage.tsx:24-33) `interface StockTransaction { id; date; type; quantity; rate; amount; reference; party_name? }` and uses it at 114-119 (`transactions.filter(t => t.type === 'PURCHASE').reduce((s, t) => s + t.quantity, 0)`, `... reduce((s, t) => s + t.amount, 0) / totalPurchaseQty`, `const stockValue = item.current_stock * avgPurchaseRate;`) and 142-156 (`render: (t2) => formatDate(t2.date)`, `typeColors[t2.type]`, `t2.rate ? formatCurrency(t2.rate) : '—'`, `t2.amount ? ... : '—'`, `{t2.reference}`, `t2.party_name || '—'`). The API returns InventoryTransaction rows (inventory.service.ts:256-259 `prisma.inventoryTransaction.findMany({ where, include: { item: { sele
- Fix: Map the real shape in the page: date ← `created_at`, type ← `txn_type` (values IN/OUT/ADJUSTMENT/REVERSAL per the InventoryTxnType enum, so typeColors keys must change too), reference ← `reference_type`/`reference_id`, and either drop rate/amount/party or extend getTransactions to join the purchase/sale rows that supply rate, amount and party name. Derive stock value from the lots' purchase_rate (

**Lots tab on the item detail page is always empty — API returns lots but the page never stores them**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryDetailPage.tsx:81` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: On /inventory/:id the default "Lots" tab always shows "Stock lots (0)" and the no-lots empty state, even for items with many AVAILABLE/PARTIAL lots from purchases. Lots only ever appear when the API call FAILS (the catch branch loads hardcoded demo lots), so the page is more informative when the backend is down.
- Evidence: The service does return them (inventory.service.ts:63-70): `const item = await prisma.inventoryItem.findFirst({ where: { id: itemId, business_id: businessId }, include: { category: true, lots: { where: { status: { in: ['AVAILABLE', 'PARTIAL'] } }, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' } }, inventory_txns: { orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' }, take: 20 } } });` but the page discards them (InventoryDetailPage.tsx:81-86): `if (itemRes.data?.data) setItem(itemRes.data.data);\n if (txRes.data?.data) setTransactions(txRes.data.data);\n } catch {\n setItem(defaultItem);\n setLots(defaultLots);` — `setLots` is called only in the catch, so on success `lots` stays `[]` (declared line 64: `const [lots, setLots] =
- Fix: In the success branch add `setLots(itemRes.data.data.lots || [])` (and consider using the embedded `inventory_txns` instead of the separate transactions call).

**Low-stock alerting is dead end to end: min_stock is unsettable, the low-stock endpoint is never called, and the tab filter only catches zero-stock items**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryPage.tsx:62` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: The "Low stock" tab and the "Low stock items" stat card never surface genuinely low items. Because min_stock is always 0 (create and edit both drop the field), `current_stock <= min_stock` is true only for items at exactly 0 stock, so the tab lists out-of-stock items and nothing else. The purpose-built backend endpoint that would do this correctly is never invoked by any frontend code, and it is additionally hard-gated on `min_stock > 0`, so it would return an empty array anyway.
- Evidence: Client-side filter (InventoryPage.tsx:62-63): `const lowStockItems = items.filter(i => i.current_stock <= i.min_stock);\n const displayItems = tab === 'low-stock' ? lowStockItems : items;`. `inventoryApi.lowStock` exists (api.ts:376 `lowStock: () => api.get('/inventory/items/low-stock')`) and the route is correctly registered before the param route (inventory.routes.ts:16-17: `router.get('/items/low-stock', ...); router.get('/items/:itemId', ...)`) but `grep -rn "lowStock()" frontend/src` returns nothing — no caller. The query itself requires a non-zero threshold (inventory.service.ts:141-148): `SELECT * FROM inventory_items WHERE business_id = ${businessId} AND is_active = true AND current_
- Fix: Fix the minStock payload keys first, then drive the Low-stock tab (and the stat card) from `inventoryApi.lowStock()` rather than filtering the current page client-side.

**Inventory list is capped at 20 items with no pagination control, so stats, filters and the adjust dropdown silently truncate**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryPage.tsx:35` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: A business with more than 20 items only ever sees the 20 most recently created ones on /inventory — there is no next-page control anywhere on the page. "Total items" reports 20, the category count and low-stock count are computed from those 20, Export CSV exports only those 20, and the item dropdown on /inventory/adjust (which also omits limit) can only offer 20 items, making older items unadjustable through the UI.
- Evidence: InventoryPage.tsx:35 `const { data } = await inventoryApi.listItems({ search, ...filters });` and StockAdjustPage.tsx:32 `const { data } = await inventoryApi.listItems();` — neither sends page/limit; the backend defaults to 20 (shared/utils/helpers.ts:25-32 `limit: Math.min(MAX_LIMIT, Math.max(1, Number(query.limit) || DEFAULT_LIMIT))` with `DEFAULT_LIMIT = 20` in shared/constants/index.ts:28) and applies `skip: offset, take: limit` (inventory.service.ts:105-111). The service does return `meta` (inventory.service.ts:137 `return { data: enriched, meta: buildPaginationMeta(total, page, limit) };`, spread by the controller at inventory.controller.ts:41 `res.json({ success: true, ...result })`) 
- Fix: Add page state on InventoryPage, pass `{ page, limit }`, render controls from the returned `meta` (total/totalPages/hasNext), and pass `{ limit: 500 }` on StockAdjustPage's dropdown fetch. Stats that need business-wide numbers should come from `inventoryApi.dashboard()` instead of the current page.

### Ledger

**Party ledger shows both legs of every double entry, so the net balance collapses to ~0**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:34` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Click any party row in Ledger or Outstanding -> Party Ledger page. A 100,000 unpaid sale shows two rows (Debit 100,000 + Credit 100,000) and the headline stat reads "You owe 0" / "They owe 0" instead of 100,000 receivable. Every party's outstanding figure on the drill-down page is wrong, and the tfoot totals are wrong.
- Evidence: The party-ledger allow-list keeps BOTH sides of the journal — services/ledger.service.ts:34-38 `if (!query.accountType && !query.includeAll) { where.account_type = { in: ['PARTY_PAYABLE','PARTY_RECEIVABLE','CASH','BANK','INVENTORY','SALES','PURCHASE'] }; }` combined with :29 `where.reference_type = { in: ['purchase','sale','payment','manual'] };`. A sale writes both legs against the same party_id: packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:291-305 `account_type: 'PARTY_RECEIVABLE', entry_type: 'DEBIT', amount: partyAmount, ... reference_type: 'sale'` and :307-321 `party_id: data.partyId, ... account_type: 'SALES', entry_type: 'CREDIT', amount: partyAmount, ... reference_type: 'sale
- Fix: Restrict the party ledger to the party-facing control accounts only: `where.account_type = { in: ['PARTY_PAYABLE','PARTY_RECEIVABLE'] }` (the CASH/BANK/SALES/INVENTORY legs are business-side, not party-side). Alternatively drop the client-side netting and surface `party.balance` (already returned) as the outstanding figure.

**Party-ledger running balance is accumulated over newest-first rows, producing a nonsense Balance column**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/PartyLedgerPage.tsx:71` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On the Party Ledger statement the "Balance" column is meaningless: the top (newest) row shows only its own amount and the bottom (oldest) row shows the cumulative total — the exact inverse of a real running balance. Printing/handing this to a party gives wrong per-line balances.
- Evidence: The server returns descending rows — services/ledger.service.ts:41-51 `prisma.ledgerEntry.findMany({ where, include: {...}, orderBy: { entry_date: 'desc' }, skip: offset, take: limit })` — and the page accumulates in that order: PartyLedgerPage.tsx:71-75 `let runningBalance = 0; const entriesWithBalance = entries.map(e => { runningBalance += e.entry_type === 'DEBIT' ? e.amount : -e.amount; return { ...e, running_balance: runningBalance }; });`. The service already has a correct implementation on an endpoint the app never calls — services/ledger.service.ts:150-162 `orderBy: { entry_date: 'asc' } ... let runningBalance = 0; const statement = entries.map(entry => { const amount = Number(entry.a
- Fix: Switch PartyLedgerPage.fetchData to `ledgerApi.partyStatement(partyId)` and render the server-computed `runningBalance` / `closingBalance`, or sort ascending before the reduce and reverse for display.

**Party ledger silently truncates to 20 entries with no pagination control**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/PartyLedgerPage.tsx:34` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Any party with more than 20 ledger rows shows only the 20 most recent, and the Total Debit / Total Credit / You-owe stat cards and the table footer are computed from those 20 rows only. There is no next-page control anywhere on the page, so the rest of the history is unreachable and the totals are simply wrong.
- Evidence: The page requests no pagination params — PartyLedgerPage.tsx:34 `const { data } = await ledgerApi.partyLedger(partyId!);` (api.ts:389 `partyLedger: (partyId, params?) => api.get(`/ledger/party/${partyId}`, { params })`). The service always paginates — services/ledger.service.ts:14-15 `const { page, limit } = parsePagination(query); const offset = getPaginationOffset(page, limit);` and :49-50 `skip: offset, take: limit`, with shared/utils/helpers.ts:28 `limit: Math.min(MAX_LIMIT, Math.max(1, Number(query.limit) || DEFAULT_LIMIT))` and shared/constants/index.ts:28 `export const DEFAULT_LIMIT = 20;`. The returned `meta` (buildPaginationMeta, ledger.service.ts:63) is discarded by the page (:35-4
- Fix: Pass `{ limit: 500 }` (MAX_LIMIT) or render a pagination control driven by the returned `meta`, and compute the debit/credit/net totals from a server-side aggregate rather than from the current page.

**Ledger search box and every advanced filter are ignored by the backend (query-name mismatch)**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/LedgerPage.tsx:75` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On the Ledger page, typing in the search box, picking a date preset (Today / Last 7 days / custom range), or setting Account type / Entry type / Amount min-max in Advanced Filters changes nothing — the same unfiltered first 20 entries come back and the badge counter claims N filters are active.
- Evidence: The page sends the filter object verbatim — LedgerPage.tsx:75 `const { data } = await ledgerApi.entries({ search, ...filters });`, where the filter keys come from LedgerPage.tsx:208-227 `{ key: 'account_type', ... type: 'select' }, { key: 'entry_type', ... }, { key: 'amount', ... type: 'number-range' }` and AdvancedFilters emits `account_type`, `entry_type`, `amount_min`/`amount_max`, `date_from`/`date_to` — AdvancedFilters.tsx:64-65 `const fromKey = `${dateKey}_from`; const toKey = `${dateKey}_to`;` (dateKey defaults to 'date', :59), :239 `onValueChange={(val) => updateValue(field.key, val)}`, :283-291 `updateValue(`${field.key}_min`, ...)` / `_max`. The service only understands different n
- Fix: Map in fetchEntries: `{ q: search, accountType: filters.account_type === '__all__' ? undefined : filters.account_type, entryType: ..., startDate: filters.date_from, endDate: filters.date_to, minAmount: filters.amount_min, maxAmount: filters.amount_max }` and add the corresponding handling (narration/party name `contains` for search, amount range, party_id) to getBusinessLedger.

**Bulk "Delete selected" deletes nothing but reports success**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/LedgerPage.tsx:197` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Ledger -> select rows -> "Delete selected" -> confirm in the danger dialog. A green "entries deleted" toast appears, the table refetches, and the entries are still there. The user believes accounting records were removed when nothing happened.
- Evidence: The confirm handler never calls an API — LedgerPage.tsx:197-206 `const handleBulkDelete = async () => { if (!bulkDeleteItems) return; try { toast.success(t('ledger:entries_deleted', { count: bulkDeleteItems.length })); setBulkDeleteItems(null); fetchEntries(); } catch { toast.error(t('ledger:entries_delete_error')); } };` wired at :540-548 `<ConfirmDialog ... onConfirm={handleBulkDelete} confirmLabel={t('ledger:delete_all')} variant="danger" />`. There is no delete endpoint to call: packages/ledger-service/src/routes/ledger.routes.ts:11-21 registers only `router.get('/entries')`, `router.post('/entries')`, `router.get('/party/:partyId')`, `/party/:partyId/statement`, `/trial-balance`, `/prof
- Fix: Either add `DELETE /entries/:id` (or `POST /entries/bulk-delete`) behind requirePermission('LEDGER_EDIT') with business scoping plus a reversing party-balance update, and call it from handleBulkDelete; or remove the destructive bulk action until the backend exists.

**Ledger Import is a simulation — no request is ever sent**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Ledger -> Import -> upload a CSV -> Import. A progress bar runs to 100%, the dialog shows a success screen and toasts "N records imported", LedgerPage refetches — and zero ledger entries exist. Users will believe their opening balances/history were imported.
- Evidence: handleImport only sleeps — ImportDataDialog.tsx:146-162 `const handleImport = async () => { if (errors.length > 0) return toast.error(...); setStep('importing'); setImporting(true); setProgress(0); // Simulate import progress\n const total = parsedData.length; for (let i = 0; i <= total; i++) { await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 30)); setProgress(Math.round((i / total) * 100)); } setImporting(false); setStep('done'); toast.success(t('common:import_success_toast', { count: parsedData.length, module })); };` — there is no api call in the file. It is wired into the ledger page at LedgerPage.tsx:271-273 `<Button variant="outline" onClick={() => setShowImport(true)}> <Upload .../> {t('common:im
- Fix: Add a bulk-import endpoint (e.g. POST /api/v1/ledger/entries/bulk) and have handleImport POST parsedData in chunks, driving the progress bar off real responses and surfacing per-row errors. Until then, disable the Import button rather than faking success.

**CreateLedgerEntryDialog offers five account types that are not in the AccountType enum**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/CreateLedgerEntryDialog.tsx:68` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: Even after the snake_case/camelCase mismatch is fixed, choosing Sale, Payment In, Payment Out, Adjustment or Other in the "Account type" dropdown (5 of the 7 options, and Sale/Payment In/Payment Out are also offered as Ledger filters) makes the create fail with a Prisma enum error. Only Purchase and Expense are storable.
- Evidence: Dialog options — CreateLedgerEntryDialog.tsx:68-76 `const accountTypes = [{ value: 'PURCHASE' }, { value: 'SALE' }, { value: 'EXPENSE' }, { value: 'PAYMENT_IN' }, { value: 'PAYMENT_OUT' }, { value: 'ADJUSTMENT' }, { value: 'OTHER' }];` (mirrored in the Ledger filter list, LedgerPage.tsx:210-217 `{ value: 'SALE' }, { value: 'PAYMENT_IN' }, { value: 'PAYMENT_OUT' }`). The schema enum — packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:962-972 `enum AccountType { INVENTORY PARTY_PAYABLE PARTY_RECEIVABLE CASH BANK SALES PURCHASE EXPENSE INCOME }`. Note SALE vs SALES, and PAYMENT_IN/PAYMENT_OUT/ADJUSTMENT/OTHER do not exist at all. The value is written straight through: services/ledger.service.ts:106 `account
- Fix: Drive the dropdown (and the Ledger account_type filter) from the real AccountType enum — SALES/PURCHASE/EXPENSE/INCOME/CASH/BANK/PARTY_PAYABLE/PARTY_RECEIVABLE/INVENTORY — and add a zod enum validator on POST /entries so an invalid value returns 400 instead of a 500.

**"No party" option posts the literal string "__none__" as party_id**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/CreateLedgerEntryDialog.tsx:157` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: New Entry -> Party dropdown -> "No party" -> Create. The sentinel value is submitted as a real party id, so once the payload naming is fixed the insert fails on a foreign-key violation (or, worse, prisma.party.update throws a 500 for a non-existent id) instead of creating an unlinked entry.
- Evidence: The sentinel is a normal select value and the form is posted unmapped — CreateLedgerEntryDialog.tsx:157 `<SelectItem value="__none__">{t('common:no_party')}</SelectItem>` bound at :152 `<Select value={form.party_id} onValueChange={(val) => setForm(p => ({ ...p, party_id: val }))}>`, then :49 `await ledgerApi.createEntry(form);` with no sanitization of `party_id`. The service uses it directly as an FK and as an update target — services/ledger.service.ts:104 `party_id: data.partyId,` (schema.prisma:949 `party Party? @relation(fields: [party_id], references: [id])`) and :116-123 `if (data.partyId) { const delta = ...; await prisma.party.update({ where: { id: data.partyId }, data: { balance: { i
- Fix: Normalize before posting: `partyId: form.party_id && form.party_id !== '__none__' ? form.party_id : undefined`, and defensively treat non-uuid party ids as absent in the service.

**?partyId= deep link into the Ledger page is ignored — all business entries are shown**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/LedgerPage.tsx:36` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: Any navigation to /ledger?partyId=<id> renders the full business ledger while the Advanced Filters badge shows "1" active filter, so the user reads another party's (or every party's) debits/credits as if they belonged to the selected party — including the Total Debit/Credit/Net stat cards.
- Evidence: LedgerPage.tsx:36-39 `const [filters, setFilters] = useState<Record<string, any>>(() => { const partyId = searchParams.get('partyId'); return partyId ? { party_id: partyId } : {}; });` -> LedgerPage.tsx:75 `await ledgerApi.entries({ search, ...filters })` -> GET /ledger/entries?party_id=... . getBusinessLedger never looks at party_id or partyId — services/ledger.service.ts:71-76 `const where: any = { business_id: businessId }; if (query.startDate) ...; if (query.endDate) ...; if (query.accountType) ...; if (query.entryType) where.entry_type = query.entryType;` (contrast getPartyLedger:17 `const where: any = { business_id: businessId, party_id: partyId };`).
- Fix: Add `if (query.partyId) where.party_id = query.partyId;` to getBusinessLedger and send `partyId` from the page, or redirect /ledger?partyId=x to /ledger/party/x.

### Parties & Cutters

**Receivable and Payable are swapped on the Parties list — sign convention is inverted vs the backend**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:212` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On /parties, every supplier you owe money to is shown in the green "Receivable" column and rolled into the "Total Receivable" stat card, and every customer who owes you money is shown in the red "Payable" column / "Total Payable" card. The two headline numbers on the page are exactly backwards, and the balance filter ("Receivable"/"Payable") returns the opposite set.
- Evidence: Frontend treats positive as receivable — frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:212-213 `const totalReceivable = parties.filter(p => p.balance > 0).reduce((s, p) => s + p.balance, 0); const totalPayable = parties.filter(p => p.balance < 0).reduce((s, p) => s + Math.abs(p.balance), 0);` and the columns at 233-248 (`p.balance > 0 ? ...Receivable... : '—'`). The backend stores the opposite — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:358-365 `// Update party balance (only the party-facing amount, excluding expenses)\n// Negative = receivable (customer owes us)\nawait tx.party.update({ where: { id: data.partyId }, data: { balance: { decrement: new Prisma.Decimal(partyBalanceAmo
- Fix: Flip PartiesPage.tsx:212-213 and the receivable/payable column renderers at 233-248 to `p.balance < 0` = receivable / `p.balance > 0` = payable, and flip profile.service.ts:243-245 to match (receivable -> `{ lt: 0 }`, payable -> `{ gt: 0 }`).

**Opening-balance receivable/payable toggle writes the wrong sign — list page and detail page disagree for the same party**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/AddPartyDialog.tsx:372` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Create a party with opening balance ₹1,000 and the toggle set to "Receivable" (they owe you). The Parties list shows ₹1,000 as Receivable, but opening the same party's detail page shows a red hero card "₹1,000 — ↑ You owe party", and the Record Payment dialog it launches defaults to type OUT (paying them) instead of IN. The same ₹1,000 is reported in opposite directions on two pages, and the ledger/reports pages inherit the inverted value.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/AddPartyDialog.tsx:372-374 `openingBalance: form.opening_balance ? (form.opening_balance_type === 'payable' ? -Math.abs(parseFloat(form.opening_balance)) : Math.abs(parseFloat(form.opening_balance))) : 0,` (identical logic in frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:452-456) — receivable => positive. profile.service.ts:141-142 stores it straight into the same column the ledger uses: `opening_balance: data.openingBalance ?? 0, balance: data.openingBalance ?? 0, // Initialize balance = opening_balance`. frontend/src/components/parties/PartyDetailPage.tsx:235-236 `// opening_balance: positive = party owes us, negative = we owe them\nconst openingBalance 
- Fix: Pick the backend convention (negative = receivable) and negate in both dialogs: receivable => `-Math.abs(...)`, payable => `Math.abs(...)` (AddPartyDialog.tsx:372-374, EditPartyDialog.tsx:452-456), and fix the stale comment at PartyDetailPage.tsx:235.

**Cutter "Mark as Paid" / "Mark all as paid" never clears the cutter party's payable balance or ledger entry**
- `packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:394` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On /cutters/:id, click "Mark as Paid" on a cutter row (or "Mark all as paid"). The row flips to PAID and the Unpaid stat card drops to ₹0, but the cutter's linked party still carries the full payable balance forever: the party keeps showing that amount as outstanding in the Parties list and the PARTY_PAYABLE ledger entry is never reversed, so "View in Ledger" (the button right there on the same page) still shows the cost as unpaid. Money paid to cutters is permanently double-counted as still owed. The reverse hole exists on the way in: "Record Payment" on the cutter page creates an unpaid transaction that never appears in the party balance/ledger at all.
- Evidence: Mark-paid only touches the flag — packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:394-404 `return prisma.cutterTransaction.update({ where: { id: transactionId, cutter_id: cutterId }, data: { ...(data.isPaid !== undefined && { is_paid: data.isPaid }), ... } });` and 414-417 `return prisma.cutterTransaction.updateMany({ where: { cutter_id: cutterId, is_paid: false }, data: { is_paid: true } });` — no ledgerEntry, no party.update. But the balance/ledger were created by the purchase path: packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:305-336 `// If unpaid — create a cutter payable ledger entry ... await tx.ledgerEntry.create({ data: { ... party_id: cutter?.party_id || null,
- Fix: In updateCutterTransaction/markAllCutterTransactionsPaid, wrap in a $transaction: for each transaction flipped false->true, decrement the linked cutter party's balance by the amount and write the offsetting DEBIT ledger entry (mirror purchase.service.ts:305-336); and in createCutterTransaction, create the PARTY_PAYABLE entry + balance increment when isPaid is false.

**Import button on the Parties page is entirely fake — no API call, but reports "Import successful"**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On /parties, click Import, download the template, upload a CSV of 200 parties, click "Import 200 records". A progress bar animates, the dialog shows a green tick, "Import successful — 200 records imported", and PartiesPage runs fetchParties(). Nothing was ever sent to the server; zero parties are created. The user believes their data was imported.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:281-283 `<Button variant="outline" onClick={() => setShowImport(true)}>...` and :503 `<ImportDataDialog open={showImport} ... defaultModule="parties" />`. frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146-162 `const handleImport = async () => { if (errors.length > 0) return toast.error(...); setStep('importing'); setImporting(true); setProgress(0); // Simulate import progress\n const total = parsedData.length; for (let i = 0; i <= total; i++) { await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 30)); setProgress(Math.round((i / total) * 100)); } setImporting(false); setStep('done'); toast.success(t('common:import_success_toast', { count: parsedData.leng
- Fix: Either wire handleImport to a real bulk endpoint (loop `profileApi.createParty` over parsedData mapping name/phone/email/type/opening_balance -> name/phone/email/type/openingBalance, collecting per-row errors), or remove the Import button until a backend import endpoint exists.

**Bulk "Deactivate selected" toasts success without calling any API**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:360` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On /parties, tick several parties, click the red "Deactivate selected" bulk button. Toast says "Deactivated", selection clears, list refetches — and every selected party is untouched. Silent data-loss illusion identical to the single-row delete.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:360-363 `{ label: t('common:deactivate_selected'), icon: <Trash2 className="h-3.5 w-3.5" />, variant: 'destructive', onClick: () => { toast.success(t('common:deactivated')); fetchParties(); }, },` — the handler receives `items` (BulkSelectDataTable.tsx:96-99 `onClick={async () => { await action.onClick(selectedItems); clearSelection(); }}`) but ignores it and issues no request. Compare the sibling bulk action at PartiesPage.tsx:348-359 which does real work (client-side CSV).
- Fix: `onClick: async (items) => { await Promise.all(items.map(p => profileApi.updateParty(p.id, { isActive: false }))); toast.success(...); fetchParties(); }` with a try/catch that reports failures.

**EditPartyDialog's contacts and bank-account editing is silently discarded**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:427` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Open Edit on an existing party, expand "Contacts / Owners" or "Banking Details", add a contact (name/phone/email/tags) or a bank account (bank, IFSC, account no., branch) — or delete an existing one with the X — and click Save. Toast says "Party updated" and the dialog closes, but nothing was sent: the contact/bank changes vanish on the next open. The exact same fields DO save on the Add dialog, so the behaviour looks random to the user.
- Evidence: The UI exists and mutates local state: frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:307-338 (addContact/removeContact/updateContact/addTag/removeTag/addBank/removeBank/updateBank), rendered at 614-681 and 894-936, and pre-populated from the party at 376-394. But the submit payload has no contacts/bankAccounts keys at all — EditPartyDialog.tsx:427-457 `await profileApi.updateParty(party.id, { name, phone, whatsapp, type, email, isMine, gstRegistrationType, gstNumber, gstState, panNumber, address, city, state, pincode, creditPeriodDays, creditLimit, openingBalance });`. Compare AddPartyDialog.tsx:393-412 which does send `contacts:` and `bankAccounts:`. Even if they were sent, the service
- Fix: Add contacts/bankAccounts to the EditPartyDialog payload and implement them in profile.service.ts updateParty (deleteMany + create inside a $transaction, or per-row upsert/delete), or hide both sections in edit mode until supported.

**EditPartyDialog hardcodes type:'BOTH' and overwrites the party's real type on every save**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:348` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Edit any SUPPLIER or CUSTOMER party (e.g. seeded/imported ones) and change only the phone number: the save silently converts type to BOTH, changing its badge on the detail page and its behaviour in every type-filtered query. Worse, if a cutter's mirror party is opened directly at /parties/<cutter party id> and saved, its type flips from CUTTER to BOTH and the cutter now also appears as a normal party in the Parties list (which filters CUTTER out), producing a visible duplicate of the cutter that can be given purchases/sales independently.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:343-350 populate block hardcodes the value instead of reading party.type: `setForm({ name: party.name || '', phone: party.phone || '', whatsapp: party.whatsapp || '', email: party.email || '', type: 'BOTH', is_mine: party.is_mine ?? false, });` and it is always sent at :431 `type: form.type,`. The service applies it: packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:202 `...(data.type && { type: data.type }),`. Non-BOTH parties really exist: packages/shared/prisma/seed/index.ts:235 `type: 'SUPPLIER',` and :246 `type: 'CUSTOMER',`; cutters create a CUTTER party at profile.service.ts:271-277 `const party = await tx.party.create({ data: 
- Fix: Populate from the record (`type: (party.type as any) || 'BOTH'`) and omit `type` from the update payload unless the user actually changes it; add a SUPPLIER/CUSTOMER/BOTH selector (and never send it for CUTTER parties).

### Profile, Notifications, Help

**/profile/me responses leak the user's bcrypt password_hash (and it is then written to localStorage)**
- `packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:8` — *data-leak*
- Impact: Every profile read/write returns the full `users` row, including `password_hash`, plus `aadhar_number` and full bank account numbers. ProfilePage then persists that whole object into localStorage under `bk_user`, so any XSS or shared-browser access yields the offline-crackable password hash.
- Evidence: profile.service.ts:8-19 selects the entire row with no `select`: `const user = await prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { id: userId }, include: { profile: true, bank_accounts: true, business_users: {...} } });` and schema.prisma:25 declares `password_hash String?` on that model. profile.controller.ts:46 ships it verbatim: `res.json({ success: true, message: 'Profile updated', data: profile });` (same for getProfile:39, uploadAvatar:177 `data: { avatarUrl, user: updated }`, removeAvatar:202). ProfilePage.tsx:64 `dispatch(setUser(data.data));` and authSlice.ts:52-55 `setUser(state, action) { state.user = action.payload; ... localStorage.setItem('bk_user', JSON.stringify(action.payload)); }`. Con
- Fix: Add an explicit `select` in profile.service.ts:getProfile (id, phone, name, email, avatar_url, is_active, is_super_admin, has_paid_plan, created_at) plus the nested profile/business fields actually used, or delete `password_hash` before responding. Also have ProfilePage dispatch a whitelisted user object rather than the raw payload.

**Uploaded avatar never renders in production — /uploads/avatars is not proxied by the frontend deployment**
- `frontend/vercel.json:9` — *missing-route*
- Impact: Avatar upload appears to succeed (toast fires, redux updates) but the picture is a broken image on the profile page and in the header for every production user: the URL resolves against the frontend host, which returns the SPA index.html instead of a JPEG. Only the dev Vite proxy makes this work locally.
- Evidence: profile.controller.ts:172 stores a host-relative path — `const avatarUrl = `/uploads/avatars/${req.file.filename}`;` — and ImageUpload.tsx:69 / AppLayout.tsx:315 render it raw (`<img src={src} .../>`, `<AvatarImage src={user.avatar_url} />`) with no API-base prefix. frontend/vite.config.ts:server.proxy declares BOTH `'/api'` and `'/uploads'` -> http://localhost:3000, but frontend/vercel.json only rewrites the API: `"rewrites": [{ "source": "/api/:path*", "destination": "https://bahi-khata-backend.vercel.app/api/:path*" }]` — there is no `/uploads/:path*` rule, even though api-gateway/src/index.ts:204-211 does expose the avatar proxy (`pathFilter: '/uploads/avatars'`).
- Fix: Add `{ "source": "/uploads/:path*", "destination": "https://bahi-khata-backend.vercel.app/uploads/:path*" }` to frontend/vercel.json. Separately, packages/profile-service/vercel.json deploys index.ts as an @vercel/node function, whose filesystem is read-only/ephemeral, so multer's `path.join(process.cwd(),'uploads','avatars')` destination (profile.controller.ts:11) cannot persist there at all — mo

**Header notification badge is a hardcoded "3"**
- `frontend/src/components/layout/AppLayout.tsx:307` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Every user always sees a red "3" on the bell on every page. It does not reflect real unread notifications, does not disappear after Mark All Read, and shows "3" even for a brand-new account with zero notifications — so the badge is permanently misleading and permanently nags.
- Evidence: AppLayout.tsx:305-308 `<Button ... onClick={() => navigate('/notifications')} className="relative"><Bell .../><span className="absolute -top-1 -right-1 ...">3</span></Button>` — no state, no selector, no API call. The real number is already returned by the API and thrown away: notification.service.ts:70 `prisma.notification.count({ where: { user_id: userId, is_read: false } })` -> :73-77 `return { data: notifications, meta: ..., unreadCount }` -> controller:8 `res.json({ success: true, ...result })`, while NotificationsPage.tsx:79 recomputes its own `unreadCount` locally and nothing in AppLayout ever calls notificationApi.
- Fix: Fetch GET /notifications?limit=1 (or add a dedicated /notifications/unread-count) into a redux slice on mount, render `unreadCount > 0 && <span>{unreadCount}</span>`, and invalidate it after markRead/markAllRead.

**Help "Contact Support" form posts nowhere — fake 1.5s delay then a success toast**
- `frontend/src/components/help/HelpSupportPage.tsx:104` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Users type a support request, are told "Message sent! We'll get back to you soon.", the form clears — and the message is silently discarded. No ticket, no email, nothing reaches the operator.
- Evidence: HelpSupportPage.tsx:98-109 is the entire submit handler: `const handleContact = async (e) => { e.preventDefault(); if (!contactForm.subject.trim() || !contactForm.message.trim()) return toast.error(...); setSending(true); /* Simulate sending */ await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 1500)); toast.success(t('help:message_sent')); setContactForm({ subject: '', message: '' }); setSending(false); };` — the file imports no API client at all (imports at :1-12 are react/framer/i18n/lucide/ui/toast only), and en/help.json:12 supplies the reassuring copy `"message_sent": "Message sent! We'll get back to you soon."`. No support/contact route exists in any service (grep over packages/*/src/routes found n
- Fix: Either add a real endpoint (e.g. POST /api/v1/notifications/support-ticket that stores the ticket and emails support@bahikhata.in) and call it, or remove the form and keep only the mailto:/WhatsApp links so the UI stops making a promise it cannot keep.

**notification-service trusts x-business-id blindly — cross-tenant inventory read and cross-tenant WhatsApp sends; process-bill-reminders is global with no admin check**
- `packages/notification-service/src/shared/middleware/rbac.middleware.ts:12` — *missing-authz*
- Impact: Any logged-in user can (a) read another business's low-stock inventory (item names, current/min stock) by sending that business's id in the header, (b) trigger WhatsApp "payment reminder" messages to another business's customers and inject in-app notifications into that business owner's feed, and (c) fire reminder WhatsApp messages for EVERY business in the database with a single request.
- Evidence: rbac.middleware.ts:12-25 only checks presence: `const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string || req.params.businessId; if (!businessId) { res.status(400)... } (req as ...).businessId = businessId; next();` — there is no BusinessUser lookup (the file's own requirePermission at :30-37 does that work, but notification.routes.ts never uses it). notification.routes.ts:20-23 mounts the sensitive routes behind that middleware only: `router.use(requireBusiness); router.post('/payment-reminder', ctrl.sendPaymentReminder); router.post('/low-stock-alerts', ctrl.sendLowStockAlerts); router.post('/process-bill-reminders', ctrl.processBillReminders);`. The controller then uses the raw header:
- Fix: Replace `requireBusiness` with a membership-verifying guard (query BusinessUser for {user_id, business_id} and 403 on miss) for /payment-reminder and /low-stock-alerts, and put /process-bill-reminders behind requireSuperAdmin or drop the HTTP route entirely since index.ts:38-53 already schedules it internally.

**Notifications page invents six fake notifications whenever the API fails, and swallows mark-read failures**
- `frontend/src/components/notifications/NotificationsPage.tsx:47` — *fake-data*
- Impact: If notification-service is down (its default PORT 3009 collides with expense-service) or the token is rejected, the user is shown six fabricated notifications — "Gupta Trading paid ₹80,000 via UPI", "Ram Traders has an overdue payment of ₹28,000" — as if they were real business events, with no error indication. Likewise, mark-read / mark-all-read tick the items in the UI (and toast "All notifications marked as read") even when the request failed, so the state silently reverts on reload.
- Evidence: NotificationsPage.tsx:46-54 `} catch { setNotifications([{ id: '1', type: 'PAYMENT', title: 'Payment Received', message: 'Gupta Trading paid ₹80,000 via UPI', is_read: false, created_at: '2024-01-15T10:30:00Z' }, ... ]); }` replaces the real list on ANY error from `notificationApi.list()` (:44). The mutations do the same: :64-66 `} catch { setNotifications(prev => prev.map(n => n.id === id ? { ...n, is_read: true } : n)); }` and :74-76 `} catch { setNotifications(prev => prev.map(n => ({ ...n, is_read: true }))); }`. Server side these calls are real and would have reported the truth — notification.controller.ts:11-21 markAsRead/markAllAsRead — and note the mock `type` values ('PAYMENT','PURC
- Fix: Delete the mock fallback; on error set an error state and render the EmptyState/retry UI. In the mutation catches, revert the optimistic update and `toast.error(...)` instead of asserting success.

### Purchases

**Expense/cutter `isPaid` and `receiptUrl` stripped on create — unpaid expenses are stored as paid, payable ledger entries are never created, receipts are lost**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:165` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: User adds a ₹5,000 transport expense, clicks the Unpaid toggle and attaches a receipt photo. The receipt uploads successfully (POST /purchases/expenses/receipt-upload returns a URL) but the purchase is created with is_paid = true and receipt_url = NULL, so the detail page shows the expense as "Paid" with no receipt link and no PARTY_PAYABLE ledger entry is created for it. Same for cutter rows: an unpaid cutter is recorded as paid and the cutter's party balance is never incremented, so the money owed to the cutter disappears from the books.
- Evidence: Frontend sends them — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:804-819: `expenses: validExpenses.map(ex => ({ expenseTypeId, expenseCategory, amount, isPaid: ex.is_paid, receiptUrl: ex.receipt_url || undefined, notes }))` and `cutterTransactions: validCutters.map(c => ({ cutterId, quantity, rate, isPaid: c.is_paid, receiptUrl: c.receipt_url || undefined, notes }))`. Schemas drop both keys — packages/purchase-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:165-177: `export const purchaseExpenseSchema = z.object({ expenseTypeId, expenseCategory, amount, notes });` and `export const cutterTransactionSchema = z.object({ cutterId, quantity, rate, notes });` (stripped by validate.middleware.ts:10-
- Fix: Add `isPaid: z.boolean().optional(), receiptUrl: z.string().url().optional()` to both purchaseExpenseSchema and cutterTransactionSchema.

**Purchase attachment / receipt URLs are unreachable: gateway has no /uploads/purchase proxy and the service stores an absolute internal-host URL**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/controllers/purchase.controller.ts:143` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: Every uploaded bill attachment, payment receipt, expense receipt and cutter receipt produces a link/thumbnail that fails to load. The detail page attachment cards render broken images and their "open" links 404; the same for the "Receipt" links next to payments/expenses/cutters and the preview modal in the form. Files are stored as `http://localhost:3003/uploads/...` because the gateway proxies with changeOrigin (so `req.get('host')` is the internal service host) and never sets x-forwarded-host — a URL no browser can resolve in any real deployment. Even a relative path would fail because the gateway only proxies /uploads/business and /uploads/avatars.
- Evidence: Service builds an absolute URL from the proxied Host header — packages/purchase-service/src/controllers/purchase.controller.ts:143-145: `const host = req.get('x-forwarded-host') || req.get('host') || \`localhost:${process.env.PURCHASE_SERVICE_PORT || 3003}\`;` / `const fileUrl = \`${protocol}://${host}/uploads/purchase/attachments/${req.file.filename}\`;` (identical at :171-173 and :193-195 for receipts). Gateway sets no forwarded-host header — packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:174-181 proxyReq only forwards `Authorization` and `x-business-id` (with `changeOrigin: true` at :166), and the only static passthroughs are packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:195-210: `pathFilter: '/uploads/business'`
- Fix: Store relative URLs (`/uploads/purchase/attachments/${req.file.filename}`) as business-service does, and add a gateway passthrough: `app.use(createProxyMiddleware({ target: serviceHost('purchase-service', SERVICE_PORTS.PURCHASE_SERVICE), changeOrigin: true, pathFilter: '/uploads/purchase' }))`.

**Payment mode CARD is offered in the UI but rejected by the create validator (400 on save)**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:179` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: User picks "Card" in the Payment Mode dropdown and clicks Save Purchase. The whole create fails with a red toast ("Validation failed" -> `payments.0.paymentMode Invalid enum value`), and nothing is saved — even though receipts were already uploaded. The same value saves fine when editing an existing purchase (PATCH is unvalidated), and CARD is a legal DB value, so the failure looks arbitrary.
- Evidence: UI offers CARD — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:1727: `<SelectItem value="CARD">{t('common:mode_card')}</SelectItem>` (sent verbatim at :821 `paymentMode: p.payment_mode`). Validator omits it — packages/purchase-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:179-180: `export const paymentSchema = z.object({ paymentMode: z.enum(['CASH', 'BANK_TRANSFER', 'UPI', 'CHEQUE', 'CREDIT', 'MIXED']),`. Prisma allows it — packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:886-894: `enum PaymentMode { CASH BANK_TRANSFER UPI CARD CHEQUE CREDIT MIXED }`.
- Fix: Add `'CARD'` to the paymentMode enum in paymentSchema so it matches the Prisma PaymentMode enum and the UI dropdown.

**Deleting an already-uploaded attachment in edit mode never calls the delete endpoint — the attachment comes back on reload**
- `frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:692` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On the Edit Purchase screen the user hovers an existing bill, clicks the trash icon (it disappears), and clicks Update Purchase. Nothing is deleted server-side: reopening the purchase shows the attachment again. `purchaseApi.deleteAttachment` exists and the route works, but no code path in the app ever calls it — the delete button is decorative for persisted files.
- Evidence: Handler is local-state only — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:692-699: `const removeAttachment = (gadiKey: string, attKey: string) => { setGadis(prev => prev.map(g => { ... return { ...g, attachments: g.attachments.filter(a => a._key !== attKey) }; })); };` — no API call, no bookkeeping of removed ids, and the submit path only ever uploads (:842 `for (const att of g.attachments.filter(a => a.file && !a.uploadedId))`). The API method and route exist but are unreferenced — frontend/src/lib/api.ts:325-326: `deleteAttachment: (purchaseId, attachmentId) => api.delete(\`/purchases/${purchaseId}/attachments/${attachmentId}\`)`; packages/purchase-service/src/routes/purchase.routes.
- Fix: Track removed uploaded attachments (e.g. push `att.uploadedId` into a `deletedAttachmentIds` ref inside removeAttachment) and, in handleSubmit, `await Promise.all(deletedAttachmentIds.map(id => purchaseApi.deleteAttachment(purchaseId, id)))` before/after the PATCH.

**Import Data dialog reports a successful purchase import without calling any API**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: From Purchases -> Import, the user uploads a CSV, sees a validated preview, clicks Import, watches a progress bar to 100% and gets "N records imported". Nothing is sent anywhere — no purchase is created. The user believes their bulk data is in the system and it silently isn't.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146-162: `const handleImport = async () => { ... // Simulate import progress\n const total = parsedData.length;\n for (let i = 0; i <= total; i++) { await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 30)); setProgress(...); }\n setImporting(false); setStep('done'); toast.success(t('common:import_success_toast', { count: parsedData.length, module })); };` — the file contains no api/purchaseApi import at all.
