Mounted as a real feature on the purchases list — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchasesPage.tsx:159-161 `<Button variant="outline" onClick={() => setShowImport(true)}> <Upload .../> {t('common:import')}` and :234-239 `<ImportDataDialog open={show
- Fix: Either wire handleImport to a real bulk endpoint (e.g. loop `purchaseApi.create` over mapped rows, or add POST /purchases/import) and surface per-row errors, or disable the Import button and stop emitting a success toast.

**Cutter party balance is double-counted on every edit and never reversed on delete**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:1004` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Create a purchase with an unpaid cutter cost of ₹2,000: the cutter's party balance goes to ₹2,000 (correct). Open the purchase, change anything, click Update Purchase: the cutter row is deleted and re-created, and the balance is incremented again -> ₹4,000. Every subsequent save adds another ₹2,000. Deleting the purchase entirely leaves the inflated balance behind. The cutter/party ledger page therefore shows money owed that does not exist.
- Evidence: updatePurchase reverses only the supplier's balance — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:825-830: `const oldPartyAmount = Number(existing.subtotal) + ...; await tx.party.update({ where: { id: existing.party_id }, data: { balance: { decrement: new Prisma.Decimal(oldPartyBalance) } } });` — nothing decrements the cutter parties, even though the old cutter rows are wiped at :834 `await tx.cutterTransaction.deleteMany({ where: { purchase_id: purchaseId } });`. Then it increments again for each re-created unpaid cutter — purchase.service.ts:1004-1010: `// Increment the cutter Party's payable balance\n if (cutter?.party_id) { await tx.party.update({ where: { id: cutter.part
- Fix: Before deleting the old cutter_transactions in updatePurchase/deletePurchase, load them with their cutter's party_id and decrement each cutter party's balance by the unpaid amount (`is_paid === false ? amount : 0`), mirroring the increment logic.

**Editing or deleting a purchase whose lots were partially sold corrupts lots and stock**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:808` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Buy 100 units (lot LOT-1), sell 40 (lot shows available 60, sold 40, stock 60). Now edit that purchase (e.g. fix the bill number) and save: LOT-1 is CANCELLED with available_qty 0 and a brand-new lot with available_qty 100 is created, so the Sales lot picker again offers the 40 units that were already sold — the business can oversell, and the existing SaleLot rows now point at a cancelled lot (COGS/lot history broken). Deleting the purchase is worse: current_stock is decremented by the full original 100 even though sales already removed 40, so stock is understated by 40 (clamped at 0).
- Evidence: Edit path reverses full original quantity and cancels lots with no regard for sold_qty — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:808-823: `for (const oldItem of existing.items) { ... quantity_in: new Prisma.Decimal(Math.max(0, Number(inv.quantity_in) - Number(oldItem.quantity))), current_stock: new Prisma.Decimal(Math.max(0, Number(inv.current_stock) - Number(oldItem.quantity))) ... }` then `for (const lot of existing.lots) { await tx.lot.update({ where: { id: lot.id }, data: { status: 'CANCELLED', available_qty: 0 } }); }`, and :923-937 creates a fresh lot with `initial_qty` = `available_qty` = full new quantity. Delete path does the same double-deduction — purchase.servi
- Fix: Refuse the edit/delete (throw a 409 AppError) when any lot of the purchase has `sold_qty > 0`; otherwise reverse only the unsold portion and carry sold_qty/available_qty forward instead of cancel-and-recreate.

### Sales

**Selecting payment mode 'Card' makes the whole sale fail with 400 Validation failed**
- `packages/sales-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:180` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: User records a sale, picks 'Card' in the Payments Received mode dropdown, clicks Save. The POST /sales returns 400 and the page shows the toast 'Validation failed'. The sale is never created and there is no indication which field is wrong — the user has to guess and change the payment mode.
- Evidence: Frontend offers CARD — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:1161-1166: `<SelectItem value="CASH">...<SelectItem value="UPI">...<SelectItem value="BANK_TRANSFER">...<SelectItem value="CARD">{t('common:mode_card')}</SelectItem><SelectItem value="CHEQUE">...<SelectItem value="CREDIT">...` and it is sent verbatim at :652-653: `payments: validPayments.map(p => ({ paymentMode: p.payment_mode, ... }))` Validator rejects it — packages/sales-service/src/shared/validators/index.ts:179-181: `export const paymentSchema = z.object({ paymentMode: z.enum(['CASH', 'BANK_TRANSFER', 'UPI', 'CHEQUE', 'CREDIT', 'MIXED']), amount: z.number().positive(), ...` The database enum DOES allow it — packages
- Fix: Add `'CARD'` to the paymentMode enum in paymentSchema (and createPaymentSchema at validators/index.ts:269, which has the same gap) so it matches the Prisma PaymentMode enum.

**Edit mode: changed lots, quantities, rates and payments are silently discarded by PATCH /sales/:saleId**
- `packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:710` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: On /sales/:id/edit the form renders fully editable lot rows (lot, qty, rate) and payment rows, and the header shows a recomputed total. The user changes a quantity from 10 to 5, deletes a payment row, clicks 'Update Sale' and gets the green 'Sale updated' toast. Back on the detail page nothing changed: sale_lots, sale_items, paid_amount, lot available_qty and inventory are all untouched, and total_amount is recomputed from the OLD subtotal. Stock and receivables silently diverge from what the user thinks they saved.
- Evidence: Frontend sends the full payload for edit — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:642-665: `saleLots: validItems.map(i => { ... return { lotId: i.lot_id, itemId: ..., quantitySold: i.qty_in_stock_unit, rate: i.rate }; }), expenses: [], payments: validPayments.map(p => ({ paymentMode: p.payment_mode, amount: p.amount, ... })) }; ... if (isEditMode && entry.saleId) { await salesApi.update(entry.saleId, payload); saleId = entry.saleId; toast.success(t('sales:sale_updated')); }` Backend accepts none of it — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:710-722: `async updateSale(saleId: string, businessId: string, _userId: string | undefined, data: { partyId?: string; saleDate?:
- Fix: Either make the lot/payment sections read-only in edit mode (disable the selects/inputs and hide add/remove buttons when isEditMode) and stop sending saleLots/payments, or implement lot+payment re-processing in updateSale (reverse old lot/inventory/ledger deltas inside the transaction, then re-apply) as the create path does.

**Edit mode cannot clear a discount, round-off or GST — zeroing a field leaves the old value in the DB**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:630` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: A sale has a Rs 500 discount and 18% GST. The user opens /sales/:id/edit, clears the discount field to 0 and switches GST to 'None', clicks Update, sees 'Sale updated'. The detail page still shows the Rs 500 discount and the GST line, and total_amount/balance_amount/party balance are unchanged — the correction is impossible through the UI.
- Evidence: Frontend converts 0/NONE into `undefined` (i.e. omits the field) — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:630-634: `gstMode: entry.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? entry.gst_mode : undefined, gstValue: entry.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? entry.gst_value : undefined, gstAmount: entry.gst_mode !== 'NONE' ? entryGstAmount(entry) : undefined, discount: entry.discount || undefined, roundOff: entry.round_off || undefined,` (JSON.stringify drops undefined keys). Backend treats an absent field as 'keep old' — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:732-734: `const newDiscount = data.discount !== undefined ? data.discount : Number(existing.discount); const newRoundOff = data.roundOff !== undefine
- Fix: Send explicit values in edit mode: `discount: entry.discount ?? 0, roundOff: entry.round_off ?? 0, gstMode: entry.gst_mode, gstValue: entry.gst_mode === 'NONE' ? 0 : entry.gst_value, gstAmount: entry.gst_mode === 'NONE' ? 0 : entryGstAmount(entry)`. Keep the backend's 'undefined = keep' semantics, which are then correct.

**/sales/:id/edit creates a duplicate new sale instead of updating when the prefill does not run**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:272` — *data-bug*
- Impact: The prefill is gated on the lots list being non-empty and on salesApi.get succeeding. If either master-data request fails (network blip, 403, 500) or sale fetch throws, the edit page renders a BLANK form still titled 'Edit Sale' with entry.saleId undefined. The user fills it in and clicks 'Update Sale' — because entry.saleId is undefined the code takes the CREATE branch, so a second, duplicate sale is inserted (deducting stock again) while the sale being edited is untouched.
- Evidence: Prefill gate — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:272-277: `useEffect(() => { if (editId && lots.length > 0 && !prefillDone.current) { prefillDone.current = true; prefillSale(); } }, [editId, lots]);` and the master-data catch that empties lots at :287-289: `} catch { setLots([]); }` (a failure of EITHER promise in the Promise.all at :281-284 lands here). prefillSale's own failure path at :386-388: `} catch { toast.error(t('sales:load_error')); }` — entries is left as the initial `[blankEntry(defaultPartyId)]` from :263, whose saleId is undefined (blankEntry at :166-175 sets no saleId). The save branch keys off entry.saleId, not isEditMode — :663-671: `if (isEditMode && entry.s
- Fix: Gate the prefill on editId only (`if (editId && !prefillDone.current)`) and set `saleId: editId` on the entry immediately; block submit while an edit prefill is pending or failed. Additionally guard the save branch: `if (isEditMode) { if (!entry.saleId) { toast.error(...); return; } await salesApi.update(...) }` so an edit route can never fall through to create.

**Lot dropdown only lists status === 'AVAILABLE', so any partially-sold lot can never be sold again**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:868` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Sell 10 of a 100-unit lot. createSale flips that lot's status to PARTIAL. From then on the lot never appears in the 'Select lot' dropdown on /sales/new, even though 90 units are still available — the remaining stock is unsellable through the UI and shows up as permanently stuck inventory. Every lot in a business becomes invisible after its first sale.
- Evidence: Frontend filter — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:868-871: `{lots.filter(l => l.status === 'AVAILABLE' || (isEditMode && entry.lineItems.some(li => li.lot_id === l.id))).map(l => (` — PARTIAL is only tolerated for lots already on the sale being edited. The backend explicitly supports PARTIAL — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:65-71: `const lot = await tx.lot.findFirst({ where: { id: saleLot.lotId, business_id: businessId, status: { in: ['AVAILABLE', 'PARTIAL'] } } });` and create always demotes the lot to PARTIAL — :196: `const lotStatus = newAvailable <= 0 ? 'SOLD_OUT' : 'PARTIAL';` (LotStatus enum at packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:685-691 confirms
- Fix: Change the filter to `l.status === 'AVAILABLE' || l.status === 'PARTIAL' || (isEditMode && ...)` — or better, pass the filter to the server: `salesApi.lots({ status: 'AVAILABLE,PARTIAL' })` is not supported, so filter client-side on `Number(l.available_qty) > 0 && !['SOLD_OUT','CANCELLED','EXPIRED'].includes(l.status)`.

**'Import' on the sales page is a fake progress bar — no data is ever imported**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: User clicks Import on /sales, downloads the sales template, uploads a CSV of 200 sales, sees a progress bar animate to 100% and the toast '200 records imported (sales)'. Zero sales are created. SalesPage's onSuccess refetches the list, which is unchanged, so the user assumes a display bug and may re-import repeatedly.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146-162: `const handleImport = async () => { if (errors.length > 0) return toast.error(...); setStep('importing'); setImporting(true); setProgress(0); // Simulate import progress\n const total = parsedData.length; for (let i = 0; i <= total; i++) { await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 30)); setProgress(Math.round((i / total) * 100)); } setImporting(false); setStep('done'); toast.success(t('common:import_success_toast', { count: parsedData.length, module })); };` — the file imports no api client; there is no POST anywhere in the component. Wired into the sales page at frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:234-239: `<ImportDataDialog op
- Fix: Either hide the Import button on SalesPage until a real bulk endpoint exists, or add POST /api/v1/sales/bulk-import to the sales service and call it from handleImport, reporting real per-row errors instead of a simulated progress loop.

**Sale bill/receipt URLs are built from the internal upstream host and the gateway has no /uploads/sale proxy, so attachment links are broken**
- `packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:201` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: User uploads a bill on the sale detail page (or a payment receipt in the create form). The upload succeeds, but the stored file_url points at the sales-service's own host as seen by the proxy — e.g. http://sales-service:3004/uploads/sale/attachments/x.pdf in Docker/deployed setups. Clicking the external-link / Eye / Download button opens a URL the browser cannot resolve, so every uploaded bill and receipt is unviewable. Requests to /uploads/sale/... through the gateway 404 because the gateway only proxies /uploads/business and /uploads/avatars.
- Evidence: Gateway forwards no x-forwarded-host and rewrites Host (changeOrigin) — packages/api-gateway/src/index.ts:173-181: `proxyReq: (proxyReq, req) => { if (req.headers.authorization) { proxyReq.setHeader('Authorization', req.headers.authorization); } if (req.headers['x-business-id']) { proxyReq.setHeader('x-business-id', req.headers['x-business-id'] as string); } }` (only those two headers) with `changeOrigin: true` at :167; and the only static uploads proxies are :196-212: `pathFilter: '/uploads/business'` and `pathFilter: '/uploads/avatars'` — nothing for /uploads/sale. Controller builds an absolute URL from that host — packages/sales-service/src/controllers/sales.controller.ts:153-155: `const 
- Fix: Add `createProxyMiddleware({ target: serviceHost('sales-service', SERVICE_PORTS.SALES_SERVICE), changeOrigin: true, pathFilter: '/uploads/sale' })` to the gateway, forward `x-forwarded-host`/`x-forwarded-proto` in proxyReq, and store a relative path (`/uploads/sale/attachments/<file>`) in file_url so the browser resolves it against its own origin.

**Sales list falls back to four hard-coded fake sales on any API error**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:45` — *data-bug*
- Impact: If GET /sales fails for any reason — 403 (user lacks SALE_VIEW), 400 (no x-business-id yet), 500, 502 from the gateway, or a dropped connection — the page renders four fabricated sales ('Gupta Trading' Rs 72,000, 'Krishna Exports' Rs 95,000, 'Jai Hind Stores' Rs 54,000, 'Agarwal Traders' Rs 118,000) with no error message. The StatCards then show Rs 3,39,000 total / Rs 2,42,000 received as if real, and clicking a row navigates to /sales/1 which 404s. A user can read and act on entirely invented financial data.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:40-55: `const fetchSales = async () => { try { setLoading(true); const { data } = await salesApi.list({ search, ...filters }); setSales(data?.data || []); } catch { setSales([ { id: '1', sale_number: 'SAL-001', ..., total_amount: 72000, paid_amount: 72000, ... }, { id: '2', sale_number: 'SAL-002', ..., total_amount: 95000, ... }, ... ] as Sale[]); } finally { setLoading(false); } };` — the catch is unconditional (no dev-mode guard) and never surfaces an error. The totals are computed straight off that array — :68-70: `const totalAmount = sales.reduce((s, p) => s + Number(p.total_amount), 0); const totalPaid = ...; const totalBalance = ...;`
- Fix: Only use mock data behind the existing dev-mock token check (`store.getState().auth.token?.startsWith('dev-mock-') && import.meta.env.DEV`, cf. frontend/src/lib/api.ts:117-122); otherwise set an error state and render an error banner with a retry, leaving `sales` empty.

### Subscription & Referrals

**POST /subscriptions/cancel cancels the subscription of any business the caller merely belongs to (no OWNER check)**
- `packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:326` — *cross-tenant-write*
- Impact: A STAFF/ACCOUNTANT invited into someone else's business can kill that owner's active paid subscription with a single authenticated POST. Because auth-service then reports the trial as expired, the owner and every other member are locked out of the whole app.
- Evidence: Membership is collected with no role filter and the first matching subscription across all of them is cancelled: `const businessUsers = await prisma.businessUser.findMany({ where: { user_id: userId, is_active: true }, select: { business_id: true } });` … `const subscription = await prisma.subscription.findFirst({ where: { business_id: { in: businessIds }, status: { in: ['TRIAL','ACTIVE'] } } });` … `return prisma.subscription.update({ where: { id: subscription.id }, data: { status: 'CANCELLED', cancelled_at: new Date() } });` (subscription.service.ts:327-348). Non-owner memberships are routinely created: `const businessUser = await prisma.businessUser.create({ data: { ... role: role as any }
- Fix: Restrict the query to businesses where the caller is OWNER (`prisma.businessUser.findMany({ where: { user_id: userId, role: 'OWNER', is_active: true } })`) and require the target subscription id in the request so an arbitrary tenant's row can never be selected implicitly.

**POST /subscriptions attaches the new subscription to another tenant's business and cancels that tenant's existing plan**
- `packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:67` — *cross-tenant-write*
- Impact: A user who was first invited as staff into someone else's business and later created their own business gets their upgrade written onto the employer's business — cancelling the employer's live subscription in the process — while their own business remains without a plan (and therefore still locked/expired).
- Evidence: "Primary business" is resolved from every membership regardless of role, ordering by a business-level flag that belongs to whoever created the business: `const primaryBusinessUser = await prisma.businessUser.findFirst({ where: { user_id: userId, is_active: true }, include: { business: { select: { id: true, is_primary: true } } }, orderBy: [{ business: { is_primary: 'desc' } }, { joined_at: 'asc' }] }); ... const businessId = primaryBusinessUser.business_id;` (subscription.service.ts:67-73). `is_primary` is true for the *creator's* first business — `const existingBusinessCount = await tx.businessUser.count({ where: { user_id: userId, is_active: true } }); const isPrimary = existingBusinessCou
- Fix: Scope subscription ownership to businesses the user OWNS (`role: 'OWNER'`) and pick the target deterministically per-user (e.g. a `users.primary_business_id`, or the oldest OWNER membership); never mutate subscriptions of a business the caller does not own.

**Referral leaderboard renders blank/garbage: service returns {name, phone, count, total_rewards}, page destructures {rank, referrals, earnings}**
- `frontend/src/components/referrals/ReferralsPage.tsx:348` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: The whole Leaderboard card is broken for every user: rank badges read "#undefined", each row says "undefined successful referrals" (translated count with an undefined value), every earnings figure renders ₹0, and the "You" self-highlight never activates.
- Evidence: Server field names (raw SQL aliases): `const leaderboard = await prisma.$queryRaw<Array<{ referrer_id: string; name: string; phone: string; count: number; total_rewards: number; }>>` … `SELECT r.referrer_id, u.name, u.phone, COUNT(r.id)::int as count, COALESCE(SUM(...),0)::float as total_rewards` (packages/referral-service/src/services/referral.service.ts:385-398), returned verbatim as `res.json({ success: true, data: leaderboard })` (referral.controller.ts:50). Client expects different names: `interface LeaderboardEntry { rank: number; name: string; referrals: number; earnings: number; }` (ReferralsPage.tsx:19-24) and renders `{entry.rank <= 3 ? ['🥇','🥈','🥉'][entry.rank - 1] : `#${entry.
- Fix: Map in the service before responding: `leaderboard.map((r, i) => ({ rank: i + 1, name: r.referrer_id === currentUserId ? 'You' : maskName(r.name), referrals: r.count, earnings: r.total_rewards }))`, pass the caller's userId into getReferralLeaderboard, and drop `u.phone` from the SELECT.

**Expired subscriptions are still returned as "current", so the renew button for that plan is disabled — locked-out users cannot re-buy their own plan**
- `packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:47` — *correctness*
- Impact: Nothing ever flips a lapsed subscription out of TRIAL/ACTIVE, so GET /subscriptions/current keeps returning it. On the page a user is redirected to *because* their plan expired, that exact plan shows "Current plan" with a disabled button — the only way to regain access is to buy a different tier.
- Evidence: The query filters on status only, never on the period end: `return prisma.subscription.findFirst({ where: { business_id: { in: businessIds }, status: { in: ['TRIAL','ACTIVE'] } }, include: { plan: true }, orderBy: { current_period_end: 'desc' } });` (subscription.service.ts:47-54) — compare auth-service, which does gate on time: `status: { in: ['ACTIVE','TRIAL'] }, current_period_end: { gte: new Date() }` (packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:47-52), which is why trialInfo says expired while `current` still looks live. The page turns that into a dead button: `const isCurrent = isSetup ? current?.plan?.slug === plan.slug : (current?.plan?.slug === plan.slug || (!current && plan.
- Fix: Add `current_period_end: { gte: new Date() }` to getCurrentSubscription (and/or a scheduled job flipping lapsed rows to EXPIRED), and in the UI compute isCurrent as `current?.plan?.slug === plan.slug && !trialInfo?.expired` so renewal of the same plan stays clickable.

**Redeem rewards burns all earned days against a possibly-expired period end and reports a past expiry date**
- `packages/referral-service/src/services/referral.service.ts:253` — *correctness*
- Impact: A user whose subscription lapsed more than a month ago clicks "Redeem 30 days", sees a green toast "Redeemed 30 days! Subscription extended to <a date in the past>", and loses the reward permanently — redeemed_days is written even though access was not restored. The banner then disappears because redeemableDays is now 0.
- Evidence: The target subscription is selected without any time check and the new end is derived from the stale value: `subscriptions: { where: { status: { in: ['ACTIVE','TRIAL'] } }, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' }, take: 1 }` (referral.service.ts:243-247), `const newEnd = new Date(subscription.current_period_end); newEnd.setDate(newEnd.getDate() + totalRedeemableDays);` (referral.service.ts:258-259). Days are then marked consumed in a separate, non-transactional loop: `data: { redeemed_days: r.redeemable_days, redeemed_at: new Date() }` (referral.service.ts:271-277). The success text is generated from that past date — `message: \`Redeemed ${result.daysRedeemed} days! Subscription extended to ${result
- Fix: Base the extension on `const base = new Date(Math.max(Date.now(), subscription.current_period_end.getTime()))`, and wrap the subscription update plus all referral redeemed_days updates in a single `prisma.$transaction` so days can never be consumed without a matching extension.

**Razorpay webhook can never verify: HMAC is computed over a re-serialized body with an empty secret**
- `packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:234` — *correctness*
- Impact: Even once checkout is wired up, every payment.captured/payment.failed callback throws "Invalid webhook signature" and returns 500 to Razorpay. Invoices stay PENDING, subscriptions are never activated by payment, and the referral first-paid-purchase credit never fires — paid users would be left unprovisioned.
- Evidence: The secret defaults to empty and is not present in any env file (`grep -c RAZORPAY .env` → 0): `const RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET = process.env.RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET || '';` (subscription.service.ts:11). The digest is taken over Express's re-stringified JSON rather than the raw request bytes: `const expectedSignature = crypto.createHmac('sha256', RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET).update(JSON.stringify(body)).digest('hex'); if (expectedSignature !== signature) { throw new Error('Invalid webhook signature'); }` (subscription.service.ts:234-241), while the body was already parsed and discarded by `app.use(express.json({ limit: '10mb' }));` (packages/subscription-service/src/index.ts:15). The thrown plain 
- Fix: Mount `express.raw({ type: 'application/json' })` for `/api/v1/subscriptions/webhook/razorpay` only, verify with `crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(req.body).digest('hex')` against `x-razorpay-signature` using `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, parse afterwards, fail fast at boot if RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET is unset, and return 200/400 (never 500) so Razorpay does not retry-storm.

### WhatsApp AI service

**Duplicate AMOUNT entities: the first (unmultiplied) match wins, understating amounts by 1000x and bypassing the approval threshold**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/agents/samajh.agent.ts:327` — *wrong-data*
- Impact: For any message that both prefixes and suffixes the amount — "bikri hui rs 2 lakh ki", "50 bag cement aaya rs 85 hazaar ka" — two AMOUNT entities are produced and every consumer uses `.find()`, i.e. the first one, which is the unmultiplied value. A ₹2,00,000 sale is captured as ₹2. The approval gate and the large-amount warning are evaluated against ₹2, so a two-lakh entry silently skips human approval.
- Evidence: samajh.agent.ts:327-348 loops all six patterns and pushes a separate entity for each hit, with no de-duplication by position: `for (const pattern of amountPatterns) { let match; while ((match = pattern.exec(text)) !== null) { ... entities.push({ type: 'AMOUNT', value: match[0], normalizedValue: normalized.toString(), ... }); } }` — and the multiplier test only inspects that pattern's own match (`if (/hazaar|hazar|thousand|k/i.test(match[0]))`, line 333), so the rs-prefixed pattern at line 320 never sees the trailing 'hazaar'/'lakh'. Consumers all take the first: orchestrator.ts:551-553 `const amountEntity = intent.entities.find(e => e.type === 'AMOUNT'); if (!amountEntity) return undefined; 
- Fix: After the pattern loop, collapse overlapping AMOUNT entities by span and keep the widest/highest-confidence one; or run the patterns most-specific-first and skip a match whose [start,end] overlaps an already-recorded AMOUNT. Additionally derive the multiplier from the text following the captured number rather than from match[0] alone.

**10 of the 17 advertised MCP tools are unimplemented, and the failure is returned as HTTP 200 success:true**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/mcp/index.ts:210` — *response-shape*
- Impact: GET /wa/mcp/tools advertises 17 callable tools. Calling any of search_item, create_item, get_stock, create_purchase, create_sale, record_payment, get_outstanding, cash_flow, profit_loss or stock_report returns HTTP 200 with `success: true` at the envelope level while the payload says "not implemented yet". Any caller that checks the HTTP status or the outer `success` flag — the documented contract for every other route in this service — treats a total failure as a success.
- Evidence: mcp/index.ts:194-212 implements only 7 of the 17 definitions and returns a failure object for the rest: `switch (toolName) { case 'validate_gstin': ... case 'parse_amount': ... case 'search_party': ... case 'create_party': ... case 'get_party_outstanding': ... case 'daily_summary': ... case 'gst_summary': ... default: return { success: false, error: `Tool "${toolName}" not implemented yet` }; }` — while MCP_TOOLS (mcp/index.ts:10-189) declares 17 entries including create_purchase (:79), create_sale (:92), record_payment (:104), get_outstanding (:117), cash_flow (:145), profit_loss (:154), stock_report (:163). mcp.routes.ts:59-60 buries the failure inside a success envelope: `const result = a
- Fix: Propagate the tool result: `res.status(result.success ? 200 : 501).json(result)` (or `{ success: result.success, ... }`). Remove unimplemented tools from MCP_TOOLS until they exist, and derive the dashboard's tool list and the /agents/status toolCount from GET /wa/mcp/tools instead of hardcoding 16.

**Two rival MCP registries: the tenant-scoped one (mcp/tools.ts) is dead code, and the live one has no business_id parameter at all**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/mcp/index.ts:10` — *cross-tenant*
- Impact: The MCP tool surface that is actually served has no tenant/business parameter on any tool, so there is nothing in the contract that scopes search_party, get_stock, create_purchase or daily_summary to a business. The moment these stubs are wired to the database, every tool will read and write across all tenants. Meanwhile the version that was written with multi-tenant isolation is never loaded, so any fix applied to it has no effect.
- Evidence: The route loads mcp/index.ts — mcp.routes.ts:6 `import { MCP_TOOLS, MCPToolExecutor } from '../mcp/index';` — and that registry declares no tenant key anywhere: mcp/index.ts:12-20 `{ name: 'search_party', ..., parameters: { query: {...}, type: {...} }, required: ['query'] }`, mcp/index.ts:78-90 `{ name: 'create_purchase', ..., parameters: { vendor_id, items, bill_no, date, gst_rate, is_interstate }, required: ['vendor_id', 'items'] }`. The unused twin does have it: mcp/tools.ts:11-20 `{ name: 'search_party', ..., parameters: { query: {...}, type: {...}, business_id: { type: 'string', description: 'Business ID for multi-tenant isolation' } }, required: ['query', 'business_id'] }` and mcp/tool
- Fix: Delete packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/mcp/tools.ts (or make mcp/index.ts re-export from it) so only one registry exists, and port the business_id requirement onto the live definitions. Then resolve business_id server-side from the authenticated principal / resolved WhatsApp user rather than accepting it from the tool caller, and validate execute bodies with mcpExecuteSchema.

**The read-only dashboard key cannot call any endpoint the dashboard uses — all three live under requireWrite**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/index.ts:116` — *permissions*
- Impact: As soon as API keys are configured (i.e. any non-dev deployment), the key that exists specifically for the dashboard gets 403 on all three of its calls. Combined with the dashboard sending no credential header at all, the page yields 401/403 and — because the errors are only console.error'd — renders as a permanently empty, silently broken screen.
- Evidence: index.ts:116 puts read-only status behind write permission: `app.use('/api/v1/wa/agents', apiRateLimiter, requireWrite, agentRouter);` where requireWrite = apiKeyAuth(['read', 'write']) (auth.ts:187) and the dashboard key is created read-only — auth.ts:47-56 `if (process.env.WHATSAPP_AI_DASHBOARD_KEY) { keys.push({ ..., name: 'dashboard', tenantId: '*', permissions: ['read'], ... }); }`. auth.ts:155-162 then rejects: `const hasPermission = requiredPermissions.every(p => keyRecord.permissions.includes(p)); if (!hasPermission) { ... res.status(403)...`. All three dashboard calls target that router: agent.routes.ts:67 `agentRouter.get('/status', ...)` (a pure constant response), agent.routes.ts
- Fix: Split the router: mount GET /agents/status (and any other read-only introspection) under requireRead and keep /classify and /test under requireWrite. Have the dashboard send credentials via the shared api.ts client, and add a visible error state so a 401/403/404 is surfaced instead of an empty page.

**Service Bus / ORCHESTRATOR_URL dispatch black-holes messages: no consumer and no /process endpoint exist**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/services/dispatcher.ts:81` — *broken-pipeline*
- Impact: Configuring AZURE_SERVICE_BUS_CONNECTION (the documented production topology) or ORCHESTRATOR_URL flips the webhook off the in-process path. The job is enqueued or POSTed, the local orchestrator block is skipped, and nothing anywhere consumes it — so the user's message is acknowledged and then never answered. Enabling the production queue is what breaks it.
- Evidence: dispatcher.ts:81-91 enqueues to a queue nobody reads: `await sender.sendMessages({ body: job, messageId: job.wamid, contentType: 'application/json', subject: 'wa-inbound', ... });` (queue name from config/index.ts:74 `incomingMessages: 'incoming-messages'`), and dispatcher.ts:117 POSTs to a route nobody serves: `await axios.post(`${orchestratorUrl}/process`, job, {...});`. Confirmed no receiver and no route exist anywhere in the monorepo: `grep -rn "createReceiver|subscribe(|receiveMessages" packages/*/src` → no matches; `grep -rn "'/process'" packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src` → no matches. Meanwhile webhook.routes.ts:176-180 makes the in-process fallback conditional on neither being set: `a
- Fix: Either add a Service Bus receiver that pulls 'incoming-messages' and runs AgentOrchestrator.processMessage + WhatsAppClient reply, and a POST /process route for the HTTP mode, or remove the dispatcher branch and always process in-process until the split service exists. Until a consumer exists, dispatcher.dispatch should not suppress the local path — run the orchestrator unconditionally.

**Multi-business picker is unreachable code: nothing handles the select_biz_* reply and selectBusiness() is never called**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/routes/webhook.routes.ts:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: For a user in more than one business, the bot sends a business list and then discards whatever they pick — the selection is never stored, so the picker is re-sent on every message and the user can never get past it. (Today the branch is also unreachable because mockResolution never sets needsBusinessPicker, so multi-business users are instead silently forced into a single fabricated business.)
- Evidence: webhook.routes.ts:145-149 emits rows whose ids carry the business: `rows: resolution.businessOptions.map(b => ({ id: `select_biz_${b.id}`, title: b.name, description: b.role, }))`, then `return;` (line 153). The reply comes back as an interactive list_reply, but the only consumer reads the *title*, not the id — orchestrator.ts:532-534 `case 'interactive': return message.interactive?.button_reply?.title || message.interactive?.list_reply?.title || '';` — and no code anywhere matches the prefix: `grep -rn "select_biz" src/` finds only the producer. The setter that would persist it is never invoked: user-resolution.ts:93-107 `selectBusiness(phone: string, businessId: string): void { ... session
- Fix: Handle the interactive id before intent classification in the webhook pipeline: if `normalised.interactive?.id` starts with 'select_biz_', call `userResolver.selectBusiness(message.from, id.slice('select_biz_'.length))`, confirm to the user, and re-resolve. Route on interactive ids generally (the same gap kills approve_entry/reject_entry/confirm_post/party_* buttons, which are also matched only by

**Conversation tenantId is fabricated from the phone number and never replaced by the resolved business**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/services/memory.service.ts:176` — *cross-tenant*
- Impact: Every tenant-scoping decision in the agent graph is made against the string `tenant_<phone>`, which matches no business in the system. Party search filters, duplicate detection and the transaction records all carry this bogus id, so entries can never be attributed to (or isolated by) a real business — and the Azure Search filter is additionally built by string concatenation of that value.
- Evidence: memory.service.ts:171-177 invents it and flags the gap: `private createDefaultState(userId: string): ConversationState { const phone = userId.replace('whatsapp:', ''); return { userId, tenantId: `tenant_${phone}`, // Will be resolved from database sessionId: ...`. Nothing ever overwrites it — `grep` shows no assignment to `.tenantId` outside this line; the webhook resolves a businessId (webhook.routes.ts:156 `const user = resolution.user!;`) but passes only userId/senderName into the graph (webhook.routes.ts:180-186 `const response = await orchestrator.processMessage({ userId: user.userId, senderName: user.name, message, phoneNumberId, timestamp: normalised.timestamp, });`) and orchestrator.
- Fix: Add businessId to AgentInput and set `conversationState.tenantId` from the resolved ResolvedUser.businessId at the start of processMessage (and persist it), keyed per (userId, businessId) so a business switch cannot inherit the previous tenant's learned mappings. Escape or parameterise the value before interpolating it into the Azure Search filter.


## MEDIUM (80)

### Admin console

**Dashboard 'Recent Users' / 'Recent Businesses' read _count and subscriptions that the list endpoints strip out — every row shows 0 biz / 0 users / plan 'Free'**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminDashboardPage.tsx:220` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: On the admin dashboard every recent user shows '0 biz' and every recent business shows '0 users' and the plan label 'Free' (styled grey) even for Enterprise subscribers, so the at-a-glance panels are actively misleading. Ironically the /admin/dashboard response already contains usable recentSignups/recentBusinesses arrays that the page ignores.
- Evidence: AdminDashboardPage.tsx:88-101 fetches the *list* endpoints: `adminApi.users({ limit: 8, sortBy:'created_at', sortOrder:'desc' })`, `adminApi.businesses({ limit: 8, ... })`, then :220 `<p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">{u._count?.business_users ?? 0} biz</p>`, :250 `const plan = b.subscriptions?.[0]?.plan?.name || 'Free';`, :274 `{b._count?.business_users ?? 0} users`. But listUsers flattens away `_count` — admin.service.ts:89-102 `const data = users.map((u:any) => { ... return { id, name, phone, email, businesses: u._count?.business_users || 0, plan, status, is_super_admin, created_at }; });` (key is `businesses`, and `_count` is not in the returned object). listBusinesses likewis
- Fix: Read the flattened keys the API actually sends (`u.businesses`, `b.users_count`, `b.plan`), or drop the two extra requests and render the `recentSignups` / `recentBusinesses` arrays already present in the dashboard payload.

**Admin pages substitute fabricated demo data whenever the API fails or returns nothing, with no error indication**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminInvoicesPage.tsx:126` — *correctness*
- Impact: A 403/500/timeout — or, on the invoices page, a legitimately empty result — makes the console display invented businesses, invoices, users and revenue as if they were real: 'Sharma Trading Co.', '₹35,013 Revenue Collected', 1250 users, 620 active subscriptions. An admin cannot distinguish a broken backend from a healthy one, and may make decisions on numbers that do not exist. This is reachable in normal operation: after super-admin is revoked, redux still holds the stale is_super_admin so AdminRoute lets the page render (App.tsx:84) while every request 403s.
- Evidence: Invoices swaps in mocks even on success when the list is empty — AdminInvoicesPage.tsx:126-129 `if (res.data?.data?.length) setInvoices(res.data.data); else setInvoices(defaultInvoices); } catch { setInvoices(defaultInvoices); }` with `defaultInvoices` at :53-79 (5 invented invoices, ₹5898.82/₹17698.82/…), feeding the stat tiles at :163-166 `const totalPaid = invoices.filter(i=>i.status==='PAID').reduce((s,i)=> s + toNum(i.total_amount), 0)`. Same pattern elsewhere: AdminUsersPage.tsx:126-130 `if (res.data?.data) setUsers(res.data.data); else setUsers(defaultUsers); } catch { setUsers(defaultUsers); }` (defaultUsers :77-84); AdminBusinessesPage.tsx:138-141 (defaultBusinesses :94-101); AdminP
- Fix: Delete the mock fallbacks; on error set an error state and render a retry/‘couldn’t load’ panel, and on empty use DataTable's emptyMessage. Also make the refresh handler re-read the user (or re-check is_super_admin) so a demoted admin is bounced to AccessDenied instead of shown placeholder data.

**Every admin table is silently capped at the first 20 rows — no page/limit/search params are sent and there are no pagination controls**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminUsersPage.tsx:125` — *pagination*
- Impact: Users, Businesses, Invoices and Audit Logs each load only the newest 20 records and there is no way to reach row 21. Header counts and every stat tile are computed from that slice, so 'N total users', 'Total Businesses', 'Total Invoices', 'N total events', 'Total Revenue' and 'Avg. Revenue/Biz' are all wrong on any real dataset, and the search boxes/status filters only search within the 20 loaded rows — an admin searching for a specific user or invoice number gets 'No users found' for records that exist. The server-side `search`/`isActive`/`type`/`status` filters are never exercised.
- Evidence: Fetches send no params — AdminUsersPage.tsx:125 `const res = await adminApi.users();`, AdminBusinessesPage.tsx:137 `const res = await adminApi.businesses();`, AdminInvoicesPage.tsx:125 `const res = await adminApi.invoices();`, AdminAuditPage.tsx:55 `const res = await adminApi.auditLogs();`. Server defaults to 20 — packages/admin-service/src/shared/utils/helpers.ts:25-32 `parsePagination` → `limit: Math.min(MAX_LIMIT, Math.max(1, Number(query.limit) || DEFAULT_LIMIT))` with packages/admin-service/src/shared/constants/index.ts:28-29 `DEFAULT_LIMIT = 20; MAX_LIMIT = 500;`. `meta` is returned but never consumed: admin.controller.ts:12-15 `res.json({ success:true, ...result })` with `meta: buildP
- Fix: Add page state to each table, pass `{ page, limit, search, status/isActive/type, sortBy, sortOrder }` through adminApi, render pagination controls from the returned `meta` (total/totalPages/hasNext/hasPrev), and source header counts and stat tiles from `meta.total` (or dedicated aggregate endpoints) rather than `array.length`.

**Manual purchase auto-amount ignores the plan's configured quarterly and half-yearly prices**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:165` — *correctness*
- Impact: The Plans page lets an admin set distinct Quarterly and Half-Yearly prices (typically discounted), but the manual-purchase dialog auto-fills monthly × 3 and monthly × 6, silently overcharging. With the seeded Pro plan (₹499/mo, ₹1349/quarter) a quarterly cash sale is pre-filled at ₹1497 instead of ₹1349, and that number is what gets written to the invoice.
- Evidence: AdminSubscriptionsPage.tsx:162-172 `const getCalculatedAmount = (planId, cycle) => { const plan = plans.find(p=>p.id===planId); if(!plan) return 0; switch(cycle){ case 'MONTHLY': return plan.price_monthly; case 'QUARTERLY': return plan.price_monthly * 3; case 'HALF_YEARLY': return plan.price_monthly * 6; case 'YEARLY': return plan.price_yearly || plan.price_monthly * 12; default: return 0; } }` — and the type it works from omits the fields entirely: :22-27 `interface PlanOption { id: string; name: string; price_monthly: number; price_yearly: number; }`. The columns exist and are editable: packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma Plan `price_quarterly Decimal @db.Decimal(10,2)` / `price_half_year
- Fix: Add `price_quarterly` and `price_half_yearly` to PlanOption and return `plan.price_quarterly` / `plan.price_half_yearly` for those two cycles (keeping the ×3/×6 only as a fallback when the field is 0/undefined).

**'Export Users' and 'Export Logs' show a success toast and download nothing**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminUsersPage.tsx:350` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Picking CSV/Excel/JSON/PDF from either export menu pops a green success toast and produces no file. The admin believes an export happened. (The Businesses, Invoices and Subscriptions pages pass `data` and do work, which makes the two broken ones indistinguishable.)
- Evidence: Both render the button with neither `onExport` nor `data` — AdminUsersPage.tsx:350 `<ExportButton label="Export Users" />` and AdminAuditPage.tsx:151 `<ExportButton label="Export Logs" />`. With both props absent, ExportButton falls through to a success toast — frontend/src/components/shared/ExportButton.tsx:47-84 `const handleExport = async (format) => { if (onExport) {...return;} if (data && data.length > 0) {...return;} toast.success(t('export_coming_soon', { format: format.toUpperCase() })); }`. Contrast the working call sites: AdminBusinessesPage.tsx:412-416 `<ExportButton data={filtered} columns={[...]} filename="admin_businesses" />`, AdminInvoicesPage.tsx:324-339, AdminSubscriptionsP
- Fix: Pass the rows: `<ExportButton data={filteredUsers} columns={['name','phone','email','businesses','plan','status','created_at']} filename="admin_users" />` and the equivalent for audit logs; or change ExportButton's no-data branch to `toast.error` so a missing wiring can never look like success.

**Status filter options that can never match: 'Suspended' on Businesses and 'Inactive' on Users**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:370` — *correctness*
- Impact: Selecting Status = Suspended on /admin/businesses always yields 'No data found', because the server can only ever emit ACTIVE or INACTIVE. Symmetrically, Status = Inactive on /admin/users always yields nothing, because listUsers only emits ACTIVE or SUSPENDED. A suspended business is filed under 'Inactive' and a deactivated user under 'Suspended', so an admin filtering for what the row action is literally named ('Suspend') finds none of them.
- Evidence: Businesses can never be SUSPENDED — admin.service.ts:370 `status: !b.is_active ? 'INACTIVE' : (activeSub ? 'ACTIVE' : 'ACTIVE'),` (both branches of the ternary are 'ACTIVE'). Yet the filter offers it: AdminBusinessesPage.tsx:394-398 `<SelectItem value="all">All Status</SelectItem><SelectItem value="ACTIVE">Active</SelectItem><SelectItem value="INACTIVE">Inactive</SelectItem><SelectItem value="SUSPENDED">Suspended</SelectItem>` filtered by :245 `const matchStatus = statusFilter === 'all' || b.status === statusFilter;`. The row action is labelled 'Suspend' (AdminBusinessesPage.tsx:339) and writes `is_active:false` (:219 → admin.service.ts:402-407). Users can never be INACTIVE — admin.service.t
- Fix: Emit one vocabulary consistently — drop the unreachable option from each Select, or introduce a real distinction (e.g. `is_active === false` → 'SUSPENDED' for businesses too) so the filter values match what the transform can produce.

**No guard prevents a super admin from demoting, suspending or deleting themselves — the platform can be locked out of administration**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:132` — *auth-enforcement*
- Impact: The Users table lists the signed-in admin like anyone else, with 'Remove Admin', 'Suspend User' and 'Delete User' in the row menu and no confirmation copy warning it is their own account. If they are the only super admin, executing any of these permanently removes all access to /admin/* for everyone (requireSuperAdmin reads isSuperAdmin from the JWT, so the next 15-minute refresh cuts them off) and there is no in-app path to restore it.
- Evidence: No self/last-admin checks anywhere in the mutators — admin.service.ts:132-137 `async toggleSuperAdmin(userId, isSuperAdmin) { return prisma.user.update({ where:{id:userId}, data:{ is_super_admin: isSuperAdmin } }); }`; :125-130 toggleUserStatus; :139-144 deleteUser. The controllers know the caller's id but never compare it: admin.controller.ts:30-33 `const userId = (req as unknown as AuthenticatedRequest).user?.userId; const user = await adminService.toggleSuperAdmin(req.params.userId, req.body.isSuperAdmin);` — `userId` is used only for the audit entry. listUsers applies no self-exclusion (admin.service.ts:49-57 builds `where` from search/isActive only), and the UI offers the actions uncond
- Fix: In toggleSuperAdmin/toggleUserStatus/deleteUser, reject when `req.params.userId === req.user.userId` (403 'You cannot modify your own admin account'), and additionally block demoting/deleting the last super admin (`prisma.user.count({ where: { is_super_admin: true, is_active: true } }) <= 1`). Disable those menu items for the current user in AdminUsersPage.

**Admin service throws bare Error for its business rules, so guard messages become 'Internal server error' with a 500 in production**
- `packages/admin-service/src/services/admin.service.ts:154` — *error-handling*
- Impact: The three most likely admin mistakes all produce a red 'Internal server error' toast with no explanation, and a 500 instead of 409/400: creating a user whose phone already exists, deleting the primary business, and deleting a plan that still has active subscriptions. The service authors wrote precise, actionable messages that the error handler discards in production.
- Evidence: Bare Errors — admin.service.ts:152-155 `const existing = await prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { phone: data.phone } }); if (existing) { throw new Error('User with this phone already exists'); }`; :412-414 `if (business.is_primary) { throw new Error('Primary business cannot be deleted. It can only be edited.'); }`; :481-483 `if (activeSubCount > 0) { throw new Error(\`Cannot delete plan with ${activeSubCount} active subscriptions. Deactivate them first.\`); }`. The handler only preserves messages for AppError — packages/admin-service/src/shared/utils/errors.ts:66-88 `if (err instanceof AppError) { ... res.status(err.statusCode).json({ success:false, message: err.message ... }); return; } ...
- Fix: Replace the three `new Error(...)` with `new ConflictError(...)` / `new BadRequestError(...)` imported from '../shared', so the intended 409/400 and message reach the toast.

**Plan create/update and feature-flag changes are never audited, and no admin action records an IP address**
- `packages/admin-service/src/controllers/admin.controller.ts:102` — *audit-coverage*
- Impact: Pricing changes — the highest-impact mutation in the console — leave no trace in Audit Logs, so an unexplained price change cannot be attributed. Feature-flag toggles are likewise unlogged. And because no call site passes ipAddress/userAgent, the Audit Logs page's IP column is empty for every admin event even once its field mapping is fixed.
- Evidence: createPlan/updatePlan write with no audit call — admin.controller.ts:102-110 `export const createPlan = asyncHandler(async (req,res) => { const plan = await adminService.createPlan(req.body); res.status(201).json({...}); }); export const updatePlan = asyncHandler(async (req,res) => { const plan = await adminService.updatePlan(req.params.planId, req.body); res.json({ success:true, data: plan }); });` — contrast deletePlan at :112-117 which does call `createAuditLog`. Same gap for feature flags: :143-151 toggleFeatureFlag / createFeatureFlag contain no createAuditLog. No caller supplies request metadata — every invocation in admin.controller.ts (lines 25, 32, 39, 47, 55, 63-67, 85, 92, 115, 16
- Fix: Add `createAuditLog({ userId, action:'ADMIN_CREATE_PLAN'|'ADMIN_UPDATE_PLAN', entityType:'plan', entityId: plan.id, oldData, newData: req.body })` to createPlan/updatePlan (updatePlan should capture the pre-image, which admin.service.ts:451 already fetches) and to the feature-flag handlers; and pass `ipAddress: req.ip, userAgent: req.get('user-agent')` at every createAuditLog call site.

**Dashboard 'Recent Activity' panel is permanently fabricated — the setter is never called**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminDashboardPage.tsx:76` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: The Recent Activity card on /admin always lists the same six invented events ('New user registered — Rahul Verma · 2 min ago', 'Payment received ₹999 — Amit Patel · 1 hr ago', 'Account deactivated — Manoj Singh'), presented with live-looking relative timestamps next to genuinely fetched panels. An admin has no cue that this feed is fiction, and the real feed (audit logs) is one endpoint away.
- Evidence: AdminDashboardPage.tsx:76 `const [activity, _setActivity] = useState<RecentActivity[]>(defaultActivity);` — the setter is named with a leading underscore and appears nowhere else in the file; fetchDashboard (:85-107) only calls setStats/setRecentUsers/setRecentBusinesses. `defaultActivity` is the hardcoded mock at :64-71. It is rendered unconditionally at :300-311 `{activity.map((item) => ( ... <p className="text-sm font-medium truncate">{item.description}</p><p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">{item.user}</p><span ...>{item.timestamp}</span> ...))}`. A real source exists and is already wired into adminApi: frontend/src/lib/api.ts:503 `auditLogs: (params?: any) => api.get('/admin/au
- Fix: Fetch `adminApi.auditLogs({ limit: 6 })` in fetchDashboard and map real rows (action → description/icon, user.name, created_at) into the panel, or remove the card until it is backed by data.

### Auth & session

**Any transient failure of /auth/me or /business on startup silently redirects the user to /business/new**
- `frontend/src/App.tsx:118` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: On page load, if either /auth/me or /business fails (502 from the gateway, service cold-start, dropped Wi-Fi, 500), the catch keeps the session but `businesses` stays at its initial empty array — it is not persisted anywhere — so ProtectedRoute immediately redirects an existing user with several businesses to the "Create your business" wizard. No error is shown; it looks like their data was deleted.
- Evidence: frontend/src/App.tsx:118-138 — Promise.all, and on failure nothing repopulates businesses and no error surfaces: const [userRes, bizRes] = await Promise.all([ authApi.me(), businessApi.list() ]); ... if (bizRes.data?.data) dispatch(setBusinesses(bizRes.data.data)); } catch { if (!store.getState().auth.isAuthenticated) { dispatch(logout()); } } finally { dispatch(setLoading(false)); } businesses is never restored from localStorage — frontend/src/store/businessSlice.ts:6-10: const initialState: BusinessState = { currentBusiness: null, businesses: [], loading: false }; and the empty list is what drives the redirect — frontend/src/App.tsx:70: if (!allowNoBusiness && businesses.length === 0) retu
- Fix: Track a distinct `businessesLoaded` / fetch-error flag and only apply the no-business redirect when the list was actually fetched successfully; on error show a retry state (and/or persist the last known business list like bk_user is persisted).

**Only one refresh token can exist per user — logging in anywhere silently kills every other session**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:522` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: The user logs in on their phone (or a second browser). The desktop session's refresh token row has been deleted, so at the next silent refresh the desktop gets "Invalid or expired refresh token", the interceptor dispatches logout and hard-redirects to /login, losing any unsaved form. The same happens on every re-login in the same browser and defeats the cross-tab sync code in api.ts.
- Evidence: packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:516-532 — every token issue wipes all of the user's tokens: private async generateRefreshToken(userId: string): Promise<string> { const jti = uuidv4(); ... // Remove any stale tokens for this user before creating a new one await prisma.refreshToken.deleteMany({ where: { user_id: userId } }); await prisma.refreshToken.create({ data: { id: jti, user_id: userId, token, expires_at: expiresAt } }); The schema supports many tokens per user (packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:84-96, `user_id String` + `@@index([user_id])`, not unique), and the frontend is explicitly written for multi-tab/multi-session use (frontend/src/lib/api.ts:82-107 cross-tab
- Fix: Delete only expired rows (`expires_at: { lt: new Date() }`) instead of all rows for the user, so rotation stays single-use per token while multiple devices keep independent sessions.

**ProtectedRoute's "no subscription yet" gate is dead code — trialInfo is never null, and no backend route enforces a subscription**
- `frontend/src/App.tsx:72` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: A user who creates a business but never picks a plan is supposed to be sent to /subscription?setup=true. That never happens: they land on the dashboard and can use every feature (purchases, sales, ledger, reports) with no subscription, because the backend gate is not mounted anywhere either. The paywall is effectively unenforced.
- Evidence: frontend/src/App.tsx:72 depends on a strict null: if (!allowWhenExpired && businesses.length > 0 && trialInfo === null) return <Navigate to="/subscription?setup=true" replace />; but every auth response returns a non-null trial object, so setTrialInfo is never given null. packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:80-83 (no subscription case) returns an object, and it is always wrapped: auth.service.ts:235-240 `trial: { expired: trialInfo.trialExpired, endsAt: ..., daysRemaining: ..., planName: ... }`; the /me controller does the same (packages/auth-service/src/controllers/auth.controller.ts:79-81 `let trial: {...} = { expired: false, endsAt: null, daysRemaining: null, planName: null
- Fix: Have the auth responses return `trial: null` when the business has no subscription row (or add an explicit `hasSubscription: boolean` and branch on that), and mount requireActiveSubscription on the mutating routes of the domain services so the gate is enforced server-side.

**login / verify-otp / register omit maxBusinesses from the trial payload, so the business cap silently drops to 1**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:235` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: Immediately after signing in (and permanently if the follow-up /auth/me call fails, since the truncated object is persisted to localStorage as bk_trial), a user on a 5-business plan sees "Add Business" disabled and the message "Your plan allows up to 1 business" on /business — they cannot create the businesses they paid for until a successful /auth/me overwrites the value.
- Evidence: The login/OTP/register responses build trial without maxBusinesses — packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:235-240 (identical at 342-347 for register and 440-445 for loginWithEmail): trial: { expired: trialInfo.trialExpired, endsAt: trialInfo.trialEndsAt, daysRemaining: trialInfo.daysRemaining, planName: trialInfo.planName, }, (getTrialInfo's return type, auth.service.ts:27-32, has no maxBusinesses field at all), while /auth/me does include it — packages/auth-service/src/controllers/auth.controller.ts:100-102 `maxBusinesses: (activeSub as any).plan?.max_businesses ?? 1`. The frontend types it as required and consumes it — frontend/src/types/index.ts:335-341 `export interface Tri
- Fix: Add maxBusinesses (from plan.max_businesses, default 1) to getTrialInfo's return and to all three trial payloads in auth.service.ts so every auth response matches the TrialInfo contract used by /auth/me.

### Business management

**Bank/credit-card list failures are swallowed, so the missing business_credit_cards table renders as a friendly "No credit cards added yet" empty state**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessCreditCardSection.tsx:291` — *error-handling*
- Impact: Answering the specific question: the page does not blank or crash — it silently lies. The HTTP 500 from GET :id/credit-cards is caught and discarded, loading resolves, and the user sees the empty state plus an "Add Your First Card" button; clicking it fires POST and only then produces a toast ("Internal server error" in prod, the raw Postgres 'table does not exist' text in dev). Existing cards would likewise appear deleted during any outage. The bank section behaves identically.
- Evidence: BusinessCreditCardSection.tsx:285-293 `const fetchCards = useCallback(async () => { if (!currentBusiness) return; try { setLoading(true); const { data } = await businessApi.listCreditCards(currentBusiness.id); setCards(data?.data || []); } catch { /* ignore */ } finally { setLoading(false); } }, [currentBusiness]);` — the empty catch leaves `cards` at `[]`, which renders :461-468 `cards.length === 0 ? ... <p>No credit cards added yet</p> ... <Button ...>Add Your First Card</Button>`. Identical swallow in BusinessBankSection.tsx:206-214 `catch { /* ignore */ }`. The write path is the only one that surfaces anything: BusinessCreditCardSection.tsx:118 `toast.error(err.response?.data?.message ||
- Fix: Track an error state in both fetchers (`catch (e) { setLoadError(true) }`) and render a retry/error panel distinct from the genuine empty state, so a 500 is never presented as "you have no cards".

**BusinessListPage injects three hard-coded fake businesses into redux on any list error, poisoning x-business-id for the whole session**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessListPage.tsx:66` — *mock-fallback*
- Impact: One failed GET /business (offline blip, 502, expired token race) makes the app display 'Sharma Trading Co.', 'Gupta Mandi Commission' and 'Krishna Retail Mart' as if they were the user's businesses. Because setBusinesses auto-selects the first entry, every subsequent request sends x-business-id: '1', so the dashboard, ledger, parties and every write either 403 or 500 — and 'bk_business_id' = '1' is persisted to localStorage the moment the user clicks Switch, so the breakage survives reloads.
- Evidence: BusinessListPage.tsx:65-68 `} catch { if (businesses.length === 0) { dispatch(setBusinesses(defaultBusinesses)); } }` with the fixtures declared at :34-38 `const defaultBusinesses: Business[] = [ { id: '1', name: 'Sharma Trading Co.', ... }, { id: '2', ... }, { id: '3', ... } ];`. businessSlice.ts:19-22 then promotes one to current: `if (!state.currentBusiness && action.payload.length > 0) { const saved = action.payload.find(b => b.id === savedBizId); state.currentBusiness = saved || action.payload[0]; }`, and api.ts:113/127-129 turns that into a header on every request: `const businessId = state.business.currentBusiness?.id; ... config.headers['x-business-id'] = businessId;`. Clicking "Swit
- Fix: Delete the defaultBusinesses fallback (or gate it behind `import.meta.env.DEV && token.startsWith('dev-mock-')`, which is the convention api.ts:117 already uses) and render an error/retry state on failure instead of fabricated tenants.

**Credit-card edit cannot clear Card Holder, Billing Date, Due Date or Credit Limit**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessCreditCardSection.tsx:102` — *correctness*
- Impact: Clearing any of those four fields in the Edit Credit Card dialog and pressing Update shows "Credit card updated" while the old value stays in the database and reappears in the card list — e.g. a wrong ₹5,00,000 credit limit can never be removed, so the utilization bar keeps computing against it.
- Evidence: Empty inputs are converted to `undefined`, which JSON.stringify drops from the request entirely: BusinessCreditCardSection.tsx:102-105 `cardHolder: form.cardHolder || undefined, billingDate: form.billingDate ? parseInt(form.billingDate) : undefined, dueDate: form.dueDate ? parseInt(form.dueDate) : undefined, creditLimit: form.creditLimit ? parseFloat(form.creditLimit) : undefined,` sent via :109 `businessApi.updateCreditCard(currentBusiness!.id, editCard.id, payload)`. The service only writes keys that are present: business.service.ts:672-675 `...(data.cardHolder !== undefined ? { card_holder: data.cardHolder || null } : {}), ...(data.billingDate !== undefined ? { billing_date: data.billingD
- Fix: Send explicit `null` for cleared optional fields (`cardHolder: form.cardHolder.trim() || null`, etc.) and accept null in the service (`data.cardHolder !== undefined ? { card_holder: data.cardHolder ?? null } : {}` already handles it once null is transmitted).

**Create wizard has no GSTIN/pincode client validation and shows only a bare "Validation failed" when the server rejects**
- `frontend/src/components/business/BusinessCreatePage.tsx:345` — *validation*
- Impact: A typo in the GSTIN (or a pincode beginning with 0, or a phone not starting 6-9) makes business creation fail with a toast that says only "Validation failed" — no field is highlighted and the message never says which input is wrong, so the user is stuck on the last step of onboarding with no way to find the problem. The field-level detail the server sends is discarded.
- Evidence: The form validates only two fields — BusinessCreatePage.tsx:295-299 `const ok = validate({ name: [!form.name.trim(), t('business:name_required')], type: [!form.type, t('business:type_required')] }); if (!ok) return;` — while GSTIN input does nothing but uppercase and cap length (:481 `onChange={(e) => updateField('gst_number', e.target.value.toUpperCase())} maxLength={15}`) even though the server enforces validators/index.ts:39 `gstNumber: z.string().regex(/^[0-9]{2}[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1}[1-9A-Z]{1}Z[0-9A-Z]{1}$/).optional()` (plus :44 `pincode: z.string().regex(/^[1-9][0-9]{5}$/)` and :8 phone `/^[6-9]\d{9}$/`, while the pincode input only strips non-digits at :614). validate.middleware.t
- Fix: Mirror the three regexes client-side via the existing useFormErrors hook so the offending Input gets `error=...`, and map `err.response.data.errors` back onto field errors as a fallback (the array already carries `field`).

### Dashboard & Reports

**Low-stock alert drill-down ignores ?filter=low-stock — InventoryPage never reads search params**
- `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:403` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Clicking the amber "N items low on stock" alert on the dashboard opens /inventory showing the full item list on the 'all' tab. The user has to find and click the Low Stock tab manually; the drill-down conveys nothing. Same for the red pending-payments alert -> /ledger?filter=outstanding.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:403 — `onClick={() => navigate('/inventory?filter=low-stock')}` and DashboardPage.tsx:419 — `onClick={() => navigate('/ledger?filter=outstanding')}`. InventoryPage has the matching tab but no URL reading — frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryPage.tsx:24-25: `const [tab, setTab] = useState('all');` `const [filters, setFilters] = useState<Record<string, any>>({});` InventoryPage.tsx:145 — `<TabsTrigger value="low-stock">{t('inventory:tab_low_stock')} ({lowStockItems.length})</TabsTrigger>` and :63 `const displayItems = tab === 'low-stock' ? lowStockItems : items;`. `grep -n "useSearchParams|pathname" frontend/src/components/inventory/I
- Fix: In InventoryPage: `const [searchParams] = useSearchParams(); const [tab, setTab] = useState(searchParams.get('filter') === 'low-stock' ? 'low-stock' : 'all');`. In LedgerPage, add `if (searchParams.get('filter') === 'outstanding') setTab('outstanding')` to the initial state (see the previous finding's fix — one URL->tab mapping covers both).

**Dashboard stat cards display hard-coded fake trend percentages**
- `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:318` — *data-bug*
- Impact: The Purchases card always shows a red "↓ 12% from last period" and the Sales card always shows a green "↑ 8% from last period", regardless of the selected period or the actual data — including for a business with zero transactions. The Reports P&L Net Profit card does the same (±12.5%/8.2%). Users read these as real KPIs.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:314-327: `<StatCard title={...} value={formatCurrency(stats.purchaseToday)} icon={ShoppingCart} trend={{ value: 12, isPositive: false }} iconColor="text-blue-400" />` `<StatCard title={...} value={formatCurrency(stats.salesToday)} icon={TrendingUp} trend={{ value: 8, isPositive: true }} iconColor="text-emerald-400" />` frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:428 — `trend={{ value: netProfit >= 0 ? 12.5 : -8.2, isPositive: netProfit >= 0 }}` And the card renders it as a literal period-over-period claim — frontend/src/components/shared/stat-card.tsx:29-32: `{trend && ( <p className={cn('text-xs mt-1', trend.isPositive ? 'text-emerald-
- Fix: Either drop the `trend` prop from these four StatCards until the backend returns comparison data, or extend business.service.ts getDashboard to also aggregate the immediately-preceding equal-length window (periodStart - diffDays .. periodStart) and return e.g. `purchaseTrendPct` / `salesTrendPct`, then feed those into `trend`.

**Reports Outstanding tab fabricates the 'Overdue' and 'Last Txn' columns**
- `frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:186` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On Reports -> Outstanding, every row shows "0 days" overdue and today's date as the last transaction, for every party, always. The red/amber ageing highlight (>20 days / >10 days) is unreachable. The exported CSV carries the same fabricated values, so an ageing analysis built from this report is wrong.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:178-189: `const today = new Date().toISOString(); const rows: OutstandingRow[] = od.parties .filter((p: any) => Number(p.balance) !== 0) .map((p: any) => ({ id: p.id, party_name: p.name, phone: p.phone, type: Number(p.balance) > 0 ? 'RECEIVABLE' : 'PAYABLE', amount: Math.abs(Number(p.balance)), days_overdue: 0, last_transaction: today, }));` Rendered as real data — ReportsPage.tsx:299-304: `{ key: 'days_overdue', header: t('reports:overdue'), render: (o) => (<span className={...o.days_overdue > 20 ? 'text-red-400...' : o.days_overdue > 10 ? 'text-amber-400' : ...}>{t('reports:days', { count: o.days_overdue })}</span>)}, { key: 'last_transaction
- Fix: Have getOutstandingSummary return real ageing: for each party include the max entry_date from ledger_entries (`last_transaction`) and derive `days_overdue` from it (or from the oldest unpaid purchase/sale due date), then drop the client-side placeholders at ReportsPage.tsx:187-188. Until then, remove those two columns rather than showing invented values.

**Export button does nothing on the Profit & Loss and Balance Sheet tabs (and PDF never works on any tab)**
- `frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:307` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On Reports -> Profit & Loss or Balance Sheet, opening Export and choosing CSV / Excel / JSON downloads nothing — the user just gets a green success-styled toast reading "export coming soon" and no file. The PDF option shows a 'coming soon' toast on all five tabs while still being presented as a normal menu item.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:307-314 returns an empty array for two of the five tabs: `const getExportData = () => { switch (tab) { case 'daybook': return dayBook; case 'trial-balance': return trialBalance; case 'outstanding': return filteredOutstanding; default: return []; } };` wired at ReportsPage.tsx:328 — `<ExportButton data={getExportData()} filename={`report_${tab}`} />` (no `onExport` prop is passed). The component then falls through to a bare toast — frontend/src/components/shared/ExportButton.tsx:63 `if (data && data.length > 0) {` … :84 `toast.success(t('export_coming_soon', { format: format.toUpperCase() }));`, and for PDF even with data, ExportButton.tsx:76 `t
- Fix: Add cases for the two tabs, e.g. `case 'profit-loss': return [...pl.income.flatMap(c => c.items.map(i => ({ section: 'Income', category: c.category, name: i.name, amount: i.amount }))), ...pl.expenses.flatMap(...)]` and `case 'balance-sheet': return [...balanceSheet.assets.currentAssets.map(i => ({ section: 'Assets', ...i })), ...balanceSheet.liabilities.currentLiabilities.map(...), ...balanceShee

**Balance Sheet 'Balance Check' banner is always green because the service back-plugs the difference into equity**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:361` — *data-bug*
- Impact: The Reports -> Balance Sheet reconciliation banner is mathematically guaranteed to read "Balanced", so it can never warn the user that their books do not tie out. Any real imbalance (double-entry gaps, missing opening balances, the mixed as-on-date issue below) is silently absorbed into an equity line called "Capital / Opening Balance", inflating or deflating Owner's Equity by exactly the error amount.
- Evidence: packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:361-373: `const diff = assets.totalCurrentAssets - liabilities.totalCurrentLiabilities - equity.totalEquity; if (Math.abs(diff) > 0.01) { equity.items.push({ name: 'Capital / Opening Balance', amount: diff }); equity.totalEquity += diff; } return { asOnDate, assets, liabilities, equity, totalAssets: assets.totalCurrentAssets, totalLiabilitiesAndEquity: liabilities.totalCurrentLiabilities + equity.totalEquity };` After the plug, totalLiabilitiesAndEquity === totalCurrentLiabilities + (equity + diff) === totalCurrentAssets === totalAssets, so the frontend's amber branch is unreachable — frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:638-6
- Fix: Return the plug separately instead of folding it into equity, e.g. add `unreconciledDifference: diff` to the response and keep `totalLiabilitiesAndEquity` computed without it; then render the amber "diff" state from that field. If a genuine opening-capital figure is wanted it should come from a real CAPITAL account type, not from the residual.

**Balance Sheet mixes as-on-date ledger balances with live inventory and party balances**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:291` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Setting the Reports 'To' date to any past date produces a nonsense balance sheet: Cash/Bank and Retained Earnings are computed as of that date, but Inventory (Stock in Hand), Accounts Receivable and Accounts Payable are always today's live values. Every historical balance sheet is therefore wrong, and the error is hidden by the equity plug described above.
- Evidence: The date-scoped part — packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:274-283: `SELECT account_type, ... FROM ledger_entries WHERE business_id = ${businessId} AND entry_date <= ${asOnDate} GROUP BY account_type`. The un-scoped parts, packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:291-304: `const lots = await prisma.lot.findMany({ where: { business_id: businessId, status: { in: ['AVAILABLE', 'PARTIAL'] } }, select: { available_qty: true, purchase_rate: true }, });` `const parties = await prisma.party.findMany({ where: { business_id: businessId, is_active: true, balance: { not: 0 } }, select: { name: true, balance: true, type: true }, });` — neither has any asOnDate condition, 
- Fix: Derive receivables/payables as of asOnDate from ledger_entries (sum PARTY_RECEIVABLE / PARTY_PAYABLE debits-credits per party_id up to asOnDate) rather than the mutable Party.balance column, and value inventory from LotHistory (`quantity_change` summed up to asOnDate, joined to purchase_rate) rather than Lot.available_qty. If that is out of scope, force asOnDate to 'now' and remove the picker's ef

**Dashboard's empty-state trap: a business with no activity in the selected period shows the onboarding screen with no way to change the period**
- `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:113` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: The default period is 'today'. A business whose purchases/sales are all settled (zero outstanding) and that has had no transaction today gets `total === 0`, so the page early-returns the "let's get you started" onboarding screen — which does not render the period preset bar. There is no control to switch to Last Month/Last Year, so an established business is locked out of its own dashboard until it records a transaction. The same early return also hides the low-stock / pending-payment alerts and the cutters widget even when those counts are non-zero.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:109-113: `const total = (d.purchaseToday?.total ?? d.purchaseToday ?? 0) + (d.salesToday?.total ?? d.salesToday ?? 0) + (d.outstandingReceivable ?? 0) + (d.outstandingPayable ?? 0); setIsEmpty(total === 0);` — note `lowStockAlerts` and `partialPaymentAlerts` are deliberately excluded from `total`. The early return, DashboardPage.tsx:146-200: `if (isEmpty) { return (<div className="space-y-6"> …onboarding… </div>); }` — the block contains only the title, the trial banner and three navigate buttons; the `PRESETS.map(...)` preset bar (DashboardPage.tsx:227-240) and the custom From/To inputs live only in the non-empty branch. The backend period
- Fix: Render the header + period preset bar above the `isEmpty` branch (move the early return to only replace the stats/charts region), and/or gate the onboarding screen on a business-level signal (e.g. add `hasAnyTransactions` to the dashboard response computed without a date filter) instead of on the selected period's totals.

**Reports page swallows every API error — STAFF users (no REPORT_VIEW/LEDGER_VIEW) see five silently blank tabs**
- `frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:217` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: Reports is in the sidebar for every role with no gating, but a STAFF-role member has neither REPORT_VIEW nor LEDGER_VIEW, so all five report requests return 403. The catch block resets every state to empty and shows nothing — no toast, no 'no permission' message. The user sees ₹0 totals and empty tables and reasonably concludes the business has no data. The same silence hides genuine 500s/502s.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/reports/ReportsPage.tsx:217-226: `} catch { setDayBook([]); setTrialBalance([]); setPL(defaultPL); setOutstanding([]); setOutstandingTotals({ receivable: 0, payable: 0 }); setBalanceSheet(defaultBalanceSheet); } finally { setLoading(false); }` — no `toast.error`, no error state (contrast: the file does not import react-hot-toast at all). Every report route requires a permission STAFF lacks — packages/ledger-service/src/routes/ledger.routes.ts:17-21: `router.get('/trial-balance', requirePermission('REPORT_VIEW'), ctrl.getTrialBalance); router.get('/profit-loss', requirePermission('REPORT_VIEW'), ctrl.getProfitAndLoss); router.get('/balance-sheet', requirePermission('RE
- Fix: Capture the error in ReportsPage.fetchReport (`catch (err: any) { ... setError(err?.response?.status === 403 ? t('common:no_permission') : t('common:load_failed')); toast.error(...) }`) and render that message in place of the empty tables. Additionally gate the /reports sidebar item (and the route) on the user's permission set so STAFF members are not sent to a page that cannot load.

### Expenses & Bills

**Filter "All" sentinel __all__ is not stripped for expense_type_id/category — Category=All returns a 500 and blanks the page**
- `packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:89` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: In Advanced Filters, picking "All" in the Expense Type dropdown makes the list go empty (queries expense_type_id = '__all__').
