Picking "All" in the Category dropdown sends '__all__' into a Prisma enum column, which throws PrismaClientValidationError -> controller returns 500 -> page shows the fetch_error toast and an empty table with no way to recover except Reset All.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/AdvancedFilters.tsx:244 every select renders `<SelectItem value="__all__">{t('common:all')}</SelectItem>` and :239 `onValueChange={(val) => updateValue(field.key, val)}`; ExpensesPage.tsx:44 spreads them into the query: `expenseApi.listAll({ search, type: typeFilter, ...filters })`. packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:89-91 `if (expenseTypeId) where.expense_type_id = expenseTypeId;` / `if (filters.category) where.expense_category = filters.category;` / `if (isPaidFilter && isPaidFilter !== '__all__') where.is_paid = isPaidFilter === 'true';` — only is_paid guards the sentinel. Same unguarded lines are repeated for purchases (:119-120) and s
- Fix: Normalize the sentinel once at the top of listAllExpenses (e.g. treat '__all__' and '' as undefined for expenseTypeId, category, isPaid, type) as purchase-service already does at purchase.service.ts:575 `if (paymentStatus && paymentStatus !== '__all__')`.

**Search box does not filter purchase- or sale-linked expenses**
- `packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:118` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Typing anything in the Expenses search box narrows only standalone rows; every purchase-linked and sale-linked expense in the business keeps coming back and is then interleaved into the results, so the "filtered" list still shows unrelated rows. On the Purchase or Sale tab the search box does nothing at all.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/expenses/ExpensesPage.tsx:44 `const { data } = await expenseApi.listAll({ search, type: typeFilter, ...filters });` packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:92-97 standalone branch does apply it: `if (filters.search) { where.OR = [ { notes: { contains: filters.search, mode: 'insensitive' } }, { expense_type: { name: { contains: filters.search, ... } } } ]; }` :118-121 purchase branch: `const where: any = {}; if (expenseTypeId) ...; if (filters.category) ...; if (isPaidFilter ...) ...;` — filters.search never referenced. :163-165 sale branch: `const where: any = {}; if (expenseTypeId) ...; if (filters.category) ...;` — same omission.
- Fix: Add a search OR-clause to the purchase and sale where-objects (expense_type.name / notes / purchase.purchase_number / purchase.party.name and the sale equivalents).

**Amount-range filter is completely ignored by the backend**
- `packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:47` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: Entering Min/Max in the "Amount Range" filter changes nothing — the full unfiltered list comes back while the Filters badge counts the filter as active, so the user believes an amount range is applied.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/expenses/ExpensesPage.tsx:105 `{ key: 'amount', label: t('expenses:amount_range'), type: 'number-range' },` and frontend/src/components/shared/AdvancedFilters.tsx:277-292 emit `amount_min` / `amount_max` (`updateValue(`${field.key}_min`, e.target.value)`), which ExpensesPage.tsx:44 spreads into the query string. packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:47-61 the filters signature is `{ page, limit, startDate, endDate, date_from, date_to, expenseTypeId, expense_type_id, category, isPaid, is_paid, search, type }` — no amount keys, and `amount` never appears in any where-clause in the file. The sibling services do implement it, e.g. packages/billing-servi
- Fix: Read amount_min/amount_max in listAllExpenses and add `where.amount = { gte, lte }` to all three source branches.

**Payment-status filter is not applied to sale expenses, which are also hardcoded as Paid**
- `packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:163` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Filtering Payment Status = Unpaid still returns every sale-linked expense, and each is rendered with a green "Paid" badge regardless of reality. The Unpaid view therefore shows rows that contradict the filter, and no sale expense can ever appear as unpaid.
- Evidence: packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:163-165 sale branch where-object omits the is_paid clause that the purchase branch has at :121 `if (isPaidFilter && isPaidFilter !== '__all__') where.is_paid = isPaidFilter === 'true';` :191 `is_paid: true, // Sale expenses don't have is_paid yet` packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:803-818 `model SaleExpense { id sale_id expense_type_id expense_category amount notes created_at updated_at ... }` — there is genuinely no is_paid column (PurchaseExpense has one at :538). Rendered by frontend/src/components/expenses/ExpensesPage.tsx:189-200 `e.is_paid ? <Badge ...>{t('expenses:paid')}</Badge> : <Badge ...>{t('expenses:unpaid')}</Badge>`.
- Fix: Add is_paid to SaleExpense in the schema (and both vendored copies) and filter on it, or exclude sale expenses from the result set whenever an is_paid filter is supplied and render their status as "—" instead of "Paid".

**Expense list silently truncates to 20 rows: per-source skip/take then slice(0, limit), bogus meta, and no pagination UI**
- `packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:209` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: A business with more than 20 expenses can only ever see 20 of them — the page never sends a page param and renders no pagination control, and on the "All" tab up to 40 already-fetched rows are thrown away by slice(). The tab counters ("All (20)", "Purchase (n)") are computed from that truncated array, and meta.total is the post-slice count so it can never be used to build pagination either.
- Evidence: packages/expense-service/src/services/expense.service.ts:99-105 / :123-139 / :167-183 each run `skip, take: limit` independently, then :206 `expenses.sort(...)`, then :209 `const totalCount = expenses.length; // Simplified - in production, count separately` and :211-220 `return { data: expenses.slice(0, limit), meta: { total: totalCount, page, limit, totalPages: Math.ceil(totalCount / limit), ... } };` frontend/src/components/expenses/ExpensesPage.tsx:44-45 `const { data } = await expenseApi.listAll({ search, type: typeFilter, ...filters }); setExpenses(data?.data || []);` — no page/limit ever sent, meta ignored. :242-244 `const standaloneCount = expenses.filter(e => e.source === 'standalone
- Fix: Count each source with a separate prisma.count, return a real total in meta, and either add pagination controls to ExpensesPage or pass an explicit large limit; do not slice away rows that were already paid for.

**Payment is created and party balances moved even when the referenced bill is not found — user gets a success toast for a no-op**
- `packages/billing-service/src/services/billing.service.ts:150` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: If the purchase/sale row is missing (deleted, cancelled, or an id from another tenant that the lookup can't see), the transaction still commits the Payment row and still adjusts the party balance, and the API returns 201 "Payment recorded". BillsPage shows "Payment recorded successfully", refetches, and the bill's outstanding amount is unchanged — the money is now recorded against nothing and the party balance is wrong.
- Evidence: packages/billing-service/src/services/billing.service.ts:132-147 creates the payment first, then :150-152 `if (data.referenceType === 'PURCHASE') { const purchase = await tx.purchase.findUnique({ where: { id: data.referenceId } }); if (purchase) {` … and :198-200 `} else if (data.referenceType === 'SALE') { const sale = await tx.sale.findUnique({ where: { id: data.referenceId } }); if (sale) {` — both `if` blocks simply fall through with no else/throw; :247-258 then updates party.balance unconditionally. packages/billing-service/src/controllers/billing.controller.ts:82 `res.status(201).json({ success: true, message: 'Payment recorded', data: payment });` frontend/src/components/bills/BillsPa
- Fix: Throw NotFoundError when the scoped purchase/sale lookup returns nothing, so the transaction rolls back and the dialog surfaces a real error.

**BillsPage hard-caps at 200 purchases + 200 sales and computes Receivable/Payable from that slice**
- `frontend/src/components/bills/BillsPage.tsx:76` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Any business with more than 200 active purchases or 200 active sales sees a silently incomplete bill list (older bills simply vanish, with no pagination control on the page) and, worse, the "Receivable" and "Payable" money totals in the header are sums over only the first 200 rows of each — understated with no warning. The Total/Paid/Partial/Unpaid counters are likewise capped.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/bills/BillsPage.tsx:76-79 `const [purchasesRes, salesRes] = await Promise.allSettled([ purchaseApi.list({ limit: 200, payment_status: ... }), salesApi.list({ limit: 200, payment_status: ... }) ]);` :128-130 `const totalReceivable = saleBills.reduce((s, b) => s + b.balance_amount, 0); const totalPayable = purchaseBills.reduce((s, b) => s + b.balance_amount, 0); setStats({ total: allBills.length, paid, partial, unpaid, totalReceivable, totalPayable });` The backends honour that limit verbatim with no cap and return real totals the page ignores: packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:548-550 `const limit = Number(filters.limit) || 20;` and :605-615 `r
- Fix: Compute receivable/payable server-side (an aggregate endpoint) or page through with meta.total, and add pagination/infinite scroll instead of a magic 200.

### Inventory

**API failures are swallowed and replaced with hardcoded fake inventory (Wheat/Rice/Mustard Oil) the user can act on**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryPage.tsx:37` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: If the gateway is down, the token/x-business-id is rejected (403 from requirePermission for a STAFF user without INVENTORY_VIEW), or the query 500s, the user is shown a fully populated inventory of five products that do not exist, with no error message. On /inventory/adjust the same fallback offers items with ids '1','2','3'; submitting an adjustment against them sends a bogus itemId that (per the itemId/item_id bug) mutates a real, unrelated item. The detail page fallback likewise shows demo lots and transactions for any item that fails to load.
- Evidence: InventoryPage.tsx:37-44: `} catch {\n setItems([\n { id: '1', name: 'Wheat Grade A', sku: 'WHT-A', unit: 'Quintal', current_stock: 150, min_stock: 50, category: { id: '1', name: 'Grains' } }, ... ] as InventoryItem[]);` ; StockAdjustPage.tsx:34-40: `} catch {\n setItems([{ id: '1', name: 'Wheat Grade A', ... }] as InventoryItem[]);` ; InventoryDetailPage.tsx:83-87: `} catch {\n setItem(defaultItem);\n setLots(defaultLots);\n setTransactions(defaultTransactions);`. None of the three catch blocks surface an error to the user.
- Fix: Remove the mock fallbacks; on failure show an error state / toast with the server message and an empty list, so a 403/500 is visible instead of being masked by fabricated stock.

**Bulk "Delete selected" makes no API call — it only shows a success toast**
- `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryPage.tsx:184` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On /inventory the user ticks several rows, opens the bulk-action menu and clicks "Delete selected". A toast reports "N deleted" and the table refetches, so nothing is deleted and the rows reappear — the user believes data was removed when it never was.
- Evidence: InventoryPage.tsx:184-192: `{ label: t('common:delete_selected'), icon: <Trash2 className="h-3.5 w-3.5" />, variant: 'destructive', onClick: (items) => { toast.success(t('inventory:deleted_count', { count: items.length })); fetchItems(); } }` — no inventoryApi call of any kind in the handler (contrast the sibling export action which at least builds a real CSV).
- Fix: Loop the selected ids through a real deactivate/delete call (`updateItem(id, { isActive: false })` or a new DELETE route), await them all, and only toast on success — or remove the bulk delete action until it is implemented.

**Import Data dialog on the inventory page is a simulation — no upload, no API call**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/ImportDataDialog.tsx:146` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On /inventory the "Import" button opens a dialog that validates a CSV, animates a progress bar to 100%, and reports "N records imported". Nothing is sent anywhere — the items never appear in inventory, and the user has been told their import succeeded.
- Evidence: ImportDataDialog.tsx:146-162: `const handleImport = async () => { if (errors.length > 0) return toast.error(...); setStep('importing'); setImporting(true); setProgress(0);\n\n // Simulate import progress\n const total = parsedData.length;\n for (let i = 0; i <= total; i++) { await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 30)); setProgress(Math.round((i / total) * 100)); }\n\n setImporting(false); setStep('done'); toast.success(t('common:import_success_toast', { count: parsedData.length, module }));` — the file contains no api/axios import. It is mounted from InventoryPage.tsx:221-226 `<ImportDataDialog ... defaultModule="inventory" />`, and there is no bulk-import route in packages/inventory-service (
- Fix: Either implement a POST /inventory/items/bulk (or per-row createItem loop with camelCase keys) and wire handleImport to it, or hide the Import button until the endpoint exists.

**Categories can never be created or edited from the UI — createCategory/updateCategory are unreachable**
- `frontend/src/lib/api.ts:378` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: The Category dropdown on /inventory/new (and the category filter on /inventory) can only ever offer the two categories the seed routine creates ("Marble", "Granite"). There is no "New category" or "Edit category" control on any inventory page, so a user cannot organise their catalogue at all. PATCH /inventory/categories/:categoryId has no client method whatsoever.
- Evidence: api.ts:377-378 exposes `categories: () => api.get('/inventory/categories'), createCategory: (data: any) => api.post('/inventory/categories', data),` and the backend supports all three verbs (inventory.routes.ts:21-23: `router.post('/categories', ..., validate(createCategorySchema), ctrl.createCategory); router.get('/categories', ...); router.patch('/categories/:categoryId', ..., ctrl.updateCategory);`), but `grep -rn "createCategory\|updateCategory" frontend/src` outside lib/api.ts returns nothing — no component calls either, and inventoryApi has no updateCategory method at all. The only category source in practice is inventory.service.ts:302-318 seedDefaults, which creates exactly 'Marble' 
- Fix: Add a category management surface (inline "+ New category" in the create/edit item forms plus a small manage dialog) calling `createCategory({ name, description?, parentId? })` — note createCategorySchema requires name length >= 2 — and add `updateCategory: (id, data) => api.patch('/inventory/categories/' + id, data)` to inventoryApi.

### Ledger

**Party Ledger "Export statement" exports nothing**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/PartyLedgerPage.tsx:103` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: Party Ledger -> Export statement -> CSV / Excel / JSON / PDF: a green "export coming soon" toast fires and no file is ever downloaded. (Print works only because it falls back to window.print.)
- Evidence: The button is rendered with no data, columns, or onExport — PartyLedgerPage.tsx:103 `<ExportButton label={t('ledger:export_statement')} />`. ExportButton needs one of them: shared/ExportButton.tsx:48-49 `if (onExport) { ... return; }`, :63 `if (data && data.length > 0) { ... return; }`, falling through to :84 `toast.success(t('export_coming_soon', { format: format.toUpperCase() }));`. Compare the working call site LedgerPage.tsx:266-270 `<ExportButton data={entries} columns={['entry_date','account_type','entry_type','amount','narration']} filename="ledger" />`.
- Fix: Pass the statement rows: `<ExportButton label={...} data={entriesWithBalance} columns={['entry_date','narration','account_type','entry_type','amount','running_balance']} filename={`statement_${party?.name ?? partyId}`} />`.

**Day Book "Type" column is always JOURNAL — service reads a non-existent Prisma field**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:451` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Ledger -> Day Book tab: every row's Type chip reads "JOURNAL" regardless of whether the entry is a purchase, sale, payment or manual entry, so the day book cannot be used to tell transaction kinds apart.
- Evidence: services/ledger.service.ts:445-453 `return entries.map((e: any) => ({ id: e.id, date: e.entry_date, narration: e.narration || e.description || '', debit: ..., credit: ..., type: e.ref_type || 'JOURNAL', party_name: e.party?.name }));` — `ref_type` (and `description`) do not exist on the model; packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:943-945 declares `narration String?` / `reference_type String?` / `reference_id String?`. So `e.ref_type` is always undefined and `type` is always the fallback. The page maps that field straight into the account-type chip — LedgerPage.tsx:95 `account_type: e.type ?? e.account_type ?? 'JOURNAL',` rendered at :237-239 `<span className="text-xs px-2 py-1 rounded bg-mut
- Fix: Use the real column: `type: e.reference_type || e.account_type || 'JOURNAL'` (and `narration: e.narration || ''`).

**Manual-entry party balance update is not business-scoped (cross-tenant write)**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:118` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: A user of business A who posts a manual DEBIT/CREDIT with a partyId belonging to business B mutates that other tenant's party balance (which then feeds B's Outstanding tab and balance sheet). The ledger row itself is scoped to A, so the corruption is invisible from A's UI.
- Evidence: services/ledger.service.ts:116-124 `if (data.partyId) { const delta = data.entryType === 'DEBIT' ? data.amount : -data.amount; await prisma.party.update({ where: { id: data.partyId }, data: { balance: { increment: delta } } }); }` — the lookup is by primary key only, with no business_id predicate. Every other read in the same file is scoped, e.g. :56-58 `const party = await prisma.party.findFirst({ where: { id: partyId, business_id: businessId } });` and :132-134 in getPartyStatement. The controller does have the tenant id available: controllers/ledger.controller.ts:19-21 `const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string; ... ledgerService.createManualEntry(businessId, userId, req.b
- Fix: Verify ownership first (`const party = await prisma.party.findFirst({ where: { id: data.partyId, business_id: businessId } }); if (!party) throw new NotFoundError('Party');`) and wrap the entry insert + balance update in a single prisma.$transaction so they cannot diverge.

**Trial Balance and P&L exclude same-day entries because endDate is parsed as midnight**
- `packages/ledger-service/src/services/ledger.service.ts:175` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Ledger -> Trial Balance (and Profit & Loss with the default To = today): payments recorded today never appear, so Trial Balance can show a non-zero "Difference" and today's cash/bank movements and P&L figures are understated until tomorrow.
- Evidence: Both reports build the upper bound from the bare date string — services/ledger.service.ts:174-175 `const startDate = query.startDate ? new Date(query.startDate) : start; const endDate = query.endDate ? new Date(query.endDate) : end;` used at :186-187 `AND entry_date >= ${startDate} AND entry_date <= ${endDate}`, and the same in getProfitAndLoss:211-212 + :219 `entry_date: { gte: startDate, lte: endDate }`. The frontend sends a plain YYYY-MM-DD, which `new Date()` parses as 00:00:00Z — LedgerPage.tsx:25 `const today = new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0];`, :111 `await ledgerApi.trialBalance({ startDate: thirtyDaysAgo, endDate: today })`, :141 `await ledgerApi.profitLoss({ startDate: from ?
- Fix: Normalize the upper bound to end-of-day in getTrialBalance/getProfitAndLoss/getBalanceSheet (`const endDate = query.endDate ? new Date(new Date(query.endDate).setHours(23,59,59,999)) : end;`), the same way getDayBook already does.

**All ledger fetches swallow errors, so a 403/500 renders a silently empty page**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/LedgerPage.tsx:77` — *broken-handler*
- Impact: A STAFF user sees the Ledger item in the sidebar, clicks it, and every request 403s — the page renders "no data" empty states on all five tabs with no error message or access-denied screen, so it looks like the business has no ledger at all. The same silent blank happens for any 500/502 (e.g. ledger-service down).
- Evidence: Every fetch has a bare catch that resets state — LedgerPage.tsx:77-79 `} catch { setEntries([]); } finally {`, :101-103 `} catch { setDayBookEntries([]); }`, :131-133 `} catch { setTrialBalance([]); }`, :155-157 `} catch { setPlData(null); }`, :186-189 `} catch { setOutstandingParties([]); setOutstandingTotals({ receivable: 0, payable: 0 }); }`, and PartyLedgerPage.tsx:49-52 `} catch { setParty(null); setEntries([]); }`. The routes require permissions STAFF does not have — packages/ledger-service/src/routes/ledger.routes.ts:11-21 `requirePermission('LEDGER_VIEW')` / `requirePermission('REPORT_VIEW')` versus packages/ledger-service/src/shared/constants/index.ts:97-102 `STAFF: ['PURCHASE_CREAT
- Fix: Distinguish 403 from empty in the catch blocks (show AccessDeniedPage, which already exists at components/shared/AccessDeniedPage.tsx, on 403 and an error toast/retry state otherwise), and filter navItems by the current business-user role/permissions.

**Ledger entries list has no pagination, and the stat cards total only the first 20 rows**
- `frontend/src/components/ledger/LedgerPage.tsx:194` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Ledger -> All entries shows at most 20 rows with no next-page control, and the Total Debit / Total Credit / Net Balance cards at the top of the page are the sum of just those 20 rows — presented as business-wide totals.
- Evidence: The page requests no page/limit and ignores the returned meta — LedgerPage.tsx:75-76 `const { data } = await ledgerApi.entries({ search, ...filters }); setEntries(data?.data || []);` while the service always pages (services/ledger.service.ts:68-69 `const { page, limit } = parsePagination(query); const offset = getPaginationOffset(page, limit);`, :86-87 `skip: offset, take: limit`, :92 `return { data: entries, meta: buildPaginationMeta(total, page, limit) };` with DEFAULT_LIMIT = 20 at shared/constants/index.ts:28). The totals are then derived from that page — LedgerPage.tsx:194-195 `const totalDebit = entries.filter(e => e.entry_type === 'DEBIT').reduce((sum,e) => sum + Number(e.amount), 0);
- Fix: Add page state + the shared Pagination component driven by `meta` from the response, and have getBusinessLedger also return debit/credit aggregates for the filtered set so the stat cards stop being page-scoped.

### Parties & Cutters

**Optional party/cutter fields can never be cleared — empty input is sent as undefined and dropped**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:430` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: Edit a party, clear its WhatsApp number (or email, GSTIN, PAN, street address, city, state, pincode, credit period, credit limit) and save. Toast says updated, but the old value is still there on reload — the field is un-erasable through the UI. Same for a cutter's phone on both cutter edit dialogs.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/EditPartyDialog.tsx:430-449 `whatsapp: form.whatsapp.trim() || undefined, ... email: form.email.trim() || undefined, gstNumber: gst.gstin.trim() || undefined, gstState: gst.gstState || undefined, panNumber: gst.panNumber.trim() || undefined, address: addr.address.trim() || undefined, city: addr.city || undefined, state: addr.state || undefined, pincode: addr.pincode ? ... : undefined, creditPeriodDays: credit.periodDays ? parseInt(...) : undefined, creditLimit: credit.limit ? parseFloat(...) : undefined,` — JSON.stringify drops undefined keys, and the service gates every field on presence: packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:199-219 `...(da
- Fix: Send `null`/empty string for cleared optional fields (the zod update path is unvalidated for PATCH, and the columns are nullable) — e.g. `whatsapp: form.whatsapp.trim() || null` — and keep the service's `!== undefined` gates so null explicitly clears.

**Parties CSV/Excel export writes empty Receivable and Payable columns**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:280` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On /parties, Export -> CSV (or Excel/JSON toast path uses whole objects, but CSV/Excel is the documented flow): the downloaded parties_<date>.csv has headers name,phone,receivable,payable with the last two columns blank on every row. The exported file is useless for the numbers users actually want. The bulk "Export selected" action on the same page gets it right, so the two exports disagree.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:280 `<ExportButton data={parties} columns={['name', 'phone', 'receivable', 'payable']} filename="parties" />` but the Party objects have no such keys — frontend/src/types/index.ts:68-69 `balance: number; opening_balance?: number;` (server returns the prisma row, profile.service.ts:247-251). frontend/src/components/shared/ExportButton.tsx:32-38 `const rows = data.map(row => headers.map(h => { const val = row[h]; const str = val == null ? '' : ...` -> `undefined` becomes ''. The correct derivation exists 70 lines below: PartiesPage.tsx:352 `...items.map(p => `${p.name},${p.phone || ''},${p.balance > 0 ? p.balance : 0},${p.balance < 0 ? Math.abs(p.
- Fix: Pass a projected array to ExportButton, e.g. `data={parties.map(p => ({ name: p.name, phone: p.phone || '', receivable: p.balance < 0 ? Math.abs(p.balance) : 0, payable: p.balance > 0 ? p.balance : 0 }))}` (using the corrected sign convention).

**Cutter detail totals are computed from only the 50 most recent transactions while the count chip shows all**
- `packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:443` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: For any cutter with more than 50 transactions, /cutters/:id shows "137 purchases" in the chip but Total Quantity / Total Earned / Paid / Unpaid are silently computed over just the newest 50 rows, so the earnings and (critically) the Unpaid amount are far too low. The "Mark all as paid" button is also hidden whenever all of the visible 50 happen to be paid, even though older unpaid rows exist — and when shown it marks unpaid rows the user was never able to see.
- Evidence: packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:430-457 getCutter: `include: { _count: { select: { transactions: true } }, transactions: { where: { OR: [ { purchase_id: null }, { purchase: { status: 'ACTIVE' } } ] }, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' }, take: 50, ... } }` — `_count` is unfiltered/uncapped while the array is capped at 50 and additionally filters out non-ACTIVE purchases. The page derives every number from the truncated array: frontend/src/components/parties/CutterDetailPage.tsx:388-396 `const transactions = cutter.transactions || []; const totalQty = transactions.reduce(...); const totalEarned = transactions.reduce(...); const unpaidAmount = transactions.filter(t => t.is
- Fix: Return server-computed aggregates (sum of quantity/amount and unpaid sum via prisma.cutterTransaction.aggregate with the same OR filter) alongside a paginated transaction page, and make _count use the identical filter as the transaction list; render totals from the aggregates.

**Standalone cutter transactions show no date — the Date column is hard-wired to the purchase relation**
- `frontend/src/components/parties/CutterDetailPage.tsx:565` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Use "Record Payment" on /cutters/:id (the primary way to log manual cutter work). The new row appears with Purchase "—", Supplier "—" and Date "—": there is no way to tell when any manually recorded cutter payment happened, in the table or anywhere else in the UI.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/parties/CutterDetailPage.tsx:565-570 `<td className="px-4 py-3 text-sm text-muted-foreground"><div className="flex items-center gap-1.5"><Calendar className="h-3.5 w-3.5 shrink-0" />{tx.purchase?.purchase_date ? formatDate(tx.purchase.purchase_date) : '—'}</div></td>` — `tx.created_at` is available (frontend/src/types/index.ts:166 `created_at?: string;`, and the prisma row is returned whole) but never used. The backend also advertises a date it throws away: packages/profile-service/src/services/profile.service.ts:341-361 signature `data: { quantity: number; rate: number; notes?: string; isPaid?: boolean; transactionDate?: string }` yet the create block is `data: { cut
- Fix: Render `tx.purchase?.purchase_date ?? tx.created_at` in the Date cell, and either persist `transactionDate` in createCutterTransaction (add a `transaction_date` column or map it onto created_at) or drop it from the signature and add a date input to the Record Payment dialog.

**Period/date-range filter on the Parties page does nothing — listParties ignores date_from/date_to**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:330` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On /parties the filter bar renders "Period: Today | Last 7 days | Last 30 days | Last year | Custom". Clicking any of them highlights the chip, triggers a refetch, and returns the exact same unfiltered list — no rows change and no message explains why. Same for the custom from/to date inputs.
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/shared/PartiesPage.tsx:330 `<AdvancedFilters fields={filterFields} values={filters} onChange={setFilters} onReset={() => setFilters({})} />` with only one field declared (`balance_type`, lines 215-224), but AdvancedFilters unconditionally renders the preset bar and injects date keys: frontend/src/components/shared/AdvancedFilters.tsx:63-64 `const fromKey = `${dateKey}_from`; const toKey = `${dateKey}_to`;` (dateKey defaults to 'date', line 59) and :86-89 `const range = getPresetRange(preset); if (range) { onChange({ ...values, [fromKey]: range.from, [toKey]: range.to }); }`. Those land in the request via PartiesPage.tsx:162 `const { data } = await profileApi.parties({
- Fix: Either support created_at range filtering in listParties (`if (query.date_from || query.date_to) where.created_at = {...}`) or add a prop to AdvancedFilters to hide the period bar and don't render it on the Parties page.

**Party detail bill amounts and the Record Payment dialog's outstanding amounts disagree for the same bill**
- `frontend/src/components/parties/PartyDetailPage.tsx:60` — *data-bug*
- Impact: On a party whose purchases carry direct/indirect expenses or cutter cost, the Bills tab shows e.g. "₹1,00,000 due" for purchase PUR-12 (party-facing amount only), but clicking "Record Payment" on the same page lists PUR-12 with balance ₹1,12,500 and caps/allocates against that larger figure. The user cannot reconcile the two numbers, and "Pay full balance" pays more than the page says is owed.
- Evidence: Page excludes expenses — frontend/src/components/parties/PartyDetailPage.tsx:60-95 `const subtotal = Number(raw.subtotal || 0); ... if (subtotal > 0) { return subtotal + gst - discount + roundOff; }` / `getPartyBalance = partyAmount - Number(tx.paid_amount)`. The dialog uses the stored balance which includes them — frontend/src/components/shared/RecordPaymentDialog.tsx:97-99 `const { data } = await billingApi.partyOutstandingBills(partyId, type); const fetched: OutstandingBill[] = data?.data || [];` -> packages/billing-service/src/services/billing.service.ts:509-517 `return purchases.map((p: any) => ({ id: p.id, ref: p.purchase_number, ..., balance: Number(p.balance_amount), ... }))` and pac
- Fix: Have billing-service compute the party-facing outstanding (subtotal + gst - discount + round_off - paid_amount) in getPartyOutstandingBills, or store a dedicated party_balance_amount column that both the party ledger and the payment allocator read.

### Profile, Notifications, Help

**WhatsApp webhook always throws: session upsert keyed on uuid PK with a phone number, and create uses business_id 'system' which violates the FK**
- `packages/notification-service/src/services/notification.service.ts:371` — *correctness*
- Impact: Every inbound WhatsApp message to the configured number makes POST /api/v1/notifications/whatsapp/webhook return 500 instead of 200, so Meta retries and eventually disables the webhook; no session/last-message is ever recorded.
- Evidence: notification.service.ts:371-384 `await prisma.whatsAppSession.upsert({ where: { id: msg.from }, update: { last_message: ..., updated_at: new Date() }, create: { id: uuidv4(), business_id: 'system', // Would need mapping logic\n phone: msg.from, ... } });` — `id` is the uuid primary key (schema.prisma:1151 `id String @id @default(uuid())`), so a phone number never matches and the create branch always runs; that branch writes `business_id: 'system'` against schema.prisma:1160 `business Business @relation(fields: [business_id], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)`, i.e. Prisma P2003 foreign-key failure. The throw propagates through controller:62 `await notificationService.handleWhatsAppWebhook
- Fix: Key the upsert on a unique business+phone (add `@@unique([business_id, phone])` and use `where: { business_id_phone: {...} }`), resolve the real business from the receiving phone_number_id in `change.value.metadata`, and skip/park the message when no business can be resolved instead of inserting a fake FK. Also verify Meta's X-Hub-Signature-256 before processing, since routes :8-9 are mounted befo

**Notification list ignores the selected business — all businesses' notifications are mixed together**
- `packages/notification-service/src/services/notification.service.ts:58` — *wrong-data*
- Impact: A user who owns two businesses sees business B's low-stock and bill reminders while working inside business A, with no indication which business they belong to — and reminders keep appearing for businesses they have switched away from.
- Evidence: The header is sent on every call (api.ts request interceptor: `if (businessId) { config.headers['x-business-id'] = businessId; }`) and notifications are stored per business (notification.service.ts:24 `business_id: data.businessId`, schema.prisma:1120 `business_id String?`), but the query drops it: notification.service.ts:58-60 `const where: any = { user_id: userId }; if (query.isRead !== undefined) where.is_read = query.isRead === 'true'; if (query.type) where.type = query.type;` — no business_id clause, and the controller never reads the header (:5-9 uses only `user?.userId`). The frontend also never passes one: api.ts:449 `list: (params?: any) => api.get('/notifications', { params })`, ca
- Fix: Read `req.headers['x-business-id']` in getUserNotifications (after verifying membership) and add `where.OR = [{ business_id: businessId }, { business_id: null }]`, or at minimum render the business name on each row so cross-business items are distinguishable.

**Only the newest 20 notifications are reachable; counts and the Unread tab are computed from that page**
- `frontend/src/components/notifications/NotificationsPage.tsx:44` — *pagination*
- Impact: Once a user has more than 20 notifications, everything older is permanently invisible (there is no pagination control anywhere on the page), the tab labels lie — "All (20)" and "Unread (n of the last 20)" — and after Mark All Read the server clears every unread row while the page can only re-render the 20 it holds.
- Evidence: NotificationsPage.tsx:44 `const { data } = await notificationApi.list();` sends no params, so notification.service.ts:55 `const { page, limit } = parsePagination(query)` falls back to shared/constants/index.ts:28 `export const DEFAULT_LIMIT = 20;` and the query truncates: service :63-68 `findMany({ where, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' }, skip: offset, take: limit })`. The page ignores the returned `meta` (buildPaginationMeta, service :75) and the server's `unreadCount` (:76) and derives its own from the page: :79-80 `const unreadCount = notifications.filter(n => !n.is_read).length; const filtered = tab === 'unread' ? notifications.filter(n => !n.is_read) : notifications;`, rendered at :107-1
- Fix: Pass `{ limit: 50 }` plus a "Load more" control driven by `data.meta`, use the server's `unreadCount` for the Unread tab label, and refetch after markAllRead instead of patching local state.

### Purchases

**updatePurchase drops expense receipt_url — expense receipts vanish on any edit**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:951` — *data-bug*
- Impact: A purchase has an expense with an attached receipt (visible as a "Receipt" link on the detail page). The user edits the purchase for any unrelated reason and saves; the expense row is re-created without receipt_url, so the receipt link disappears permanently even though the frontend sent the URL back. Cutter receipts survive the same edit, so the loss looks random.
- Evidence: Update re-creates the expense without receipt_url — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:951-959: `await tx.purchaseExpense.create({ data: { id: uuidv4(), purchase_id: purchaseId, expense_type_id: expense.expenseTypeId, expense_category: expense.expenseCategory, amount: new Prisma.Decimal(expense.amount), is_paid: isPaid, notes: expense.notes } });` — compare create at :254-264 which includes `receipt_url: expense.receiptUrl || null,` and the cutter branch of the same update at :984 `receipt_url: ct.receiptUrl || null,`. The frontend does round-trip the value — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:260 (prefill) `receipt_url: (ex as any).receipt_url || undef
- Fix: Add `receipt_url: expense.receiptUrl || null,` to the purchaseExpense.create call inside updatePurchase.

**Lot numbers are never readable back: PurchaseItem has no lot_number column, so the detail page always shows "—" and every edit regenerates lot numbers**
- `frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseDetailPage.tsx:174` — *response-shape-mismatch*
- Impact: User types lot number "MB-2024-07" on a lot row and saves. The purchase detail page's Lot column always renders "—" for every item. Worse, opening Edit shows an empty Lot # box, so saving the edit creates a replacement lot with an auto-generated number like LOT-1A2B-... — the user's own lot identifiers are permanently lost on the first edit.
- Evidence: Page reads it off the purchase item — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseDetailPage.tsx:173-175: `{item.lot_number || <span className="text-muted-foreground/40">—</span>}` (type declares it optional at frontend/src/types/index.ts:142 `lot_number?: string;`). But the column does not exist on the model the API returns — packages/shared/prisma/schema.prisma:512-530 `model PurchaseItem { id, purchase_id, item_id, quantity, rate, amount, unit, notes, created_at, updated_at ... }` — and the service only writes the number onto the separate Lot row (purchase.service.ts:187-194 `const lotNumber = item.lotNumber || generateReferenceNumber('LOT', businessId); ... lot_number: lotNumber,`). getPurc
- Fix: Either surface the lot number by joining `purchase.lots` (match on item_id / order) in both the detail page and the edit prefill, or add a `lot_number` column to PurchaseItem and populate it in createPurchase/updatePurchase.

**Editing a purchase resets every payment's date to today (paymentDate is never sent)**
- `frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:820` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: A purchase has payments dated 5 Jan and 20 Jan. The user edits the purchase (e.g. corrects the gadi number) and saves. Both payments are deleted and re-created with payment_date = now, so the payment history on the detail page, the cash/bank ledger dates and any period reports for January are silently wrong.
- Evidence: Payload has no paymentDate — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseForm.tsx:820-826: `payments: validPayments.map(p => ({ paymentMode: p.payment_mode, amount: p.amount, transactionRef: p.transaction_ref || undefined, receiptUrl: p.receipt_url || undefined, notes: p.notes || undefined }))` (the prefill at :279-289 also never keeps `payment_date`). Service defaults to now after wiping the old rows — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:835 `await tx.payment.deleteMany({ where: { reference_id: purchaseId, reference_type: 'PURCHASE' } });` and :1022 `payment_date: payment.paymentDate ? new Date(payment.paymentDate) : new Date(),`.
- Fix: Keep `payment_date` in the PaymentRow prefill and send `paymentDate: p.payment_date` in the payload; alternatively update existing payment rows by id instead of delete-and-recreate.

**Amount-range filter on the purchases list sends amount_min/amount_max but the service reads amount_from/amount_to — the filter does nothing**
- `frontend/src/components/shared/AdvancedFilters.tsx:283` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: On Purchases -> Filters, entering Min 50000 / Max 100000 in "Amount Range" leaves the table unchanged (the badge counter increments, so the user thinks the filter applied). All purchases keep showing regardless of amount.
- Evidence: Filter widget emits `<key>_min` / `<key>_max` — frontend/src/components/shared/AdvancedFilters.tsx:283 `value={values[\`${field.key}_min\`] || ''}` / :284 `onChange={(e) => updateValue(\`${field.key}_min\`, e.target.value)}` and :290-291 the `_max` equivalents; the page registers the field as `amount` — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchasesPage.tsx:76: `{ key: 'amount', label: t('purchases:amount_range_filter'), type: 'number-range' }`, and the values object is spread straight into the query at :43 `const { data } = await purchaseApi.list({ search, ...filters });`. The service only understands from/to — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:584-588: `if (filters.amo
- Fix: Accept `amount_min`/`amount_max` as aliases in listPurchases (like the existing startDate/date_from aliasing), or have AdvancedFilters emit `_from`/`_to` for number-range.

**Purchases list silently substitutes three hard-coded fake purchases when the API call fails**
- `frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchasesPage.tsx:45` — *data-bug*
- Impact: If GET /purchases errors (expired session, no business selected -> 400 from requireBusiness, gateway 502, RBAC 403), the page shows three invented purchases — "PUR-001 Sharma Seeds ₹125,000", "PUR-002 Ram Traders ₹78,000", "PUR-003 Lakshmi Suppliers ₹38,000" — with no error message, and the three stat cards report ₹241,000 of purchases that do not exist. Clicking a row navigates to /purchases/1 and shows "purchase not found".
- Evidence: frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchasesPage.tsx:43-50: `const { data } = await purchaseApi.list({ search, ...filters }); setPurchases(data?.data || []); } catch { setPurchases([ { id: '1', purchase_number: 'PUR-001', ... total_amount: 125000, ... }, { id: '2', purchase_number: 'PUR-002', ... }, { id: '3', ... } ] as Purchase[]); }` — and the totals are derived from that same array at :68-70 `const totalAmount = purchases.reduce((s, p) => s + Number(p.total_amount), 0);`.
- Fix: Drop the mock fallback: set an error state, render an error banner with a retry action, and keep `purchases` empty so the stat cards read ₹0.

**No pagination on the purchases list — only the newest 20 purchases are reachable and the stat cards sum just that page**
- `frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchasesPage.tsx:43` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: A business with 200 purchases sees only 20; there is no next-page control anywhere on the page, so the other 180 are unreachable from the Purchases screen (search is the only workaround). The three header cards labelled "Total Purchases / Total Paid / Total Balance" show the sum of those 20 rows only, which reads as a business-wide total and is wrong by an order of magnitude.
- Evidence: Page never sends page/limit and ignores the returned meta — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchasesPage.tsx:43-44: `const { data } = await purchaseApi.list({ search, ...filters }); setPurchases(data?.data || []);` (no `meta`/`page` state anywhere in the file; the only table is `<BulkSelectDataTable ... data={purchases} />` at :202-230), while totals come from that page at :68-70. The API does paginate and returns the counts — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:548-550 `const limit = Number(filters.limit) || 20; const skip = (page - 1) * limit;` and :605-615 `return { data: purchases, meta: { total, page, limit, totalPages, hasNext, hasPrev } };` (other callers alre
- Fix: Add page state + pagination controls driven by `data.meta` (hasNext/hasPrev/totalPages), and compute the stat cards from a server-side aggregate (purchaseApi.dashboard() already returns overall totals) instead of the current page.

**payment_status can be PAID while balance_amount is still positive — a fully paid purchase keeps offering "Record Payment"**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:128` — *data-bug*
- Impact: Purchase with items ₹100,000 + a ₹20,000 transport expense, fully paid to the supplier (₹100,000): the detail page shows a green "PAID" badge and, right below, "Balance Payable ₹20,000" in red, plus an active "Record Payment" button. Clicking it creates another ₹20,000 payment to the supplier who was never owed it (billing-service then recomputes against total_amount and flips the status back to PARTIAL), inflating supplier payments and the cash ledger. The list page shows the same contradiction (PAID badge next to a red balance).
- Evidence: Status is computed against the party-facing amount but balance against the full total — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:121 `const partyAmount = subtotal + gstAmount - discount + roundOff;`, :128 `const balanceAmount = totalAmount - paidAmount;`, :133-135 `let paymentStatus ... = 'UNPAID'; if (paidAmount >= partyAmount) paymentStatus = 'PAID'; else if (paidAmount > 0) paymentStatus = 'PARTIAL';` (totalAmount at :116 includes `directExpense + indirectExpense + cutterCost`). The UI keys the payment CTA off balance_amount — frontend/src/components/purchase/PurchaseDetailPage.tsx:352-356: `{Number(purchase.balance_amount) > 0 && (<Button size="sm" onClick={() => setPay
- Fix: Pick one basis and use it everywhere: store a party-facing balance (e.g. `balance_amount = partyAmount - paidAmount`, keeping expense/cutter payables as their own ledger rows) or compute payment_status from total_amount, and gate the Record Payment button on the same field billing-service updates.

**createPurchase/updatePurchase never verify that partyId, itemId, cutterId or expenseTypeId belong to the caller's business**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:435` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: An authenticated user of business A who knows (or guesses/enumerates from another endpoint) a UUID from business B can POST /purchases with that party/item id: the transaction increments business B's party balance, mutates business B's inventory item stock, and writes lot/ledger rows referencing business B's records — a cross-tenant write. It also silently books purchases against the wrong party if a stale id is posted.
- Evidence: Nothing scopes the ids to businessId — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:435-442: `await tx.party.update({ where: { id: data.partyId }, data: { balance: { increment: new Prisma.Decimal(partyBalanceAmount) } } });` and :218-232 `const existingItem = await tx.inventoryItem.findUnique({ where: { id: item.itemId } }); if (existingItem) { ... await tx.inventoryItem.update({ where: { id: item.itemId }, data: { quantity_in: ..., current_stock: ... } }); }` — both keyed on id only. Same in updatePurchase (:1075-1078 `await tx.party.update({ where: { id: partyId }, ... })`, :938-947). The cutter lookup is equally unscoped (:308-311 `tx.cutter.findUnique({ where: { id: ct.cutt
- Fix: Before the writes, assert ownership: `const party = await tx.party.findFirst({ where: { id: data.partyId, business_id: businessId } }); if (!party) throw new AppError('Party not found', 404);` and the equivalent findFirst-with-business_id checks for every itemId, cutterId and expenseTypeId in the payload.

**Attachment upload does not check that the purchase belongs to the caller's business**
- `packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:1130` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: POST /api/v1/purchases/<a purchase id from another business>/attachments succeeds: the file is stored and an Attachment row is created with `purchase_id` pointing at the other tenant's purchase. That attachment then renders on the other business's purchase detail page (its include has no business filter), letting one tenant inject files into another tenant's records. It also silently creates orphan rows for a bad/typo'd purchase id instead of returning 404.
- Evidence: No ownership lookup before the write — packages/purchase-service/src/services/purchase.service.ts:1124-1142: `async addAttachment(purchaseId: string, businessId: string, uploadedBy: string, file: {...}) { return prisma.attachment.create({ data: { id: uuidv4(), business_id: businessId, reference_type: 'purchase', purchase_id: purchaseId, ... } }); }` — called straight from packages/purchase-service/src/controllers/purchase.controller.ts:147-152 `const attachment = await purchaseService.addAttachment(purchaseId, businessId, userId, {...})`. The read side does not re-filter attachments by business — purchase.service.ts:507-516 `include: { ... attachments: true, ledger_entries: true }`. Contrast
- Fix: In addAttachment, first `const purchase = await prisma.purchase.findFirst({ where: { id: purchaseId, business_id: businessId } }); if (!purchase) throw new AppError('Purchase not found', 404);` (and consider filtering the attachments include by business_id).

### Sales

**No pagination on the sales list: only the first 20 sales are ever shown, and the KPI cards and export cover just that page**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:43` — *missing-wiring*
- Impact: A business with 300 sales sees exactly 20 rows with no next-page control anywhere on the page, so 280 sales are unreachable from /sales (only findable via search). The three StatCards ('Total Sales', 'Total Received', 'Total Balance') sum only those 20 rows, so they under-report revenue and receivables by an order of magnitude, and the Export button writes a CSV containing 20 rows while claiming to export the sales list. The service returns the correct `meta.total`/`totalPages` but the page ignores it.
- Evidence: Frontend never sends page/limit and never reads meta — frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:43-44: `const { data } = await salesApi.list({ search, ...filters }); setSales(data?.data || []);` (no page state, and the rendered tree at :202-231 has no pagination control; BulkSelectDataTable has none either). Backend defaults to 20 — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:465-467: `const page = Number(filters.page) || 1; const limit = Number(filters.limit) || 20; const skip = (page - 1) * limit;` and returns :517-527: `return { data: sales, meta: { total, page, limit, totalPages: Math.ceil(total / limit), hasNext: ..., hasPrev: ... } };` KPIs/export are page-scoped — :68-70 (
- Fix: Add page state + a pagination control driven by `data.meta`, and source the three StatCards from GET /sales/dashboard (already implemented at packages/sales-service/src/routes/sales.routes.ts:16 -> getSalesDashboard, which returns business-wide `overall.totalAmount/paidAmount/balanceAmount`) instead of reducing the current page.

**Amount-range filter on the sales list is inert — UI emits amount_min/amount_max, controller reads amount_from/amount_to**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:81` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: User opens Filters on /sales, types 50000 in the amount Min box, and the list does not change — sales below Rs 50,000 stay visible. The unknown query params are ignored by the service, so the filter appears broken with no error.
- Evidence: The shared filter component emits `<key>_min` / `<key>_max` — frontend/src/components/shared/AdvancedFilters.tsx:277-293: `{field.type === 'number-range' && (<div ...><Input ... value={values[`${field.key}_min`] || ''} onChange={(e) => updateValue(`${field.key}_min`, e.target.value)} /><Input ... value={values[`${field.key}_max`] || ''} onChange={(e) => updateValue(`${field.key}_max`, e.target.value)} /></div>)}` with the sales field declared as `{ key: 'amount', label: t('sales:amount_range_filter'), type: 'number-range' }` (frontend/src/components/sales/SalesPage.tsx:81) and spread straight into the query at :43 `salesApi.list({ search, ...filters })` — i.e. `?amount_min=50000&amount_max=`
- Fix: Map the keys before calling the API (`amount_from: filters.amount_min, amount_to: filters.amount_max`) or accept both spellings in listSales: `const amountFrom = filters.amount_from || filters.amount_min;`. Note the date filter on other pages uses `_from`/`_to`, so the service naming is the outlier only for number-range.

**Lot dropdown in the sale form only ever loads the first 20 lots**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:283` — *payload-mismatch*
- Impact: A business with 60 lots opens /sales/new: the 'Select lot' dropdown lists at most 20 (the 20 most recently created), so most existing stock cannot be selected and the sale cannot be recorded. There is no search or 'load more' in the select, so there is no workaround. Combined with the AVAILABLE-only filter above, the visible set is frequently empty.
- Evidence: Called with no params — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:281-286: `const [partiesRes, lotsRes] = await Promise.all([ profileApi.parties(), salesApi.lots(), ]); setParties(partiesRes.data?.data || []); setLots(lotsRes.data?.data || []);` and frontend/src/lib/api.ts:350: `lots: (params?: any) => api.get('/sales/lots/all', { params }),` Server paginates by default — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:572-593: `const page = Number(filters.page) || 1; const limit = Number(filters.limit) || 20; const skip = (page - 1) * limit; ... prisma.lot.findMany({ where, include: {...}, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' }, skip, take: limit })` — the returned `meta.total`/`hasNex
- Fix: Request everything the picker needs: `salesApi.lots({ limit: 500, status: 'AVAILABLE' })` (listLots already supports `status` and `itemId` filters at sales.service.ts:576-578), or paginate/typeahead the select against meta.hasNext.

**Attachment upload does not verify the sale belongs to the caller's business — cross-tenant attachment injection**
- `packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:834` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: Any authenticated user with SALE_CREATE in their own business can POST a file to /api/v1/sales/<another-tenant's-saleId>/attachments. The row is created with the attacker's business_id but the victim's sale_id, and because getSaleById includes attachments without a business filter, the file appears in the victim's Bills & Attachments card on their sale detail page (and is openable/downloadable from there). The victim cannot delete it either, since deleteAttachment filters on business_id.
- Evidence: No ownership check on the write path — packages/sales-service/src/controllers/sales.controller.ts:143-162: `export const uploadAttachment = asyncHandler(async (req, res) => { const businessId = req.headers['x-business-id'] as string; ... const { saleId } = req.params; if (!req.file) {...} ... const attachment = await salesService.addAttachment(saleId, businessId, userId!, {...}); })` and packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:834-853: `async addAttachment(saleId, businessId, uploadedBy, file) { return prisma.attachment.create({ data: { id: uuidv4(), business_id: businessId, reference_type: 'sale', sale_id: saleId, ... } }); }` — no `prisma.sale.findFirst({ where: { id: saleId, 
- Fix: In addAttachment (and in uploadAttachment before writing the file), first `const sale = await prisma.sale.findFirst({ where: { id: saleId, business_id: businessId } }); if (!sale) throw new NotFoundError('Sale');`. Also scope the `attachments` include in getSaleById to `{ where: { business_id: businessId } }` for defence in depth.

**PATCH /sales/:saleId is guarded by SALE_CREATE instead of SALE_EDIT, so STAFF can modify existing sales**
- `packages/sales-service/src/routes/sales.routes.ts:32` — *auth-bug*
- Impact: A STAFF user is intentionally granted SALE_CREATE but not SALE_EDIT. Because the update route checks SALE_CREATE, that user can open /sales/:id/edit and change the party, date, notes, discount, GST, round-off and reminders on any existing sale — altering totals, payment_status and the customer's ledger balance — even though the role model says they may only create. The SALE_EDIT permission is never checked anywhere in the service.
- Evidence: packages/sales-service/src/routes/sales.routes.ts:31-33: `router.get('/:saleId', requirePermission('SALE_VIEW'), ctrl.getSale);` / `router.patch('/:saleId', requirePermission('SALE_CREATE'), ctrl.updateSale);` / `router.delete('/:saleId', requirePermission('SALE_DELETE'), ctrl.deleteSale);` Role matrix distinguishes the two — packages/sales-service/src/shared/constants/index.ts:93-99: `STAFF: [ 'PURCHASE_CREATE', 'PURCHASE_VIEW', 'SALE_CREATE', 'SALE_VIEW', 'INVENTORY_VIEW', 'PAYMENT_VIEW' ],` versus :76-79 `MANAGER: [ ..., 'SALE_CREATE', 'SALE_EDIT', 'SALE_VIEW', ... ]`. requirePermission enforces exactly the strings passed — shared/middleware/rbac.middleware.ts: `const hasPermission = requ
- Fix: Change the PATCH route to `requirePermission('SALE_EDIT')` (and consider `requirePermission('SALE_EDIT')` for the attachment routes, which currently also use SALE_CREATE/SALE_DELETE).

**Payments with mode CREDIT are counted as cash received but no Payment row is written**
- `packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:124` — *data-bug*
- Impact: User records a sale, picks 'Credit' as the payment mode and enters the full amount (the amount field is required — zod rejects amount 0, and the frontend drops rows with amount 0 entirely). The sale is saved as PAID with paid_amount = full amount, the customer's receivable balance is zeroed and a CASH debit ledger entry is created — yet no payment was actually received and the sale detail page shows no payment record under 'Payment Records'. The receivable simply disappears.
- Evidence: CREDIT is summed into paidAmount and drives the status — packages/sales-service/src/services/sales.service.ts:123-135: `let paidAmount = 0; for (const p of data.payments) { paidAmount += p.amount; } const balanceAmount = totalAmount - paidAmount; ... if (paidAmount >= partyAmount) paymentStatus = 'PAID'; else if (paidAmount > 0) paymentStatus = 'PARTIAL'; else if (data.payments.some(p => p.paymentMode === 'CREDIT')) paymentStatus = 'CREDIT';` — the CREDIT branch is unreachable because any CREDIT row has amount > 0 (validators/index.ts:181 `amount: z.number().positive()`). But the Payment row is skipped — :266-267: `for (const payment of data.payments) { if (payment.paymentMode === 'CREDIT') 
- Fix: Exclude CREDIT rows from paidAmount (`for (const p of data.payments) { if (p.paymentMode === 'CREDIT') continue; paidAmount += p.amount; }`) so the CREDIT status branch becomes reachable and the receivable is preserved — or remove 'Credit' from the sale payment-mode dropdown, since a credit sale is simply a sale with no payment rows.

**Removing an already-uploaded bill in edit mode never deletes it server-side**
- `frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:580` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: On /sales/:id/edit the user hovers an existing bill thumbnail, clicks the trash icon (it disappears from the grid), then clicks 'Update Sale' and sees 'Sale updated'. The attachment is still on the sale: reopening the sale detail page or the edit form shows it again. There is no way to remove a wrongly-attached bill from the edit screen.
- Evidence: Removal is local-state only — frontend/src/components/sales/SaleCreatePage.tsx:580-587: `const removeAttachment = (entryKey: string, attKey: string) => { setEntries(prev => prev.map(e => { if (e._key !== entryKey) return e; const att = e.attachments.find(a => a._key === attKey); if (att?.preview && !att.uploadedUrl) URL.revokeObjectURL(att.preview); return { ...e, attachments: e.attachments.filter(a => a._key !== attKey) }; })); };` — no API call, and no record of the removed id is kept. Submit only ever uploads new files — :674-681: `const newAttachments = entry.attachments.filter(a => a.file && !a.uploadedId); for (const att of newAttachments) { ... const res = await salesApi.uploadAttachm
- Fix: Track removed ids (e.g. a `removedAttachmentIds` set when `att.uploadedId` exists) and in handleSubmit call `await salesApi.deleteAttachment(saleId, id)` for each before/after the uploads; or call deleteAttachment immediately on click with an optimistic-revert on failure, as SaleDetailPage does at :62-74.

### Subscription & Referrals

**Redeem picks an arbitrary business membership, so multi-business users get "No active subscription found" or extend someone else's plan**
- `packages/referral-service/src/services/referral.service.ts:238` — *correctness*
- Impact: The Redeem button fails with "No active subscription found to extend." for users whose first/arbitrary membership is a business without a subscription — even though their subscription exists on another business — and can silently add the earned days to a business the user is only a staff member of.
- Evidence: `const businessUser = await prisma.businessUser.findFirst({ where: { user_id: userId }, include: { business: { include: { subscriptions: { where: { status: { in: ['ACTIVE','TRIAL'] } }, orderBy: { created_at: 'desc' }, take: 1 } } } } });` — no `role` filter, no `is_active` filter, no ordering — then `if (!businessUser?.business?.subscriptions?.length) throw new BadRequestError('No active subscription found to extend.');` (referral.service.ts:238-255). Subscriptions are, however, always written to a single "primary" business by `orderBy: [{ business: { is_primary: 'desc' } }, { joined_at: 'asc' }]` (packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:67-71), so the two selecti
- Fix: Resolve the subscription the same way subscription-service does — search across all OWNER memberships for the newest ACTIVE/TRIAL subscription (`prisma.subscription.findFirst({ where: { business_id: { in: ownedBusinessIds }, ... } })`) — and never extend a business where the caller is not OWNER.

**Leaderboard returns every top referrer's real name and unmasked phone number to any authenticated user**
- `packages/referral-service/src/services/referral.service.ts:388` — *data-leak*
- Impact: Any logged-in user (including a free-trial user — the route has no eligibility gate) can read GET /api/v1/referrals/leaderboard and harvest up to 20 other customers' full names and phone numbers from the JSON, even though the UI does not display them.
- Evidence: `SELECT r.referrer_id, u.name, u.phone, COUNT(r.id)::int as count, ...` (referral.service.ts:388) with the rows returned untouched: `return leaderboard;` (referral.service.ts:399) → `res.json({ success: true, data: leaderboard })` (referral.controller.ts:50); the only protection is `router.use(authenticateToken); ... router.get('/leaderboard', ctrl.getLeaderboard);` (referral.routes.ts:7,16). The service masks phones everywhere else, proving the intent: `referred_phone: r.referred?.phone ? r.referred.phone.replace(/(\d{3})(\d+)(\d{2})/, '$1****$3') : '—'` (referral.service.ts:351-353).
- Fix: Drop `u.phone` from the SELECT and return a display name only (first name + masked surname, or 'You' for the caller); never ship referrer_id/phone to the client.

**GET /subscriptions/invoices is unreachable — subscriptionApi has no invoices() method and no billing-history UI exists**
- `frontend/src/lib/api.ts:456` — *missing-endpoint-binding*
- Impact: Users have no way to see or download their subscription invoices/GST receipts. The data is created and served, but nothing in the app ever requests it.
- Evidence: Client surface is only four calls: `export const subscriptionApi = { plans: () => api.get('/subscriptions/plans'), current: () => api.get('/subscriptions/current'), subscribe: (data: any) => api.post('/subscriptions', data), cancel: () => api.post('/subscriptions/cancel'), };` (frontend/src/lib/api.ts:456-461) — no invoices entry. The route and controller exist: `router.get('/invoices', ctrl.getInvoices);` (packages/subscription-service/src/routes/subscription.routes.ts:26) → `const invoices = await subscriptionService.getInvoices(userId); res.json({ success: true, data: invoices });` (subscription.controller.ts:31-32) with plan details joined (subscription.service.ts:316-320). The only invo
- Fix: Add `invoices: () => api.get('/subscriptions/invoices')` to subscriptionApi and render a Billing History card on SubscriptionPage (invoice_number, total_amount, status, paid_at) — and fix the leaky query first (it currently spans every business the user is a member of, see the createSubscription finding).

**No cancel/downgrade path in the UI, and the cancel endpoint would revoke access instantly instead of at period end**
- `frontend/src/lib/api.ts:460` — *dead-endpoint*
- Impact: A paying user cannot cancel or downgrade anywhere in the app — SubscriptionPage renders only per-plan subscribe buttons. If the endpoint is ever wired up as-is, cancelling immediately locks the user out of the entire product for the remainder of the period they already paid for.
- Evidence: `cancel: () => api.post('/subscriptions/cancel')` (frontend/src/lib/api.ts:460) has zero call sites — `grep -rn "subscriptionApi\." frontend/src` returns only plans() (SubscriptionPage.tsx:41), current() (:42) and subscribe() (:101). The handler sets a terminal state with no `cancel_at_period_end` concept: `data: { status: 'CANCELLED', cancelled_at: new Date() }` (packages/subscription-service/src/services/subscription.service.ts:344-347); auth-service then reports `trialExpired: true` because no ACTIVE/TRIAL row remains (packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:47-52,85-90) and ProtectedRoute hard-redirects: `if (!allowWhenExpired && trialInfo?.expired) return <Navigate to="/subsc
- Fix: Add a "Cancel subscription" action (with confirm dialog) on the current-plan card, and change the service to set a `cancel_at_period_end` flag / keep status ACTIVE until current_period_end passes so paid time is not forfeited.

**Referrals page shows the "locked — upgrade to unlock" screen whenever GET /referrals/my-referrals fails**
- `frontend/src/components/referrals/ReferralsPage.tsx:57` — *error-handling*
- Impact: A paid, eligible user hitting a 500/502 from referral-service is told "Referrals locked — upgrade your plan to unlock" and pushed toward the subscription page, instead of seeing an error or a retry. The load-error toast is unreachable dead code.
- Evidence: `Promise.allSettled` never rejects, so the catch (and its toast) can never run: `const [refRes, lbRes] = await Promise.allSettled([referralApi.myReferrals(), referralApi.leaderboard()]); if (refRes.status === 'fulfilled' && refRes.value.data?.data) { setData(refRes.value.data.data); } ... } catch { toast.error(t('referrals:load_error')); }` (ReferralsPage.tsx:57-70). `data` therefore stays null on failure and null is treated as ineligible: `if (!data?.isEligible) { ... <h2>{t('referrals:locked_title')}</h2> ... <Button onClick={() => window.location.href = '/subscription'}>{t('referrals:upgrade_unlock')}` (ReferralsPage.tsx:145-164). Note SubscriptionPage handles the same hazard correctly wi
- Fix: Track the rejected case explicitly (`if (refRes.status === 'rejected') setLoadError(true)`) and render an error card with a Retry button, showing the locked state only when the API actually returned `isEligible: false`.

**Trial countdown banner is shown to paying customers because daysRemaining is derived from the paid period end**
- `packages/auth-service/src/services/auth.service.ts:57` — *wrong-data*
- Impact: A user on a paid ACTIVE monthly plan sees "Your free trial expires in 3 days / today" in the last week of every billing cycle, and "Trial expired" once the period lapses — telling paying customers they are on a trial and never mentioning renewal.
- Evidence: getTrialInfo makes no distinction between TRIAL and ACTIVE: `status: { in: ['ACTIVE','TRIAL'] }` … `const daysRemaining = Math.max(0, Math.ceil(msRemaining / (1000*60*60*24))); return { trialExpired: false, trialEndsAt: activeSub.trial_ends_at?.toISOString() || activeSub.current_period_end.toISOString(), daysRemaining, planName: ... }` (auth.service.ts:47-68) and the expired branch reports `trialExpired: true` for a lapsed *paid* subscription (auth.service.ts:85-90). The page renders that as trial copy unconditionally: `trialInfo.expired ? ... t('subscription:trial_expired') ... : trialInfo.daysRemaining !== null && trialInfo.daysRemaining <= 7 ? ... t('subscription:trial_days_left', { count
- Fix: Include the subscription status/isTrial flag in the /auth/me trial payload and switch the banner copy on it ("trial ends in N days" vs "renews on <date>" / "subscription expired").

### WhatsApp AI service

**"Aaj ka Hisaab" always returns the Outstanding report — the report router matches 'hisaab' as an outstanding query first**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/agents/hisaab.agent.ts:26` — *logic*
- Impact: The bot's own primary quick-reply button, "Aaj ka Hisaab" (offered in the greeting and the unknown-intent fallback), and any phrase containing "hisaab" — the most common Hindi word for accounts — return the overall receivables/payables report instead of the daily summary. The daily-summary branch is effectively unreachable for the wording the bot itself teaches users.
- Evidence: hisaab.agent.ts:26-34 checks outstanding before daily: `if (this.isOutstandingQuery(query)) { return this.generateOutstandingReport(intent, context); }` ... `if (this.isDailySummary(query)) { return this.generateDailySummary(context); }`, and both predicates match the same token — hisaab.agent.ts:211-213 `private isOutstandingQuery(query: string): boolean { return /(?:baaki|baki|outstanding|udhar|pending|hisaab|hisab|ledger|balance)/.test(query); }` vs hisaab.agent.ts:219-221 `private isDailySummary(query: string): boolean { return /(?:aaj|today|daily|summary|hisaab|din)/.test(query); }`. The text reaching it is the button title: orchestrator.ts:482 `{ id: 'daily_summary', title: 'Aaj ka His
- Fix: Route on the classified intent and the button id rather than re-regexing the raw text: use intent.intent (OUTSTANDING_QUERY / GST_QUERY / REPORT_REQUEST) plus the interactive reply id ('daily_summary', 'outstanding'), and remove the overlapping tokens ('hisaab', 'balance') from the ambiguous predicates.

**MCP parse_amount multiplies by 1000/100000 on bare 'k'/'l' with no word boundary — "50 kg" parses as ₹50,000**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/mcp/index.ts:236` — *wrong-data*
- Impact: The tool advertised for turning Hinglish amount text into numbers returns figures inflated by 1000x or 100,000x whenever the text contains a unit that starts with k or l — "50 kg cement" → ₹50,000, "2 litre" → ₹2,00,000. Any caller (agent or external MCP client) that trusts parse_amount records grossly wrong money.
- Evidence: mcp/index.ts:234-247 uses unbounded single-letter aliases and stops at the first hit: `const patterns: Array<{ regex: RegExp; multiplier: number }> = [ { regex: /(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(?:crore|cr)/i, multiplier: 10000000 }, { regex: /(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(?:lakh|lac|l)/i, multiplier: 100000 }, { regex: /(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(?:hazaar|hazar|thousand|k)/i, multiplier: 1000 }, { regex: /(\d+(?:,\d{2,3})*(?:\.\d{1,2})?)/i, multiplier: 1 } ]; for (const pattern of patterns) { const match = text.match(pattern.regex); if (match) { amount = parseFloat(match[1].replace(/,/g, '')) * pattern.multiplier; break; } }` — none of the aliases is terminated by \b, and there is no requirement that the alias be the end o
- Fix: Terminate every alias with a word boundary and drop the bare single letters, or require them to end the token: `/(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(?:lakh|lac|l)\b(?!tr|itre)/i` is still fragile — prefer `/(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(?:lakh|lac)\b/i` plus an explicit `\bl\b` case, and reuse the (boundary-correct) extraction from samajh.agent.ts so the two code paths cannot disagree.

**All Zod schemas and validate* middleware are written and exported but wired to zero routes**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/middleware/sanitize.ts:150` — *validation*
- Impact: Every request body and path parameter in the service is unvalidated. PATCH /wa/conversations/:userId/preferences spreads arbitrary JSON into the stored preferences — a caller can set approvalThreshold to 1e12 (disabling the human approval gate for that user), replace entityMappings, or inject unknown keys — despite a .strict() schema existing for exactly that body. userId path params bypass their format regex, and /agents/classify accepts unbounded text.
- Evidence: sanitize.ts:150-171 defines conversationParamsSchema (`userId: z.string().min(1).max(50).regex(/^[a-zA-Z0-9_:+\-@.]+$/, 'Invalid userId format')`), preferencesBodySchema (`z.object({ language: ..., approvalThreshold: z.number().min(0).max(10000000).optional(), ... }).strict()`), mcpExecuteSchema and agentClassifySchema, and sanitize.ts:176-229 defines validateBody/validateParams/validateQuery. `grep -rn "validateBody|validateParams|validateQuery|agentClassifySchema|mcpExecuteSchema|preferencesBodySchema|conversationParamsSchema|strictInputValidation" src/` returns hits ONLY in middleware/index.ts:26-33 (the barrel re-export) — no route file uses any of them. The unvalidated write is conversa
- Fix: Wire them: `conversationRouter.patch('/:userId/preferences', validateParams(conversationParamsSchema), validateBody(preferencesBodySchema), handler)`, `agentRouter.post('/classify', validateBody(agentClassifySchema), handler)`, `mcpRouter.post('/execute', validateBody(mcpExecuteSchema), handler)`, and validateParams(conversationParamsSchema) on the conversation GET/DELETE routes.

**The per-user WhatsApp message rate limiter is built but never mounted, so the documented 30 msg/min abuse cap does not exist**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/index.ts:110` — *missing-guard*
- Impact: A single WhatsApp number can drive unlimited messages through the pipeline (up to the shared 300/min per-IP webhook cap, which Meta's egress IPs all share). Each message can trigger Azure OpenAI, Document Intelligence and Speech calls, so one abusive sender both drives unbounded AI spend and can starve every other user by consuming the shared IP budget.
- Evidence: The limiter exists and is even keyed off the WhatsApp sender — rate-limiter.ts:138-153 `export const userMessageRateLimiter = createRateLimiter('user-msg', { windowMs: 60000, maxRequests: 30, blockDurationMs: 120000, keyExtractor: (req) => { ... const from = body?.entry?.[0]?.changes?.[0]?.value?.messages?.[0]?.from; return from || req.ip || 'unknown'; }, ... });` — and it is exported through the barrel (middleware/index.ts:10 `userMessageRateLimiter,`) and imported into index.ts (index.ts:17). But the webhook mount omits it: index.ts:110 `app.use('/api/v1/wa/webhook', webhookRateLimiter, verifyWebhookSignature, verifyWebhookFreshness(), webhookRouter);` — only the per-IP webhookRateLimiter 
- Fix: Add it to the webhook chain after signature verification: `app.use('/api/v1/wa/webhook', webhookRateLimiter, verifyWebhookSignature, userMessageRateLimiter, verifyWebhookFreshness(), webhookRouter);`. Note its keyExtractor parses req.body, so it must run after the body is a real object (see the sanitizeInput/raw-Buffer finding) — and back it with Redis, since the in-memory Map in rate-limiter.ts:2

**Webhook verify token has a hardcoded fallback that disagrees with .env.example, so verification fails or succeeds on a public value**
- `packages/whatsapp-ai-service/src/config/index.ts:15` — *config*
- Impact: Two failure modes from one line. If WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN is unset (the current state — it appears in no .env), the endpoint accepts a token that is published in this repository, letting anyone who finds the URL complete Meta's subscribe handshake against it. If an operator configures Meta with the value printed in .env.example, verification returns 403 and the webhook cannot be registered at all.
- Evidence: config/index.ts:15 `verifyToken: process.env.WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN || 'bahikhata_webhook_verify',` versus .env.example line `WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN=bahikhata_webhook_verify_2026` — the two strings differ by the `_2026` suffix. webhook.routes.ts:35-41 compares against the effective value and 403s on mismatch: `if (mode === 'subscribe' && token === config.whatsapp.verifyToken) { logger.info('WhatsApp webhook verified successfully'); res.status(200).send(challenge); } else { logger.warn('WhatsApp webhook verification failed', { mode, ip: req.ip }); res.sendStatus(403); }`. The variable is set nowhere: root .env contains only `WHATSAPP_AI_SERVICE_PORT=3013` (line 30), and packages/whatsapp-ai-ser
- Fix: Remove the fallback and fail fast: in initializeServices, throw when WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN or WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_SECRET is missing and NODE_ENV !== 'test' (they are currently only pushed onto a warnings array — init.ts:11-28 never fails). Keep .env.example's value as a placeholder only, and add the two vars to the deployment env.

**WhatsAppAIDashboard is not routed in App.tsx and not linked from any nav — the whole cluster's UI is unreachable**
- `frontend/src/components/whatsapp-ai/WhatsAppAIDashboard.tsx:25` — *dead-ui*
- Impact: There is no URL and no navigation entry that renders this page, so none of its four tabs, the classify form, the chat tester or the MCP tools list can be reached by a user. The 373-line component ships in the repo but not in the app.
- Evidence: `grep -rn "WhatsAppAIDashboard|whatsapp-ai" frontend/src` returns exactly one line — the declaration itself: `components/whatsapp-ai/WhatsAppAIDashboard.tsx:25:export default function WhatsAppAIDashboard() {`. No import, no <Route>, no lazy() reference. `grep -rn "whatsapp|WhatsApp|munshi|Munshi" frontend/src/App.tsx frontend/src/components/layout/*.tsx` returns nothing. (This compounds the double-prefix and missing-auth-header findings: even if it were routed, its requests would 404 and then 401.)
- Fix: Register a lazy route in App.tsx behind the appropriate role guard (e.g. `<Route path="/whatsapp-ai" element={<WhatsAppAIDashboard />} />`) and add the nav entry in AppLayout — or delete the component. Do not leave it in the tree unreferenced, where its breakage is invisible.


## LOW (28)

### Admin console

**Invoices page never sends the server-side status/business/search filters, and its 'Refunded'/'Cancelled' filters can only ever match within 20 rows**
- `frontend/src/components/admin/AdminInvoicesPage.tsx:154` — *pagination*
- Impact: Filtering invoices by PENDING/FAILED/REFUNDED/CANCELLED only searches the 20 most recent invoices, so overdue or failed invoices older than that are invisible — which defeats the purpose of the filter, since PAID invoices dominate the recent set. The 'Revenue Collected' and Paid/Pending tiles are computed from the same slice.
